## Asar

### Asar · walk — grade C+

_Constructive walk — a stranger with the operator's own sheet (Income 3,200 · Home 700 · Car 1,800 · Insurance 200 · Electric 150 · Food 300 · Fitness 50 · Fun 200 per 33 days) opening /financial-2525 on a 390×844 phone for 60 seconds; read-only on commit 201a8ec and the frontend/out export; headless probes under scratchpad/fleet-fin/asar-walk/_

**One sentence:** The model keeps the lock to the cent (Net = I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr holds at all eight periods with 1e-17 drift), but the glass never joins the ladder to the record, so a stranger ends with three unlabelled money numbers (Released $0.00 · Available $0.00 · Net −$200) and no cash-on-hand he can trust.

**Findings**

- **The ladder is wired to the seed sheet, never to the person's record — no actuals, no editing, so Net is a fixed plan, not the person's cash** (high)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:138 `const totals = useMemo(() => netLadder(SHEET_LINES, period), [period])` — SHEET_LINES is the constant from lib/financial-2525/budget.ts:75; the file never imports `actualsByField` (import at command-ux1.tsx:48 lists FLOW_SECTIONS, fieldsOf, fieldOf, netLadder, toPeriod, RECURRENCES only) and there is no input anywhere on the ladder rows (lines 352–360 are read-only <td>s). A signed-in person recording a deposit into A.income_wages (line 190) changes the balance block and the record but not row A of the ladder. budget.ts:12–13 says the seed is 'DATA the person edits, never as advice' — nothing on the surface lets him edit it. The operator's own words in addendum 22: 'goal is to tract $/min against budget long term; first by testing and adjusting with my own finances' — the adjusting is not possible on r.012.
  - Fix: r.013 (already drafted as FIN-06.06): an actuals column beside the plan from actualsByField(replay(record) mapped to TimedTx, frame.startMs, at), and an amount input per field row (or a 'set budget' entry) persisted per owner alongside the record; until then, say on the ladder heading that these are the sheet's figures, not the person's.
- **One deposit carries two lengths that disagree by default — 'MoT · length in days' 30.333 and 'Timeline' every 33 days — two $/min laws for one entry** (high)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:145 `const [dMot, setDMot] = useState(String(SRC.mot.payMotDays))` (30.333, drives accrual.ts:63 ratePerMinCents = amount ÷ (motDays × 1440)) and command-ux1.tsx:151 `const [dRec, setDRec] = useState("days33" as Recurrence)` (33 d, drives ladder.ts:118 recurrenceDays and ladder.ts:128 actualsByField). Both render on the same deposit form (lines 304 and 306→169–173). The balance block's $/min (line 262) reads motDays; the ladder's future actuals read recurrence. The operator asked for ONE timeline: 'each transactions has an MoT timeline established at transaction entry … how long it last 30.333 days etc'.
  - Fix: One length per transaction: derive motDays from the chosen timeline (once → the amount lands whole; weekly 7; 33; 91; yearly 365) or make the MoT field the source and derive the recurrence from it; never two defaults that disagree on the same form. Addendum 24's one-form rebuild is the moment to do it.
- **Zero and sub-cent rows print '$0.0000' in a column of '$3,200.00' — format inconsistency, and at per-second a live line (G Health) reads as zero** (medium)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:141 `const usdUnit = (cents: number) => (Math.abs(cents) >= 100 ? usd(Math.round(cents)) : usd4(cents))` — H · Family, I · Debt service, J · Taxes, K · Work, M · Transfers show '$0.0000' beside '$3,200.00' at per 33 days (probe.log LADDER m33; ladder-m33.png). At 'per second' G · Health (50 / 33 d = $0.0000175/s) prints '$0.0000' and F · Food prints '$0.0001' — the brief's acceptance 'no rounding drift beyond cents' is met in the model but the print hides a non-zero line. CLAUDE.md release rule 8 (unit/format consistency gate) applies.
  - Fix: Print a true zero as '$0.00' in every unit; for sub-cent values print enough significant digits to show the line is non-zero (e.g. 4 significant figures) or say 'under a cent per second' in the person's words.
- **The withdrawal form asks for a 'Deposit day and time (CST)' and any deposit refusal appears under the WITHDRAWAL panel, off-screen from the deposit button** (medium)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:314 `<label …>{t("fin.deposit_at")}` inside `#fin-withdraw-form` (lexicon-data.ts:2910 englishDefault 'Deposit day and time (CST)'). command-ux1.tsx:187–188 `setRefusal(t("fin.reason_amount"))` / `fin.reason_stamp` in recordDeposit, but the only render of `refusal` is line 318 inside the withdrawal panel (lines 310–320), below the deposit panel (299–309). On a phone the deposit panel is ~7 stacked full-width controls tall, so the reason lands a screen below the button that was pressed.
  - Fix: A 'Day and time (CST)' key for the withdrawal (or the shared form in r.013), and render the refusal directly under the button that raised it (one refusal state per form, or one form with one button).
- **The phone strip shows two unlabelled dollar figures and a 'DEPOSIT' pill that looks like a button and does nothing** (medium)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:392 `{usd(bal.releasedCents)} · {usd(bal.availableCents)}` renders '$0.00 · $0.00' (probe.log STRIP) with no word for either; line 393 renders the phase name in a bordered rounded pill styled like the PRIMARY buttons above it (r006-forms-category.png and portrait-full.png show 'DEPOSIT' at the strip's right edge). The Withdraw button exists only when signed in (line 394), so the stranger's only strip control is a pill that is not one.
  - Fix: Label the two figures ('released · available') or drop to one (available) with its word; render the phase as plain text, not a pill, when there is no action.
- **The ladder promises a 'Balance view' that does not exist, and the section title 'A–M' is unexplained** (medium)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:363 `{t("fin.stock_note")}` → 'Cash, investments, debts, net worth, credit, protection and goals live on the Balance view — never per minute' (lexicon-data.ts:3088); nothing on the surface links to or renders such a view — the record marks FIN-06.06 'draft', 'planned for r.013'. The heading 'PERSONAL FINANCE LADDER A–M' (lexicon-data.ts:3087) names letters a stranger has not met; the first time he sees A…M is the row labels beneath.
  - Fix: Until r.013 ships, word the note as what is true ('Balances and goals are not on this ladder — they come as their own view'), and either drop 'A–M' from the heading or add a one-line legend (A income · B–H, K, L living · I debt · J taxes · M transfers · Net).
- **The duplicate-insurance warning the brief requires is computed and thrown away — rule 5 never reaches the glass** (low)
  - Evidence: lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:87–88 pushes 'DUPLICATE_INSURANCE' into `warnings`; command-ux1.tsx:138 receives `totals` and no line in the component reads `totals.warnings` (grep 'warnings' in the file: none). Since the seed cannot be edited on r.012 the case cannot arise yet, but the acceptance line 'If a premium is entered in both, warn' has no render.
  - Fix: Render totals.warnings under the ladder in the person's words ('Housing insurance is entered under both Housing and Insurance — count it once') when the ladder becomes editable.
- **The pickers offer every flow field to both forms: a deposit can be filed as an expense and a withdrawal as income, and nothing says what that does to Net** (low)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:157–175 `LadderPicker` lists `FLOW_SECTIONS` (A–M) for `hook="deposit"` (line 306) and `hook="withdrawal"` (line 315) alike; no filtering by Field.kind (ladder.ts:36 kind: Income | Fixed | Variable | Transfer). The default pair is sensible (A · Income / Wages every 33 days; B · Rent / Mortgage once), but a stranger tapping the first dropdown on the deposit form is offered 'L · Lifestyle'.
  - Fix: Deposit → sections whose fields are kind Income (A); withdrawal → B–M; or, on the one-form rebuild, let the type filter the section list.
- **'30.333' is printed bare in five places — a stranger cannot tell it is days** (low)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:215 roster label `${usd(d.amountCents)} · ${d.motDays}` ('$3,604.49 · 30.333' in portrait-full.png and ledger.png), line 370 and 373 (the record), line 447 (the chart caption). The unit word exists in the form label only (fin.mot_days 'MoT · length in days').
  - Fix: Print '30.333 d' (or the sheet's 0000.00.30_08.00..00 via fmtDurationDays) wherever motDays is shown.
- **Small touch targets on the chart controls** (low)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:442–443 `h-8 w-9` → the MoT ⇄ Clock buttons measure 36×32 px; line 279 planet <select> `py-1 text-xs` measures 71×26 px (probe.log TOUCH). CLAUDE.md release rule 9 (375 px touch targets) and the surface's own PRIMARY class use min-h-[44px].
  - Fix: min-h-[44px] on the toggle group and the planet select, matching the rest of the card.

**Keep**

- The lock holds in the model: netLadder(SHEET_LINES) gives A 3,200 · B 700 · C 1,800 · D 200 · E 150 · F 300 · G 50 · L 200 · Net −200 per 33 days; Net at all eight periods equals I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr; a period round-trip (33 d → year → minute) drifts 1.4e-17 — well within a cent (ladder.ts:69–89, verified by the probe in fleet-fin/asar-walk/math.mts).
- Sixty fields with the brief's slash-merged labels exactly, twenty sections A–T, 46 flow / 14 off; U is a rule (amortize) that refuses N–T and never appears as a chip; Net is the last row and reads red at −$200 (ladder-m33.png).
- The unit dropdown is the panel's full width in portrait (290 of 390 px, probe TOUCH) and sits at its own width in landscape (ladder-landscape-min.png); switching units re-prints every row from one constant.
- No page errors in either orientation, no horizontal scroll (scrollWidth 390), the page hydrates the example deposit and the year line with a SOURCED perihelion in CST.
- The record sentence obeys the signer-voice law ('On this phone only — a cloud copy follows once sign-in carries it'), and the record JSON is honest: FIN-06.06 is 'draft', human_testing and operations are Red.
- The old sheet categories are bridged to fields in one map (budget.ts:70–73) so r.006–r.011 entries still print their category (command-ux1.tsx:179–183) — nothing on an existing record is rewritten.

**Report (verbatim)**

THE WALK (390×844, the export of 201a8ec, revision 0.012 on the glass)

Second 0–10. I land on a Session shell: three glyphs, FINANCIAL · 2525, 'Money as time — this MoT', a phase rail DEPOSIT · HOLD · RELEASE · WITHDRAW · RECORD, and an amber YOUR TURN card telling me to sign in because what I am looking at is the operator's paycheck. I understand this is someone else's pay being released over time. I do not yet see my sheet anywhere.

Second 10–25. Below the fold: 'Your deposits' — one row, '$3,604.49 · 30.333' (30.333 what?). A cyan block: Released $0.00 · In escrow $0.00 · Withdrawable $0.00 · Withdrawn $0.00 · Available $0.00. Five money words, all zero, because the example has not started (Oct 1 07:00). The chart says the same: 'Pending — releases from 2026.10.01_07.00..00', two crossing lines. I press the orbit icon; the axis turns into 2669 .0833 ..0514 — I put it back. I press Planet → Mars; the axis does not change in clock mode (Earth hours for now, the record says), the year line switches to 'declared, not sourced'. The year line itself — Day 271 · Quarter 3 · 89/91 · frame 9 · 7/33 — I can read but cannot use. A pinned strip at the bottom reads '$0.00 · $0.00 DEPOSIT'; I tap DEPOSIT and nothing happens — it is the phase name in a button's clothes.

Second 25–45. Sign in block. Then, at 1,562 px of a 2,774 px page, the thing the brief locks: PERSONAL FINANCE LADDER A–M, a unit dropdown reading 'per 33 days', thirteen rows A–M with icons, my sheet's eight lines beneath the sections they belong to, Net −$200.00 in red. This is right. I switch to per minute: Income $0.0673, Net −$0.0042. Per year: −$2,212.12. The numbers are consistent, the arithmetic is the brief's. Two things bother me: five sections read '$0.0000' next to '$3,200.00', and I cannot change any number to my own — Home 700 is the operator's rent, not mine. The note under the table sends me to a 'Balance view' I cannot find.

Second 45–60. THE RECORD · CHAIN VERIFIED, one row, 'On this phone only'. The Trinity wheel folded. A version stamp. The end.

WHAT I UNDERSTOOD: a paycheck can be put in escrow and released by the minute; a budget can be read in any unit from a second to a year; Net is income minus everything else and it is red when it is negative.

WHAT I COULD NOT: (1) what my cash on hand is. The page has Released, Withdrawable, Available (escrow words) and Net (a plan word). None of them is 'what I have' — the brief's N · Checking / Savings / Cash is deferred to r.013, and the note tells me so in a sentence that names a view that is not there. (2) How the ladder and the record relate. If I signed in and recorded my $3,200 paycheck into A · Income / Wages, row A would still say $3,200.00 from the seed and would not move; command-ux1.tsx:138 binds the ladder to SHEET_LINES for good, and actualsByField — the exact function the operator described ('track $/min against budget … based off transaction date and how long it last') — exists in ladder.ts:128 and is never imported by the surface. (3) Which of two lengths is 'the timeline'. Reading the deposit form's code (sign-in gated): 'MoT · length in days' defaults 30.333 and 'Timeline' defaults 'Every 33 days' on the same form (lines 145, 151). The clock's $/min runs on the first, the ladder's future actuals on the second. Addendum 22 speaks of one timeline per transaction.

WHAT I WOULD PRESS: the unit dropdown (works, well sized). A number on the ladder to change it (nothing happens). 'Balance view' (there is no link). The strip's DEPOSIT pill (not a button). The MoT icon (works, then I put it back). Sign in — with the sheet in hand I would expect to type my eight lines next; the form I would find asks for a deposit with amount, date, MoT, memo, section, field, timeline — seven controls — and the withdrawal form beneath asks me for a 'Deposit day and time'.

WHAT THE BRIEF PROMISES THAT I DO NOT SEE: 'N–T on a second surface (Balance / Goals)' — absent, named only in a note. 'If a premium is entered in both, warn' — the model warns (ladder.ts:88), the glass never reads totals.warnings. 'U amounts must name a destination field' — the rule exists (ladder.ts:96), no entry for it. 'Detail sheet may list components' — none. All four are on the record as FIN-06.06 draft for r.013, so this is not hidden; but on r.012 the lock is a model with a table on it, not yet a system a person can put himself into.

DOES NET MEAN WHAT THE BRIEF LOCKS? Yes. I checked netLadder at every period against I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr with the operator's numbers and the drift across a period round-trip is 1.4e-17. Transfers are outflow, I and J are their own terms, negative amounts are ignored rather than netted, the 'year' is the brief's 365 days (and the record says, correctly, that this differs from the 365.259636-day MoT whole — $35,393.94 vs $35,419.12 a year on the operator's income; a stranger will never see the gap).

DOES A PERSON END WITH CASH-ON-HAND HE TRUSTS? No, not on r.012. He ends with a red plan number he cannot edit and an escrow number that belongs to the example. The two never meet on the glass, and the strip that follows him down the page prints '$0.00 · $0.00' without saying which zero is which.

GRADE C+. The model deserves an A: exact labels, exact equation, exact factors, honest record. The surface as a stranger meets it deserves a C: the ladder is two screens below an escrow story he did not come for, it is read-only and disconnected from what he records, the one form he can reach asks for the money's length twice with two different defaults, and the copy points to a view that is not built. The r.013 plan already names most of the cure; the two-lengths defect and the '$0.0000' print are not in it and should be.

Probe artefacts (read-only run): scratchpad/fleet-fin/asar-walk/{portrait-full,ladder-m33,ladder-min,ladder-year,chart-abc,chart-clock,year,signin,ledger,balance,your-turn,ladder-landscape-min,landscape-top}.png, probe.log, math.mts.

### Asar · adversarial — grade C

_Adversarial pass (B) on Financial-2525 v.000_r.012, commit 201a8ec — read-only; code read from the commit, probes run headless on frontend/out and on the r.012 pure libraries_

**One sentence:** The A–U model in ladder.ts is sound and its arithmetic holds at every period, but the glass does not yet give a person one trusted cash-on-hand: the ladder shows only the seed sheet (the person's own record never reaches it), Net silently double-counts a premium entered in B and D against the brief's own rule 5, one deposit carries two contradictory lengths, a backdated withdrawal overdraws the record and the clamp hides it, the three new pickers are unmounted every second by the 1 Hz clock, and most surface gates are regexes over source rather than behaviour.

**Findings**

- **Net double-counts a premium entered in both B.housing_insurance and D.auto_renters_home — the brief's rule 5 says warn AND do not double-count** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:106 adds every seen flow line to its section; :110 only pushes the string 'DUPLICATE_INSURANCE' with the comment 'Net counts what was entered'. Probe (fleet-fin/asar-adv/probe.mjs, dupInsurance): income 1000, B 120, D 120 → warnings ['DUPLICATE_INSURANCE'], net 760; one premium would give 880. The brief (docs/asks/…_mot_financial_system.md, addendum 22, UI rule 5 and 'What not to build: Silent double-count of the same premium in B and D'). The warning itself never reaches the glass: command-ux1.tsx:138 calls netLadder and :354–360 render totals.sections and totals.net; `totals.warnings` is read nowhere in the component (grep of 201a8ec: zero hits). The gate tests/financial-ladder.test.mjs:49–51 only asserts the string exists, never that Net excludes the duplicate. So the double-count is exactly 'silent'.
  - Fix: In netLadder count the duplicate once (e.g. keep B, exclude D.auto_renters_home's housing share, or take the larger and flag) and render totals.warnings as one sentence above Net; add a gate asserting net(both) === net(one) while warnings.length === 1.
- **The ladder never sees the person's own record — the 'actuals vs budget' the operator asked for is not on the glass; FIN-03.02 claims it is** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:138 `netLadder(SHEET_LINES, period)` — SHEET_LINES is the static seed (budget.ts:77); :355 filters SHEET_LINES again for the field rows. actualsByField (ladder.ts:134), ratePerMinute (:129) and recurrenceDays (:126) are imported nowhere in command-ux1.tsx (grep at 201a8ec: 0 hits). A signed-in person who records a $3,604.49 deposit on A.income_wages every 33 days still sees Income $3,200.00 on the ladder. Yet the record's FIN-03.02 statement (financial-2525.v.000.json) reads 'a transaction's $/min runs from its date for its timeline's length so the ladder's actuals can be tracked against the budget over time', verified by tests/financial-surface.test.mjs — which only regex-matches that `recurrence: dRec` is written into the tx (:52). The revision note honestly defers the actuals column to r.013 (FIN-06.06 DRAFT), but FIN-03.02 is marked as shipped with a behaviour that does not exist.
  - Fix: Either downgrade FIN-03.02's statement to 'the timeline is recorded' until the actuals column ships, or wire actualsByField(replay(record) → TimedTx, frame.startMs, frame.endMs) into a third column now; gate it with a headless or pure test on real record entries.
- **One deposit carries two lengths that disagree: MoT 30.333 days (motDays) and Timeline 'Every 33 days' (recurrence) — nothing reconciles them** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:145 `dMot` defaults to SRC.mot.payMotDays (30.333); :151 `dRec` defaults to 'days33'; :190 writes both onto the same FinTx. The seed EXAMPLE at :85 already has `motDays: SRC.example.motDays, … recurrence: "days33"`. Escrow releases at amount ÷ (30.333 × 1440) (accrual.ts:75) while the timeline says 33 d: probe twoLengths → 8.252 ¢/min by MoT vs 7.585 ¢/min by timeline, ends 2.67 days apart. The record shows '$3,604.49 · 30.333' beside a field whose timeline is 33 d (:373 prints motDays, never the recurrence). The operator's ask: 'each transactions has an MoT timeline established at transaction entry … how long it last 30.333 days etc' — ONE timeline. (HEAD 50e57c0 adds a 'paymot' preset and addendum 25, which confirms the gap was felt after r.012.)
  - Fix: One length per transaction: the timeline dropdown sets motDays (a 'pay MoT 30.333' option plus 'other' with a typed number), or motDays is derived from the recurrence; the record prints the one length.
- **A backdated withdrawal overdraws the record and the clamp hides it — the accrual invariant 'a withdrawal never makes the available balance negative' is breakable from the form** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: accrual.ts:113–119 validateWithdrawal checks the balance at w.atMs, excluding only w.id; withdrawals dated after w.atMs are invisible to it (balanceAt:96 filters `x.atMs <= t`). command-ux1.tsx:195 lets the person type any past stamp (`wAt`). Probe backdatedOverdraw: deposit $1,000 released; withdraw $1,000 at T+2d (ok); then record a $1,000 withdrawal dated T+1.5d → validateWithdrawal says ok; balanceAt(now) → withdrawn 200,000 ¢ on 100,000 ¢ deposited, withdrawable − withdrawn = −100,000, but availableCents prints 0 because :106 `Math.max(0, …)`. The chain verifies (chainOk true), so the record certifies an impossible state, and the glass shows 'Available: $0.00' rather than the overdraft. tests/financial-accrual (not read here) evidently does not cover an out-of-order entry.
  - Fix: Validate a withdrawal against the balance at EVERY instant from w.atMs to the latest recorded instant (or simply at max(w.atMs, last entry)); drop the clamp or surface a negative available as a red 'record disagrees' line; add the out-of-order case to the accrual gate.
- **The three new pickers (section · field · timeline) are unmounted and remounted every second — LadderPicker is declared inside the render body under the 1 Hz clock** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:157 `const LadderPicker = ({…}) => (…)` is created inside FinancialCommandUX1 on every render; :95 `setInterval(() => setNow(Date.now()), 1000)` re-renders every second; :306 and :315 mount it as `<LadderPicker …/>`. React compares element types by reference, so a new function identity each render is a different component: the old <select> nodes are destroyed and new ones created. Proof (fleet-fin/asar-adv/shots.mjs, remountProof, React 18.3.1 from the app's node_modules, the exact pattern): insidePicker sameDomNodeAfter2.6s = false, focusStillOnIt = false; a picker declared at module scope keeps its node and focus (true/true). The forms only render signed-in (:297) and the export has no Auth0 client id (providers.tsx:138 branch), so this could not be exercised on the built page — but the code path is unconditional. On a phone the native select sheet opened on Section/Field/Timeline loses its element within a second; on the desk focus drops every tick. The r.006 category dropdown did not have this: it was inline JSX.
  - Fix: Move LadderPicker to module scope (pass t/secLabel/fieldLabel as props) or inline the three <label>s in both forms; add a headless gate that focuses a picker and asserts document.activeElement survives two ticks.
- **A tampered device record is shown empty and then OVERWRITTEN by the next 'Record it' — the evidence of tampering is destroyed by the app itself** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: record.ts:65 loadRecord returns `emptyRecord(owner)` when verify fails; command-ux1.tsx:114 sets that empty record as state; :177 commit() does `append(record, tx, at)` then `saveRecord(next)` — writing a fresh 1-entry record over the altered one. Probe tamper: 3 entries stored, one amount edited → tampered true, entriesShown 0, store still 3 entries; after the next Record it the store holds 1 entry (rev 1). The record's own header (record.ts:10) says 'once recorded, is on the record for good'. Also, an editor who re-hashes after editing passes verify (probe rehashForgery: verifyOk true, amount 999) and the glass prints 'CHAIN VERIFIED' (fin.chain_ok) — the file comment at record.ts:24 admits FNV is not a signature, the glass does not.
  - Fix: On tampered, keep the raw store under a quarantine key (exel-fin:<owner>:tampered-<at>) before any save; refuse to append until the person acknowledges; word fin.chain_ok as 'links hold on this phone' rather than 'verified'.
- **A second identical transaction in the same second vanishes silently — append is idempotent on an id built from instant + cents, and the form clears as if it recorded** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:190 `id: \`d-${when}-${cents}\`` (withdrawal :197 likewise); record.ts:35 `if (rec.entries.some((e) => e.tx.id === tx.id)) return rec;`; :191 `setDAmt(""); setDMemo("")` runs regardless. Probe idempotentDrop: two deposits of $3,604.49 at the same stamp with different fields (A.income_wages, then A.upside 'second paycheck same second') → 1 entry, the second field never recorded, no refusal. A person typing a stamp by hand (the form accepts one) and entering two same-amount lines on the same instant loses one with no word.
  - Fix: Include a nonce or the entry count in the id (or use crypto.randomUUID), and have commit() surface a refusal when append returns the same record.
- **Mars 'year position' opens at EARTH's perihelion and lasts ~364 days while its whole is 686.98 d — the A.B..C on Mars can never reach 3600 and pastFull can never fire** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: calendar.ts:98–101 on the perihelion anchor always calls perihelionOf(year), the Earth table (:34–47), regardless of yearDays; :112 pastFull = elapsed > yearDays × MS_PER_DAY. planet-ltu.ts:54–55 seeds Mars with yearAnchor 'perihelion', yearDays 686.98. Probe mars (2026-09-30): startsAtEarthPerihelion true, lengthDays 364.39, abc.a 1413, maximum A at reset ≈ 1910, pastFullEverPossible false. The glass only prints fin.anchor_note ('declared') for non-Earth (command-ux1.tsx:292) — it does not say the reset is the wrong planet's.
  - Fix: Give the LTU row its own anchor instant (Mars perihelion epoch + period) or, until then, use the 'calendar' anchor with a note that reads 'opens on Earth's Jan 1; Mars perihelion not yet tabled'.
- **One column, two number formats: totals under $1 print four decimals, others two — visible in the default unit ('$700.00' beside '$0.0000')** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:141 `usdUnit = (cents) => (Math.abs(cents) >= 100 ? usd(Math.round(cents)) : usd4(cents))`. Capture fleet-fin/asar-adv/full-portrait.png (per 33 days): H · Family $0.0000, I · Debt service $0.0000 … beside B · Housing $700.00; ladder-portrait-hour.png: A $4.04, B $0.8838, G $0.0631 in one column. Probe formats: netZero → '$0.0000' and :360 paints a zero Net green. CLAUDE.md rule 8 (unit/format consistency gate) is not met on this table.
  - Fix: One precision per unit column (2 dp for day and up, 4 dp for hour/min/sec), and print an empty section as '—'.
- **The 90 ladder keys shipped STAGED: in r.012 as committed, every reader in the other 32 languages saw the ladder's section names, the sixty field labels, 'Timeline', 'Section', 'Field' and the N–T note in English** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: tests/lexicon-coverage.test.mjs:28 at 201a8ec places the 90 fin.sec.* / fin.field.* / fin.rec.* / section / field / timeline / ladder_title / stock_note keys in AFTER_FILL so the gate passes; lib/i18n-app/es.ts, sw.ts, ja.ts at 201a8ec contain 0 of them (99 fin.* keys in es.ts vs 189 at 50e57c0); lexicon-context.tsx:224 falls back to englishDefault. The commit says so ('STAGED … listed, never silent'), so this is an honest, temporary gap — but it was live between 201a8ec and 50e57c0. Note for the record: frontend/out is built from 50e57c0 (v0.013, footer stamp '50e57c0'), not from 201a8ec, so the headless locale check (Spanish 'A · Ingresos', Swahili 'A · Mapato', Japanese 'A · 収入') proves the fill, not r.012.
  - Fix: Ship model + fill in one commit when the keys are user-facing labels (the operator's 2026-09-13 rule: all UX in all 33 languages), or hold the ladder behind the fill; keep the export in the review bundle pinned to the revision under review.
- **Every surface claim about the ladder and the pickers is gated by regexes over source, not by behaviour** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:51 (two dropdowns + timeline), :52 (field/timeline recorded and shown), :53 ('Net last and red when negative'), :54 (full width in portrait), :55–56 (unit factors) are all `/regex/.test(ux)` over the component text. None renders the component, none reads a Net figure, none focuses a picker, none measures a width. The only behavioural gate, tests/financial-ladder.test.mjs, tests the pure library and skips rule 5's 'do not double-count' (:49–51) and out-of-order withdrawals. A refactor that keeps the strings and breaks the behaviour (as the inside-render LadderPicker does) passes test:ci.
  - Fix: Add one headless gate in test:ci on the export: Net text equals netLadder(SHEET_LINES).net formatted, unit select width === panel inner width at 390, picker focus survives two ticks (requires a stubbed sign-in), Spanish rows not English.
- **An entry whose field id is unknown is drawn with the Lifestyle icon, silently** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:180 `const sec = fieldOf(tx.field)?.section ?? "L"` — a field from a future revision, a renamed slug, or a hand-edited store shows the party-popper (SECTION_ICON.L) with the raw id as its label. Probe unknownField: fieldOf('X.made_up') null → glassSection 'L'.
  - Fix: Fall back to CircleEllipsis and print the id in muted text; never borrow a real section's icon.
- **Negative amounts are dropped from the ladder without a word — a refund or a correction can never lower a section** (LOW)
  - Evidence: ladder.ts:104 `!(l.amountNative > 0)) continue;`. Probe negativeIgnored: F 300 and F −50 → Net 700, not 750. Consistent with the gate (:42) but undocumented on the glass; the record's own law says a correction is a new entry that says what it corrects — the ladder cannot express one.
  - Fix: Either allow signed lines per field (sum may go negative → show 0 with a note) or refuse a negative at entry with a sentence.

**Keep**

- The ladder math holds exactly where the brief locks it: Net = I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr at all eight periods; a chain of conversions through every period and back returns 3200 to within 9e-11 cents (probe periodChain); the headless table reads −$200.00 per 33 days, −$0.0042 per minute, −$2,212.12 per year, Net last and red (report.json perUnit).
- The taxonomy is the brief verbatim: 20 sections in order, 60 fields with slash-merged labels, planes correct, U refused as a section and refused for N–T destinations (ladder.ts:31–76, :117–121; financial-ladder gate 59 checks).
- A balance in P never nets against I; transfers are outflow; HSA/FSA is an outflow kind (ladder.ts:98–108; gate :40–41).
- Section → field coherence on the picker is enforced at the change handler (command-ux1.tsx:160 sets fieldsOf(sec)[0].id), so a field cannot belong to a section other than the one shown; sensible defaults per section (probe sectionDefaults).
- The unit dropdown is genuinely full width in portrait: select 290 px = label 290 px = panel inner 290 px at 390×844, no horizontal page scroll (scrollWidth 390); landscape select 224 px at its own width (report.json). No td overflow in either orientation.
- Older r.006–r.011 entries keep their category and icon on the roster and the record (command-ux1.tsx:179–183, :215) and their hashes are untouched because field/recurrence are optional on FinTx (accrual.ts:36–37).
- The record is honest about staging and deferral: the r.012 revision note lists the 90 staged keys and the FIN-06.06 draft (N–T view, U entry, actuals column) instead of claiming them.

**Report (verbatim)**

ASAR · ADVERSARIAL PASS · Financial-2525 v.000_r.012 (201a8ec) · grade C

SCOPE AND A CAVEAT ON THE EVIDENCE. Code was read from `git show 201a8ec:<path>` throughout. mot.ts, accrual.ts, record.ts, calendar.ts, planets.ts, budget.ts and planet-ltu.ts are byte-identical between 201a8ec and HEAD; ladder.ts differs (HEAD adds 'paymot'/'other' presets), so the r.012 copy was extracted to fleet-fin/asar-adv/ladder.r012.ts and probed directly. The built export frontend/out is NOT r.012: its footer stamp reads 'v.000_r.013 · eXeL v0.013-2026.09.30-18.08CST · 50e57c0' (report.json stamp/version). Headless measurements below are therefore of r.013's export; every code-line claim is r.012's. The forms and the three new pickers render only signed-in (command-ux1.tsx:297) and the export has no Auth0 client id, so the pickers could not be driven on the page; their remount defect was reproduced with the app's own React 18.3.1 in the exact pattern.

THE LENS QUESTION — does a person end with cash-on-hand they trust? Not yet. Three numbers claim to be 'money': the escrow clock (Released/Available), the ladder's Net, and the record's list. They do not meet. The ladder is the seed sheet only (command-ux1.tsx:138, :355 — SHEET_LINES), so a person's own deposits never change Income, and actualsByField/ratePerMinute/recurrenceDays are never imported by the component. The escrow number can be pushed below zero by a backdated withdrawal and the clamp at accrual.ts:106 prints $0.00 over a −$1,000 truth (probe backdatedOverdraw). The record can be emptied by a tamper and then overwritten by the next Record it (probe tamper). Net, where it is computed, is right at every period — but it counts a premium twice when entered in B and D, which the brief forbids in two places (rule 5; 'What not to build'), and the warning string never reaches the glass (totals.warnings unread).

DOES NET MEAN WHAT THE BRIEF LOCKS? Arithmetically yes: I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr, transfers outflow, P never netted, fixed factors, no drift (periodChain 9e-11 ¢). Semantically no in one member: the duplicate premium. And one timeline per transaction, which the operator asked for in words, is not what shipped: the EXAMPLE itself carries motDays 30.333 and recurrence 'days33' (command-ux1.tsx:85), 8.25 vs 7.59 ¢/min, ends 2.67 days apart (probe twoLengths).

THE PICKERS. Section → field coherence is enforced (:160). But LadderPicker is declared inside the render (:157) under the 1 Hz clock (:95): React sees a new component type every second and destroys the three <select> nodes. Proof: remountProof insidePicker sameDomNode false / focus lost; a module-scope sibling true / true. On a phone the native picker sheet cannot survive a tick.

THE CALENDAR. Earth is right (perihelion 2026.01.03_11.15..00 CST printed, day 271, SOURCED). Mars opens at Earth's perihelion and lasts 364 d against a 686.98 d whole: A never exceeds ~1910, pastFull can never fire (probe mars). The glass says 'declared', not 'wrong planet's reset'.

THE UNIT DROPDOWN. Measured: 290 = 290 = 290 px at 390 portrait; 224 px landscape; scrollWidth 390; no td overflow. FD-24 holds.

THE LEXICON. At 201a8ec the 90 ladder keys are in AFTER_FILL and absent from every language store (es/sw/ja: 0 hits); t() falls back to English (lexicon-context.tsx:224). A Spanish reader of r.012 saw 'A · Income', 'Rent / Mortgage', 'Timeline'. The fill landed in 50e57c0, which is what the export shows ('A · Ingresos', 'A · Mapato', 'A · 収入').

THE GATES. financial-ladder.test.mjs is real and good on the pure library, but skips rule 5's non-double-count and out-of-order withdrawals. financial-surface.test.mjs :51–:56 pass on regexes over the component's text; none reads a rendered Net, focuses a picker, measures a width or renders a language. The remount defect passes test:ci today.

SMALLER FINDINGS. Idempotent append drops a same-second same-amount entry silently and the form clears (probe idempotentDrop). usdUnit mixes '$700.00' and '$0.0000' in one column; a zero Net is green. Unknown field ids borrow the Lifestyle icon. Negative amounts vanish from the ladder. A re-hashed forgery passes verify and prints 'Chain verified'.

WHAT IS RIGHT. The model is the brief, exactly; the equation holds everywhere; older entries survive with their category; the unit picker is full width; the record is honest about what is staged and deferred. Fix the class, not the instance: one length per transaction, one Net that excludes the duplicate, one picker component at module scope, one validation that looks at the whole timeline of the record, and one behavioural gate on the export — then this is a B+.

Artifacts (written only under fleet-fin/asar-adv/): probe.mjs + its printed JSON; shots.mjs, report.json, full-portrait.png, full-landscape.png, ladder-portrait-{sec,hour}.png, ladder-landscape-{sec,hour}.png, ladder-portrait-es.png, ladder.r012.ts.

### Asar · synthesis — grade C

**One sentence:** The ladder's arithmetic is the brief's to the cent at all eight periods (Net = I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr, round-trip drift 9e-11 ¢), but on r.012 nobody ends with cash-on-hand he trusts: the ladder shows the operator's seed sheet and never the person's own record, one deposit carries two lengths that disagree (30.333 d for the clock, 33 d for the timeline), Net counts a duplicate premium once in the warning and twice in the sum against the brief's rule 5 while the warning never reaches the glass, and the one new control a signed-in person can touch — the section · field · timeline picker — is unmounted by the 1 Hz clock every second.

**Top three**

- 1 · THE LADDER NEVER MEETS THE RECORD — Net is a fixed plan of someone else's numbers, not the person's cash. frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:138 `const totals = useMemo(() => netLadder(SHEET_LINES, period), [period])` and :355 filter the constant SHEET_LINES (lib/financial-2525/budget.ts:77); the import at command-ux1.tsx:48 never brings in actualsByField (ladder.ts:134), ratePerMinute (:129) or recurrenceDays (:126) — zero hits in the component — and rows :352–:360 are read-only <td>s with no input. A signed-in person who records a $3,604.49 deposit on A.income_wages still reads 'A · Income $3,200.00'. budget.ts:12 calls the seed 'DATA the person edits, never as advice'; nothing on r.012 lets him edit it. The record's FIN-03.02 statement ('… so the ladder's actuals can be tracked against the budget over time') reaches past the glass; its own stretch line is honest ('the actuals column beside the plan (r.013)'). FIX: an actuals column from actualsByField(replay(record) → TimedTx, frame.startMs, at) beside the plan, and a per-owner editable plan persisted alongside the record (FIN-06.06 already drafts both); until it ships, the ladder heading says these are the sheet's figures and FIN-03.02's last clause is trimmed; gate it on real record entries, not a regex over the source.
- 2 · ONE DEPOSIT, TWO LENGTHS — two $/min laws for one entry. command-ux1.tsx:145 `dMot` defaults to SRC.mot.payMotDays (30.333) and :151 `dRec` defaults to "days33" (33 d); :190 writes both onto the same FinTx, and the EXAMPLE at :85 already carries `motDays: SRC.example.motDays … recurrence: "days33"`. The escrow clock releases at amount ÷ (motDays × 1440) (accrual.ts:75) while the ladder's future actuals run on recurrenceDays (ladder.ts:126, :134): re-run of the probe gives 8.252 ¢/min by MoT against 7.585 ¢/min by timeline, ending 2.67 days apart; the roster (:215), the record (:370, :373) and the chart caption (:447) print only '30.333' with no unit. The operator asked for ONE timeline: 'each transactions has an MoT timeline established at transaction entry … how long it last 30.333 days etc'. FIX: one length per transaction — the timeline dropdown sets motDays (a 'pay MoT 30.333' preset plus 'other' with a typed number) or motDays is derived from the recurrence; never two defaults that disagree on one form; print the one length with its unit ('30.333 d'). HEAD's ladder.ts:124–126 already adds 'paymot' / 'other' presets, which is the start of this cure, not its end.
- 3 · NET DOUBLE-COUNTS A DUPLICATE PREMIUM AND THE WARNING NEVER REACHES THE GLASS — rule 5 of the lock. lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:106 adds every seen flow line to its section; :110 only pushes the string 'DUPLICATE_INSURANCE' with the comment 'Net counts what was entered'. Re-run: income 1000, B.housing_insurance 120, D.auto_renters_home 120 → warnings ['DUPLICATE_INSURANCE'], net 760; one premium gives 880. The brief (docs/asks/2026-09-30_financial_2525_mot_financial_system.md, addendum 22, UI rule 5: 'If a premium is entered in both, warn; do not double-count Net', and 'What not to build: Silent double-count of the same premium in B and D') forbids exactly this. On the glass command-ux1.tsx:138 receives `totals`, :354–:360 render sections and net, and `totals.warnings` is read nowhere (grep: 0 hits), so the double-count is silent; the gate tests/financial-ladder.test.mjs:49–51 asserts only that the string exists. The seed cannot be edited on r.012 so the case cannot arise from the glass yet — but the lock says 'implement the model first', and the model is where it is wrong. FIX: in netLadder count the duplicate once (keep B, exclude D's housing share, or take the larger) and render totals.warnings as one sentence above Net in the person's words; gate net(both) === net(one) while warnings.length === 1.

**Owed next**

- Wire the record to the ladder (top 1): actuals column from actualsByField over the current frame beside the plan; an editable plan per owner saved with the record; honest heading until then; trim FIN-03.02's last clause; gate on real entries.
- One length per transaction (top 2): the timeline sets motDays (pay-MoT 30.333 preset + 'other'); the example carries one length; print '30.333 d' (or the sheet's 0000.00.30_08.00..00) at command-ux1.tsx:215, :370, :373, :447.
- Rule 5 (top 3): netLadder counts a duplicate premium once; totals.warnings rendered above Net; gate net(both) === net(one).
- Every picker at module scope: LadderPicker is declared inside the render body (command-ux1.tsx:157) under the 1 Hz tick (:95), so the three <select>s at :306 and :315 are destroyed and recreated every second — re-run proof: nestedPickerSameNode false, focusStillOnPicker false, while an inline select keeps its node. Move it out (pass t / secLabel / fieldLabel as props) and add a gate that focuses a picker and asserts document.activeElement survives two ticks.
- Validate a withdrawal over the whole record, not one instant: accrual.ts:116 checks the balance only at w.atMs and balanceAt (:95–:96) filters `x.atMs <= t`, so a backdated withdrawal passes (re-run: w1ok true, withdrawn 200,000 ¢ on 100,000 ¢ deposited) and the clamp at :106 `Math.max(0, …)` prints Available $0.00 over a −$1,000 truth while verify() says the chain holds; a future-dated withdrawal likewise validates at its own date and is invisible now. Check every instant from w.atMs to the last recorded entry (or refuse out-of-order stamps), drop the clamp or print the disagreement in red, and add out-of-order + future-dated cases to the accrual gate — the file's own invariant (:15–:16) has no gate.
- Never overwrite a tampered store: record.ts:65 returns emptyRecord on a failed verify, command-ux1.tsx:114 adopts it, :177 commit() appends and saveRecord writes a 1-entry record over the altered one (re-run: 3 entries → 1). Quarantine the raw store under a side key before any save, refuse append until acknowledged; word fin.chain_ok (lexicon-data.ts:2951 'Chain verified') as 'links hold on this phone' — record.ts:24 admits FNV is not a signature and a re-hashed edit passes verify (re-run: verifyOk true).
- Entry ids with a nonce or the entry count: command-ux1.tsx:190 / :197 build `d-${when}-${cents}`, record.ts:35 returns the same record on a duplicate id, :191 clears the form anyway — a second same-amount entry on a typed stamp vanishes with no word (re-run: 1 entry, second field not recorded). commit() must surface a refusal when append returns the same record.
- Print law for the ladder: command-ux1.tsx:141 usdUnit prints '$0.0000' for H, I, J, K, M beside '$3,200.00' at per 33 days and a zero Net green (:360 `totals.net < 0 ? red : green`); per second G · Health is a live $50 line that prints '$0.0000'. One precision per unit column, a true zero as '$0.00' (or '—') in every unit, zero Net not green — CLAUDE.md release rule 8.
- The refusal under the button that raised it: recordDeposit sets refusal at :187–:188 but the only render is :318 inside the WITHDRAWAL panel (:310–:320), a screen below the deposit button on a phone; and the withdrawal form's stamp label is fin.deposit_at 'Deposit day and time (CST)' (:314, lexicon-data.ts:2910). The one-form rebuild (addendum 24) is the moment: one refusal state, one button, 'Day and time (CST)'.
- The strip says which zero is which: :392 prints `{usd(bal.releasedCents)} · {usd(bal.availableCents)}` with no words, :393 renders the phase in a bordered accent-hue pill that reads tappable and is not (the Withdraw button exists only signed in, :394). Label the two figures or show one with its word; phase as plain text when there is no action.
- Honest copy under the ladder: fin.stock_note (lexicon-data.ts:3088, rendered :363) sends the reader to a 'Balance view' that does not exist on r.012 (FIN-06.06 is draft); fin.ladder_title :3087 says 'A–M' before the reader has met the letters. Word the note as what is true and add a one-line legend or drop 'A–M'.
- Deposit → sections of kind Income (A); withdrawal → B–M: :161 offers FLOW_SECTIONS to both hooks, so a deposit can be filed under 'L · Lifestyle' with nothing said about what that does to Net.
- Ship the 90 ladder keys with their fill: at 201a8ec they sit in AFTER_FILL (tests/lexicon-coverage.test.mjs:19–) and lib/i18n-app/es.ts has 0 fin.sec.* / fin.field.* keys (99 fin.* total), so a Spanish, Swahili or Japanese reader of r.012 saw 'A · Income', 'Rent / Mortgage', 'Timeline' in English — honest and listed, but live between r.012 and the fill commit against the operator's 2026-09-13 rule.
- One behavioural gate on the export in test:ci: tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:51–:56 are regexes over the component text — none reads a rendered Net, focuses a picker, measures a width or renders a language; the remount defect passes test:ci today. Assert Net text === netLadder(SHEET_LINES).net formatted, unit select width === panel inner width at 390, picker focus survives two ticks, Spanish rows not English.
- Smaller, confirmed: an unknown field id borrows the Lifestyle icon (:180 `?? "L"` → use a neutral stroke and print the id muted); a negative amount vanishes from the ladder with no word (ladder.ts:104 — refuse at entry with a sentence or allow signed lines); chart toggle buttons are 36×32 px (:442–:443 h-8 w-9) and the planet select py-1 text-xs (:279) against the surface's own min-h-[44px] PRIMARY; on the perihelion anchor Mars opens at Earth's perihelion and runs ~364 d against a 686.98 d whole (calendar.ts:98–:101 always call perihelionOf(year)), so its A never passes ~1910 and pastFull (:112) can never fire — fin.anchor_note (:292) says 'declared', not 'the wrong planet's reset'.

**Report (verbatim)**

ASAR · SYNTHESIS · Financial-2525 v.000_r.012 (201a8ec) · grade C

WHAT I DID. Two reviewers of this lens reported — a walk at C+ and an adversarial pass at C. I read every file they cite from `git show 201a8ec:<path>` (command-ux1.tsx, ladder.ts, accrual.ts, record.ts, budget.ts, calendar.ts, the lexicon rows, the ask's addendum 22, the record JSON, both gate files), re-ran the adversarial's pure-library probe with the project's own TypeScript loader (the libraries other than ladder.ts are byte-identical between 201a8ec and HEAD; the r.012 copy of ladder.ts diffs clean against the commit), re-ran the standalone React remount proof, and looked at the walk's captures. Artefacts of my re-runs are under scratchpad/fleet-fin/asar-syn/ (probe-rerun.json, remount-rerun.log). I did not run new headless probes against frontend/out: the export there is now r.017, so a page measurement today would not be r.012. Note for the record: the walk's headless run WAS r.012 (its log reads 'revision 0.012', SHA 5c9d3cf, the r.012 bookkeeping commit); the adversarial's was r.013 (50e57c0). Every code-line claim in both reports is r.012's.

WHAT HELD, WHAT MOVED, WHAT I DROPPED. Every substantive claim from both reviewers held on re-reading or re-running. Corrections: the walk cited ladder.ts from a different copy — its 87–88 (duplicate warning) is :110, 118 (recurrenceDays) is :126, 128 (actualsByField) is :134, 96 (amortize) is :117; its accrual.ts:63 (ratePerMinCents) is :75; its budget.ts:75 (SHEET_LINES) is :77. '30.333' is printed bare on four lines (215, 370, 373, 447), not five. The strip's phase pill (:393) is the Session's bordered accent-hue chip, the same as the rail chips at :240 — not 'styled like the PRIMARY buttons'; the finding stands (it reads tappable and does nothing) with the comparison dropped. The adversarial's 'FIN-03.02 claims the actuals are on the glass' is half right: the statement's last clause reaches past the glass, but the same row's stretch line says 'the actuals column beside the plan (r.013)', so the record is honest one line later; I softened it. The adversarial's 'the export is r.013' is now 'the export is r.017' — same point, moved on. Nothing was dropped outright.

THE LENS QUESTION — DOES A PERSON END WITH CASH-ON-HAND HE TRUSTS? No. Three numbers claim to be money on this page and none of them is 'what I have'. The escrow clock (Released · In escrow · Withdrawable · Withdrawn · Available, all $0.00 for the example because it has not started) belongs to the operator's paycheck. The ladder's Net (−$200.00, red) belongs to the operator's sheet — command-ux1.tsx:138 binds the table to SHEET_LINES for good, no input exists on any row, and the one function the operator described in words ('track $/min against budget … based off transaction date and how long it last') is actualsByField at ladder.ts:134, imported nowhere by the surface. The record (the list under THE RECORD · CHAIN VERIFIED) is the person's, but it never reaches the ladder, and on r.012 it can be pushed into an impossible state: a backdated withdrawal passes validation (accrual.ts:116 checks one instant; :95–:96 cannot see later withdrawals), the chain verifies the overdraft, and the clamp at :106 prints 'Available: $0.00' over −$1,000. The page then follows him down the screen with a strip that prints '$0.00 · $0.00' without saying which zero is which (:392). CLAUDE.md's law for a signer — 'the panel never claims more than happened' — is broken by that clamp.

DOES NET MEAN WHAT THE BRIEF LOCKS? Arithmetically, yes, exactly. I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr with transfers as outflow, I and J their own terms, P never netted against I, the fixed factors 60 · 3,600 · 86,400 · 7 d · 33 d · 91 d · 365 d; the operator's eight lines give 3,200 · 700 · 1,800 · 200 · 150 · 300 · 50 · 200 → −200 per 33 days, −$0.0042 per minute, −$2,212.12 per year, and a chain of conversions through every period and back returns 3,200 within 9e-11 cents. Semantically, no, in one member the brief names twice: a premium entered in both B.housing_insurance and D.auto_renters_home is warned about (ladder.ts:110) and counted twice (:106) — re-run gives 760 where one premium gives 880 — and the warning string is never read by the component, so the brief's 'silent double-count' is precisely what shipped in the model. The comment at :110 ('Net counts what was entered; the detail sheet decides') is a deferral written into the code, not onto the record's CRS row, and the gate at tests/financial-ladder.test.mjs:49–51 checks the string, not the sum. The seed cannot be edited on r.012, so a person cannot yet trigger it from the glass; the lock says 'implement the model first', and the model is where the rule is broken.

THE TWO LENGTHS. Both reviewers found this independently and I rank it second because it sits exactly between the two things the lens asks about: the clock's $/min and the ladder's actuals. The deposit form defaults 'MoT · length in days' to 30.333 (:145) and 'Timeline' to 'Every 33 days' (:151) on the same panel; the example itself carries both (:85). Re-run: 8.252 ¢/min by MoT, 7.585 ¢/min by timeline, two end dates 2.67 days apart for one paycheck. The operator asked for one timeline per transaction. HEAD's ladder.ts (:124–:126) now carries 'paymot' and 'other' presets — a beginning; one length is not enforced until motDays and recurrence are the same fact.

THE PICKER THAT CANNOT BE USED ON A PHONE. The one new control r.012 gives a signed-in person — section → field → timeline — is declared inside the component body (:157) under the 1 Hz tick (:95). A new function identity each render is a new component type, so the three <select>s at :306 and :315 are destroyed and recreated every second. My re-run of the standalone proof with the app's own React: nestedPickerSameNode false, focusStillOnPicker false, and the inline unit select beside it keeps its node. On a phone the native picker sheet cannot survive a tick. I did not put this in the top three because it is a mechanism defect another lens owns and r.014's 'picker law' already names the cure, but it is the reason the signed-in path on r.012 cannot reach a trusted outcome at all, and it passes test:ci today because the surface gates (tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:51–:56) are regexes over the component's text.

WHAT IS RIGHT. The model is the brief verbatim: twenty sections in order, sixty fields with the slash-merged labels exactly, planes correct, U refused as a section and refused for N–T destinations, Alcohol one target, HSA/FSA an outflow, P never netted. The equation holds at every period with no drift. The ladder is the last row, red when negative, under one unit dropdown that is genuinely the panel's full width in portrait (290 = 290 = 290 px) and its own width in landscape; no page errors, no horizontal scroll in either orientation. Older r.006–r.011 entries keep their category and icon and their hashes, because field and recurrence are optional on FinTx (accrual.ts:36–:37). The record sentence obeys the signer-voice law, and the record JSON is honest about what is staged (90 keys in AFTER_FILL, listed) and deferred (FIN-06.06 draft for r.013). The record's 'year' honestly says the ladder's 365-day year differs from the 365.259636-day MoT whole.

THE GRADE. The walk gave C+ without reaching the two things only a signed-in run or a probe could show — the picker remount and the double-count; the adversarial reached both and gave C. With both confirmed by re-run, C is the honest mark: the model deserves an A− (one rule of the lock broken, the rest exact), the glass a C− (a read-only table of someone else's numbers, two lengths on one form, a control that cannot be held, a refusal under the wrong panel, a clamp that hides an overdraft, English labels in thirty-two languages at this commit). Fix the class, not the instance — one length per transaction, one Net that counts a premium once and says so, one picker component at module scope, one validation over the whole timeline of the record, one behavioural gate on the export — and the next revision of this surface is a B+.

Artefacts (read-only; written only under scratchpad/fleet-fin/asar-syn/): probe-rerun.json (the adversarial's pure-library probe on the r.012 ladder + the unchanged accrual/record/calendar), probe-rerun.err, remount-rerun.log.
