## Athena

### Athena · walk — grade C+

_Constructive walk (A) — a stranger carrying the operator's own sheet (Income 3,200 · Home 700 · Car 1,800 · Insurance 200 · Electric 150 · Food 300 · Fitness 50 · Fun 200 per 33 days), opening /financial-2525 at 390×844_

**One sentence:** The ladder itself is the best thing on the page — thirteen sections, my sheet on the right fields, Net red and correct at every unit in two taps — but the 60-second path breaks at step three: a stranger cannot place a transaction without an account, the live "$/min" number reads $0.00 because the example is dated in the future, the one transaction the form will take asks for its length twice with two different defaults, and the note under the ladder sends me to a "Balance view" that does not exist.

**Findings**

- **A stranger cannot place a transaction — the forms are behind sign-in and nothing on the page lets him try one** (high)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:297 `{owner ? (<div data-fin-forms> … ) : (<div className={SUB} data-fin-signin> … Sign in)}` — the only path to the deposit and withdrawal forms is an Auth0 login (line 142 `signIn = () => loginWithRedirect(...)`). Headless probe of the export: `forms: false`, `selects: [data-fin-planet, data-fin-budget-unit]` — no section, field or timeline picker is reachable. The 60-second path's third step ("place a transaction") is a dead end for anyone without an account; the guide card (line 252) says "Sign in ↓" and the arrow points 965 px down to a second Sign in button (data-fin-signin@1428) that does the same thing as the button under the arrow.
  - Fix: Let the stranger place a transaction against the example record in memory (no save, badge it EXAMPLE) so the section → field → timeline pickers and the refusals are seen before login; or at minimum render the pickers disabled with a one-line 'sign in to record' so the shape of the entry is visible. Drop the ↓ on the guide button when its target is the button itself.
- **The live $/min the page promises reads $0.00 — the worked example's instant is in the future at ship time** (high)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:85 `atMs: parseStampCST(SRC.example.depositStamp)` with `depositStamp: "2026.10.01_07.00..00"` (lib/financial-2525/domain.gen.ts:387); the commit is dated 2026-09-30 23:02 UTC. balanceAt (lib/financial-2525/accrual.ts:86) filters `x.atMs <= t`, so at every instant before Oct 1 07:00 CST the rail reads "RELEASE $0.00", the big clock (line 263) reads "$0.00", the rate line (line 262) falls back to the phase word "Released · Deposit" instead of a $/min, and the strip (line 391) reads "$0.00 · $0.00". Probe: balance = "Released · Deposit | $0.00 | In escrow: $0.00 · Withdrawable: $0.00 · Withdrawn: $0.00 | Available: $0.00". The stranger sees a page called "the MoT Financial System" whose one moving number does not move.
  - Fix: Show the example's rate even while pending (depositView already computes ratePerMinCents regardless of state — accrual.ts:60): print "$0.0825 per minute from 2026.10.01_07.00..00" on line 262 when the focus deposit is pending, or pick an example instant at or before the ship date so the ticker ticks on day one.
- **One deposit, two lengths: 'MoT · length in days' (30.333) and 'Timeline' (Every 33 days) are both asked and disagree by default** (high)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:145 `dMot = useState(String(SRC.mot.payMotDays))` → 30.333 (the MoT input, line 304, label fin.mot_days "MoT · length in days") and line 151 `dRec = useState("days33")` → the Timeline dropdown (LadderPicker, line 154–172, label fin.timeline) whose recurrenceDays is 33 (lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:126). Both are written on the same transaction at line 190 (`motDays: …, recurrence: dRec`). The escrow releases at amount ÷ 30.333 days (accrual.ts:60) while actualsByField would spread the same money over 33 days (ladder.ts:140). The brief's sentence is singular — "each transaction has an MoT timeline established at transaction entry … how long it last 30.333 days etc" — and FD-26 says "the timeline IS the transaction's MoT". The form asks the question twice with two answers.
  - Fix: One length per transaction: make the Timeline dropdown the MoT (once · weekly · 33 d · 91 d · yearly · other → a days input), derive motDays from it (or vice-versa), and never write two lengths that disagree into the chain hash.
- **The note under the ladder promises a 'Balance view' that does not exist on this revision** (medium)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:363 `{t("fin.stock_note")}` → "Cash, investments, debts, net worth, credit, protection and goals live on the Balance view — never per minute". There is no such view, link, tab or anchor anywhere in command-ux1.tsx (no `Balance` element, no route); the record itself files it as FIN-06.06 draft for r.013. The stranger reads a sentence that points at a door and finds a wall.
  - Fix: Until the N–T surface ships, say what is true: "Cash, investments, debts, net worth, credit, protection and goals are not on this ladder — they are never per minute" (or render the seven N–T section rows collapsed beneath a 'Balance' heading with no figures). When the view ships, make the sentence a link to it.
- **The '$/min against budget' the operator asked for is computed in the model but never reaches the glass — the ladder shows only the fixed seed sheet** (medium)
  - Evidence: lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:134 `actualsByField(...)` and :129 `ratePerMinute` are exported and gated but imported by no component or page at 201a8ec (`git grep` over frontend/components and frontend/app returns nothing). The ladder is built at frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:138 from `netLadder(SHEET_LINES, period)` — the eight seed lines only; a signed-in person's own deposits and withdrawals (which now carry a field and a timeline) change nothing in the table, and there is no way to edit a seed amount on the page although budget.ts:12 says the sheet is "DATA the person edits". The brief's last paragraph ("goal is to tract $/min against budget long term; first by testing and adjusting with my own finances") has no surface yet.
  - Fix: Add a second figure column (actual in the chosen unit, from actualsByField over the current 33-day frame) beside the budget figure, and let a tap on a field row set its budget amount (stored under the owner key). Say on the record that r.012 ships the plan column only.
- **Empty sections print '$0.0000' while everything else prints cents — five of thirteen rows read as a formatting glitch** (low)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:141 `usdUnit = (cents) => (Math.abs(cents) >= 100 ? usd(Math.round(cents)) : usd4(cents))` — zero falls into the four-decimal branch. Probe rows: "H · Family | $0.0000", "I · Debt service | $0.0000", "J · Taxes | $0.0000", "K · Work | $0.0000", "M · Transfers | $0.0000" at per 33 days and per year, beside "$3,200.00".
  - Fix: Print an empty section as "—" (or "$0.00" in the table's own unit) — `cents === 0 ? "—" : …` at line 141 — so four decimals mean only 'a real amount under a dollar'.
- **The withdrawal form asks for 'Deposit day and time (CST)'** (low)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:314 `<label …>{t("fin.deposit_at")}<input … value={wAt} …` inside `#fin-withdraw-form` — the same key as the deposit form's line 303 ("Deposit day and time (CST)", lexicon-data.ts:2910). A stranger taking money out is asked when he deposited it.
  - Fix: One key for the transaction instant ("Day and time (CST)") on both forms, or a fin.withdraw_at key; r.013's one-form fold is the moment to do it.
- **The phone strip reads '$0.00 · $0.00' with no words — two unlabeled figures and a chip** (low)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:391–394: `<span …>{usd(bal.releasedCents)} · {usd(bal.availableCents)}</span>` then the phase chip; no label says which is released and which is available. Probe: strip = "$0.00 · $0.00 | DEPOSIT". The Session's CriticalStrip it copies names its numbers; this one does not.
  - Fix: Prefix the two figures with their short words ("Released $0.00 · Available $0.00") or drop the second figure until money is withdrawable.
- **The browser tab is titled 'eXeL AI Polling' — the surface has no name of its own outside the page** (low)
  - Evidence: Probe `document.title` = "eXeL AI Polling" on /financial-2525/; frontend/app/financial-2525/page.tsx exports no metadata (line 7 `export default function FinancialPage()`), so the app-shell title is inherited. Drone-2525 got its own tab title (task #92 'eXeL Drone-2525'); a stranger with three tabs open cannot find this one.
  - Fix: Export `metadata = { title: "eXeL Financial-2525" }` from app/financial-2525/page.tsx (or its layout), the same pattern as Drone-2525.
- **Invalid SVG attribute logs an error on every load** (low)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:448 `<svg viewBox=… width="100%" height="auto" …>` — Chromium console on both orientations: `Error: <svg> attribute height: Expected length, "auto"`. Harmless visually (the viewBox sizes it) but it is the only error the page emits and it will hide a real one.
  - Fix: Remove `height="auto"` (the viewBox plus `width="100%"` already size the chart) or set the height in CSS.

**Keep**

- The ladder is exactly the brief: thirteen sections A–M in order, each with its stroke icon and the section letter, the sheet's eight lines under the right fields (Home → Rent / Mortgage, Car → Auto Payment, Insurance → Auto / Renters / Home, Electric → Electric / Gas, Food → Groceries, Fitness → Mental Health / Physical Fitness, Fun → Fun / Hobbies / Clothing), Net last and red — command-ux1.tsx:345–362, ladder.ts:31–76, budget.ts:72–77.
- The math holds at every unit by one constant and I checked it with the operator's sheet: per 33 days 3,200 / −200; per minute $0.0673 / −$0.0042 (3,200 ÷ 33 ÷ 1,440 = 0.06734); per year $35,393.94 / −$2,212.12 (× 365/33); per second $0.0011 / −$0.0001. The unit dropdown is the panel's full width in portrait (290 of 316 px) and its own width in landscape (224 px) — FD-24 kept.
- The lock's refusals are in the model, not in prose: U refuses a non-flow destination (ladder.ts:118), Net ignores N–T and unknown fields (ladder.ts:104), the duplicate-insurance warning fires only when both premiums are entered (ladder.ts:110), Alcohol has one target (ladder.ts:83), and no 30-day month exists anywhere (ladder.ts:27).
- The ask was persisted whole and hashed before the build (record handoffSha256 a465d3bd…, history of every addendum); the transaction's new field and timeline are optional so no r.006–r.011 hash moves (accrual.ts:35–37) and an older entry still prints its category (command-ux1.tsx:176–179).
- The chart's MoT ⇄ Clock toggle is one state for the card and the reveal is honest: the same five marks read '0 h 0 min · 7 d 13 h · … · 30 d 7 h' by default and '2669.0833..0514 … 2968.0696..2962' on the orbit, three lines each so five fit a 390 px phone without overprint; the year line gives Day 271 · Quarter 3 · 89/91 · frame 9 · 7/33 and the perihelion in Austin CST.
- No horizontal scroll at 390 or 844, every button at least 44 px, the record says where it lives in the person's words ('On this phone only — a cloud copy follows once sign-in carries it') and never names a vendor.

**Report (verbatim)**

ATHENA · A — THE CONSTRUCTIVE WALK · Financial-2525 v.000_r.012 (commit 201a8ec)

What I read: command-ux1.tsx and category-icon.tsx, the seven pure modules, the ask with its 24 addenda (addendum 22 is the lock), the record JSON at revision 0.012, the r.005–r.011 captures, and the export. One caveat, said plainly: the export in frontend/out stamps itself r.013 (the tree moved on while I read), so my headless captures are of r.013's export; I diffed 201a8ec..HEAD on the surface and none of the changed lines touch the pre-sign-in blocks I walked (balance, chart, year, ladder, record, strip), so the walk holds for r.012 — every line I cite is from the r.012 file.

THE SIXTY SECONDS, as a stranger with the operator's sheet in my hand.

0–10 s · LAND. Trinity glyphs, "FINANCIAL · 2525", a subtitle "the MoT Financial System · Version 000 · revision 0.013". I do not know what MoT is and the page does not tell me until the record block much further down. A card titled "Money as time — this MoT" with a pill "DEPOSIT", a five-chip rail "DEPOSIT 1 · HOLD · RELEASE $0.00 · WITHDRAW 0 · RECORD 1", one line of explanation. I understand: money goes in, is held, is released, can be withdrawn, is recorded. Good enough for ten seconds.

10–20 s · UNDERSTAND. The amber "YOUR TURN" card says "Sign in to record your own deposits — the example below is the operator's paycheck as data" with a big "Sign in ↓" button. Below it "YOUR DEPOSITS": one row, 2026.10.01_07.00..00 · Income / Wages (take-home) · PAYROLL · $3,604.49 · 30.333. Then the cyan block: "Released · Deposit  $0.00 — In escrow $0.00 · Withdrawable $0.00 · Withdrawn $0.00 · Available $0.00". Five zeros. The page is about money per minute and nothing is moving. The reason is in the code (line 85): the example is dated tomorrow morning at ship time, so balanceAt drops it. A stranger cannot know that; he reads a dead dashboard. The fixed strip at the bottom repeats "$0.00 · $0.00 | DEPOSIT" without saying which zero is which.

20–35 s · THE CHART AND THE TOGGLE. "MONEY AS TIME — THIS MOT · 0 H 0 MIN ELAPSED", Planet [Earth ▾], two icon buttons. The picture is an orange line falling and a green line rising across 30 d 7 h, a yellow tick at the start, a legend of six. I understand: escrow drains, released fills, over a month. I press the orbit icon: the title becomes "0.0000..0000 / 298.3463..2448 A-UNITS OF THE REVOLUTION" and the axis becomes stacked four-digit numbers. I do not understand it, but the clock icon brings me back and the page never forces it on me — that is the right default. The year line "Day 271 · Quarter 3 · 89/91 · 33-day frame 9 · 7/33 · Perihelion (Austin CST standard) · 2026.01.03_11.15..00 CST" I can read as a calendar even without knowing why.

35–45 s · PLACE A TRANSACTION. This is where the walk fails. I have "Car 1,800" to enter. There is no form. There is a second grey box "EXAMPLE … Sign in". I would press it; it leaves the page for a login. The pickers the brief was about — section A–M, then field, then timeline — I read in the code (lines 154–172) and they are well made: the section option reads "C · Transportation", changing it resets the field to the section's first, the timeline is one time · weekly · every 33 days · monthly (91) · yearly, all full width. But a stranger never sees them. And when he does sign in, the deposit form asks the length twice: "MoT · length in days" defaulting to 30.333 and "Timeline" defaulting to Every 33 days — two answers written into the same hash (line 190). The operator's own words were one timeline per transaction; FD-26 says the timeline IS the MoT. The withdrawal form asks a withdrawing person for the "Deposit day and time".

45–60 s · READ $/MIN. Here the page redeems itself. "PERSONAL FINANCE LADDER A–M", a Unit dropdown the width of the panel, and my own sheet laid on the right rungs: A Income $3,200 · B Housing $700 (Rent / Mortgage) · C Transportation $1,800 (Auto Payment) · D Insurance $200 · E Utilities $150 · F Food $300 · G Health $50 · L Lifestyle $200 · Net −$200.00 in red. One tap on the dropdown, "per minute": $0.0673 in, −$0.0042 net. I checked the arithmetic by hand and at per second and per year; it is one constant, no drift. This is what the operator asked for in addendum 17 and what the brief locked, and it is right. Two blemishes: the five empty sections print "$0.0000" beside "$3,200.00" (line 141), and the sentence under the table sends me to "the Balance view" (line 363), which does not exist on this revision.

WHAT THE BRIEF PROMISES THAT I DO NOT SEE. (1) N–T "on a second surface (Balance / Goals)" — named in a note, not built; the record files it as FIN-06.06 for r.013, honest on paper, misleading on the glass. (2) "$/min against budget … based off transaction date and how long it lasts" — actualsByField exists (ladder.ts:134), is gated, and is imported by nothing; the ladder is the seed sheet only (line 138) and a person's own entries change no figure in it. (3) "the sheet's figures are seed data the person edits" — no edit path on the page. (4) The U rule and the detail sheet — model only, and the brief allowed that ("model first; styling second"), so I do not count them against this revision. (5) Field labels exactly — yes, I compared all sixty against the pasted brief; they match, including "Alcohol (or Fun)" and "Home Maintenance & Repairs".

WHAT I WOULD PRESS, in order: the Unit dropdown (rewarding), the orbit icon (confusing, reversible), Sign in (leaves the page), the strip's DEPOSIT chip (it is not a button before login). The strongest interaction on the page is the dropdown; the weakest is the first screen's five zeros.

GRADE: C+. The model is an A — exact, pure, gated, honest on the record. The reading surface for the plan is a B+. The stranger's path is a D: he cannot place a transaction, the live number he came for is $0.00, and the one form that will take his money asks its length twice and points him at a view that is not there. The fixes are small and named above; three of them (the pending-example rate, one length per transaction, the Balance-view sentence) belong in r.013 with the one-form fold the operator has already asked for.

### Athena · adversarial — grade C

_ADVERSARIAL PASS on Financial-2525 v.000_r.012 (commit 201a8ec), read-only; code read from the commit, probes run in node and a headless browser under scratchpad/fleet-fin/athena-adv/_

**One sentence:** The model behind the glass honours the lock (sections, fields, fixed factors, Net at every period, U refused off A–M, old hashes untouched), but the glass a stranger meets breaks at the exact step that matters — the three new pickers are torn down and rebuilt every second by the 1 Hz clock so a phone's dropdown loses focus mid-choice, a deposit carries two contradictory lengths (MoT 30.333 vs Timeline 33 days) that yield two different $/min, a one-byte corruption of the record wipes the whole history on the next save, the duplicate-insurance warning and the actuals never reach the screen, and the "ladder" a stranger reads is the operator's own sheet, not theirs.

**Findings**

- **The Section / Field / Timeline pickers are remounted every second — a phone's open dropdown loses focus mid-choice** (CRITICAL)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:157 defines `const LadderPicker = (...) => (...)` INSIDE FinancialCommandUX1 (a new component type on every render); :95 re-renders the whole component at 1 Hz (`setInterval(() => setNow(Date.now()), 1000)`); :306 and :315 mount it on both forms. React reconciles by type identity, so each tick unmounts the three <select>s and mounts fresh ones. Proved in a headless browser with the same shape (scratchpad/fleet-fin/athena-adv/remount.html + remount-probe.mjs): focus the section select → 1.6 s later {sameDomNode:false, stillFocused:false, oldNodeDetached:true}. The r.011 category select was inline JSX and did not have this; it is new in r.012. On a phone, a picker sheet bound to a node that is removed is dismissed or its change lands on a detached node — the 'place a transaction' step cannot be completed calmly. (The forms are behind sign-in, so no headless gate on the export can see this; a source gate must.)
  - Fix: Hoist LadderPicker out of the component body to module scope (it only needs its props and `t`), or inline the three <select>s like the unit dropdown at :341. Add a source gate in tests/financial-surface.test.mjs: no `const [A-Z]\w* = \(` component definition between `export function FinancialCommandUX1` and its `return (`.
- **One deposit, two lengths, two $/min — the default MoT (30.333) and the default Timeline (Every 33 days) disagree, and FD-26's claim is not what the clock does** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:145 `dMot` defaults to SRC.mot.payMotDays (30.333); :151 `dRec` defaults to "days33" (33 d); :190 commits BOTH `motDays` and `recurrence` on the same entry. The clock's rate reads motDays (lib/financial-2525/accrual.ts:75 `amountCents / (motDays * MIN_PER_DAY)`), while the ladder's rate reads the recurrence (lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:126, :129). Probe: $3,604.49 → 8.252 ¢/min by MoT vs 7.585 ¢/min by Timeline (ratio 0.919). The record's FD-26 says 'the timeline is the transaction's MoT: its $/min runs from its date for that length' — on the shipped glass the $/min runs on the typed MoT, not the timeline. (r.013's ladder.ts diff introduces `paymot` and `other`, which acknowledges this.)
  - Fix: One length per entry: derive motDays from the timeline (recurrenceDays) and drop the free-typed MoT, or make the MoT field the only length and drop the timeline's length semantics. Gate: an entry's motDays === lengthDays(recurrence) at commit.
- **A tampered (or merely corrupted) record is thrown away on the next save — the whole history is lost, not preserved** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: lib/financial-2525/record.ts:65 `return { rec: ok ? v : emptyRecord(owner), tampered: !ok }` hands the surface an EMPTY record when one link fails; command-ux1.tsx:114 stores that empty record; :177 `commit` appends to it and `saveRecord(next)` overwrites the device key. Probe (probe-math.mjs, tamperLoad/afterNextSave): five entries saved → one character of one memo changed → loadRecord: {tampered:true, entries:0} → one new deposit → the store holds 1 entry and none of the old ids. The glass shows 'Chain broken' for exactly one session, then the evidence and every real entry are gone. This is the class the 2026-09-09 AAR names (a completed record is never discarded).
  - Fix: On a broken chain keep the raw text under a sibling key (`exel-fin:<owner>:broken:<ts>`) and refuse appends until the person exports or acknowledges; never write over a store that failed verify. Gate in tests/financial-accrual or a new record test: load(tampered) → append → save must not remove the prior raw.
- **The duplicate-insurance WARNING never reaches the glass — rule 5 of the lock is model-only** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: ladder.ts:110 pushes "DUPLICATE_INSURANCE" into `warnings`; command-ux1.tsx has no reference to `warnings` anywhere (grep on 201a8ec: none) — :138 `netLadder(SHEET_LINES, period)` computes it and :354–:360 render sections and Net only. The brief: 'If a premium is entered in both, warn; do not double-count Net.' Net counts both (probe dupB: living 200 from B 100 + D 100) and no one is told. The financial-ladder gate (tests/financial-ladder.test.mjs:51) proves the lib, not the screen.
  - Fix: Render `totals.warnings` under the Net row (amber, one sentence in the person's words: 'A housing premium is on B and on D — pick one'); gate the surface with a regex for `totals.warnings` AND a headless assertion when the fixture has both lines.
- **The 'Personal finance ladder' a stranger reads is the operator's sheet, and their own transactions never move it — the $/min-against-budget promise is not on the glass** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: lib/financial-2525/budget.ts:33–42 SHEET_BUDGET (Income 3,200 · Home 700 · Car 1,800 …) → :77 SHEET_LINES; command-ux1.tsx:138 `netLadder(SHEET_LINES, period)` and :355 render only SHEET_LINES. No budget line is editable on the surface; `actualsByField` (ladder.ts:134) is never imported by command-ux1.tsx (grep: none). Headless on the export: a signed-out stranger sees 'Personal finance ladder A–M' with Income $0.0673/min and Net −$0.0042/min — the operator's numbers presented as personal. FIN-06.06 declares the actuals column for r.013, so this is owed, not hidden — but r.012's title says 'Personal'.
  - Fix: Until FIN-06.06 lands, title the panel as the example sheet (like the deposit's EXAMPLE badge) or hide the sheet figures for a signed-in person whose record has no lines; wire actualsByField(replay(record)) beside the plan when it does.
- **The withdrawal's Timeline is a label with no behaviour — 'Weekly' never repeats, never spreads** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:152 `wRec` default "once", :197 commits `recurrence: wRec` on the withdrawal; accrual.ts:96 sums every withdrawal whole at its atMs and never reads `recurrence`. Probe: a $10 'weekly' withdrawal after three weeks = $10 withdrawn. The wording (lexicon-data.ts:3080–3083 'Weekly', 'Every 33 days', 'Monthly (91)', 'Yearly') reads as repetition; ladder.ts:123 defines it as 'the length its money covers' and the type is even named `Recurrence`. A stranger choosing 'Weekly' for rent expects a repeating charge and gets one entry.
  - Fix: Either implement the length on withdrawals (spread over recurrenceDays in balanceAt) and rename the wording to a length ('over 7 days'), or hide the timeline on a withdrawal until it does something. Never a picker whose value changes nothing.
- **Two different transactions in the same second with the same amount: the second is silently dropped and the form clears as if recorded** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:190 `id: \`d-${when}-${cents}\`` and :197 `id: \`w-${when}-${cents}\``; record.ts:35 `append` returns the SAME record when the id exists; :191/:200 then clear the amount with no refusal. `when` defaults to `at` (the 1 Hz `now`) or to a typed stamp — two $50 withdrawals typed against the same stamp (a split rent) lose the second. Probe sameIdDropped: {entries:1, sameObject:true}.
  - Fix: Make the id unique (a counter or rev in the id) and keep idempotency only for a true double-tap (same id within the same render), or surface a refusal ('already on the record') instead of clearing the field.
- **A typo in the MoT box turns a 30-day escrow into an instant release** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:190 `motDays: Math.max(0, Number(dMot) || 0)` — '3O.333' (letter O) → NaN → 0; accrual.ts:59 `if (len <= 0) return tx.amountCents` releases the whole amount at once. Probe motTypo: 360449 of 360449 cents released at t=1 ms. No refusal, no reason line (the refusals at :187–:188 cover amount and stamp only).
  - Fix: Refuse a non-numeric or zero MoT on a deposit with its own reason (fin.reason_mot), the way the stamp is refused; or, once the timeline is the one length (defect 2), drop the free-typed box.
- **The 90 ladder keys shipped English in all 32 other languages** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: tests/lexicon-coverage.test.mjs:19–28 stages fin.sec.*, fin.field.*, fin.rec.*, fin.section, fin.field, fin.timeline, fin.ladder_title, fin.stock_note in AFTER_FILL; :78 counts them as PENDING, not failing. At 201a8ec `lib/i18n-app/es.ts` contains 0 of these keys (grep), so t() fell back to English for every section, field and timeline on r.012 for a Spanish, Swahili or Japanese reader. The fill landed later (50e57c0: 88 keys in es.ts). Said in the commit ('STAGED … listed, never silent'); it is still a shipped surface that read half-English for a day.
  - Fix: None owed now — the fill is in. Keep the r.004 pattern; for a taxonomy-sized addition (90 keys) ship the fill in the same push, or hold the revision.
- **Alcohol's one target is decided by a parameter no one can set, and if set, the total and the row would disagree** (LOW)
  - Evidence: ladder.ts:100 `netLadder(lines, period, alcohol = "F")`; command-ux1.tsx:138 never passes it. If it were 'L', :105 moves the money into sections.L while :355 groups field rows by `fieldOf(l.fieldId)?.section` — the row would print under F while the total sits in L (probe alcoholL: sections.L=60, fieldSection 'F'). The sheet has no alcohol line, so it is latent.
  - Fix: Store the alcohol target on the person's settings, pass it to netLadder, and group rows by the same resolved section.
- **Both forms offer every section A–M — a deposit filed under Housing or a withdrawal under Income is accepted without a word** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:161 lists FLOW_SECTIONS for both hooks; :190/:197 commit whatever field is chosen; nothing checks the field's `kind` (Income vs Fixed/Variable/Transfer) against the transaction's kind (accrual.ts:30 `kind: TxKind`). ladder.ts:22 has the kinds; no rule joins them.
  - Fix: Filter the section list by kind (deposit → A; withdrawal → B–M), or at least refuse the mismatch with a reason.
- **On the perihelion anchor the day flips at 11:15 in the morning (Austin), and 2026's year is 364.39 days — day 365 lasts 9.3 hours and pastFull can never fire** (LOW)
  - Evidence: lib/financial-2525/calendar.ts:104 counts `day` as whole 24 h periods from the perihelion INSTANT (2026-01-03 17:15Z = 11:15 CST), not from CST midnight (the comment at :80 says midnights only on the calendar anchor). Probe: Day 270 at 11:14 CST → Day 271 at 11:16 CST on 2026-09-30. Interval table: 2026→2027 = 364.39 d, so the 'down day' (day 365, :67 DOWN_DAY) runs 2027.01.02_11.15 → 2027.01.02_20.33 CST (9.3 h) and `pastFull` (:112) is false all year; 2027 is 367.41 d. The glass prints '271/365' (command-ux1.tsx:290 Math.ceil(lengthDays) = 365 for a 364.39-day year). This is the operator's own rule (addendum 18) and the code says so — but a stranger sees 'Day N' change mid-morning with no line explaining it.
  - Fix: Print the day-boundary instant beside 'Day N' when the anchor is perihelion (it is already known: year.startMs), or name CST midnights as the day and keep the perihelion for the A.B..C only.
- **Mars is anchored to EARTH's perihelion table and its A.B..C can never reach 3600 before the reset** (LOW)
  - Evidence: lib/planet-ltu.ts:54–55 Mars row `yearAnchor: "perihelion"`; calendar.ts:97–101 resolves 'perihelion' through `perihelionOf(year)` — the Earth table (:34). Probe: positionInYear(now, 'perihelion', 686.98) → startMs === Earth's 2026 perihelion, abc.a = 1416 of a 686.98-day whole; the maximum before Earth's next perihelion is 1909.5 A. Declared 'Earth hours for now' (FD-16), but the anchor is not declared as Earth's.
  - Fix: Give the Mars row its own anchor table (Mars perihelia) or mark the row DECLARED with 'opens at Earth's perihelion' on the anchor note (command-ux1.tsx:292).
- **The gates read class names and source text; the layout and the behaviours above have no measured gate, and r.012 shipped with no capture at all** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:51 (dropdowns exist — regex), :53 (Net red — regex on 'text-red-500'), :54 (full-width — regex on `w-full … landscape:w-auto`), :35 (icons — regex over the icon file). The record's own r.011 entry says 'a layout claim needs a measured capture, not a class-name gate'. r012-shots.mjs exists in the scratchpad but shots/ holds no r012-*.png (ls: none; r012-chain.log has tsc/test:ci/build only). The three new pickers sit behind sign-in, so no headless gate can measure them — and the r.012 export on disk is already r.013 (stamp read: v.000_r.013). Only financial-ladder.test.mjs is behavioural, and only on the lib.
  - Fix: Add a diag door (?diag=1 with a stub owner, the sign-URL pattern) so the forms can be rendered and measured headless; gate the remount (defect 1), the warning on the glass (defect 4) and the record-preservation invariant (defect 3) behaviourally; produce the two-orientation captures before the bookkeeping commit.
- **The stranger's first minute has no ticking number: the example deposit is dated tomorrow** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:83–86 EXAMPLE reads SRC.example.depositStamp = '2026.10.01_07.00..00' (lib/financial-2525/domain.gen.ts:387); on 2026-09-30 the phase is 'deposit' (:75), the clock reads $0.00 and the rate ladder (:267 `bal.ratePerMinCents > 0 &&`) is hidden. Headless on the export at 390×844: clock '$0.00', pill 'Released · Deposit', guide 'Sign in to record your own deposits…'; the ladder panel starts 1,562 px down a 2,774 px page (1.85 screens), and 5 of its 13 section rows read $0.0000. The one thing the surface is for — money moving per minute — is not visible on landing until Oct 1.
  - Fix: Anchor the example to a rolling stamp (today − 1 day at 07:00 CST) so the demo always ticks, or open the guide with the per-minute figure of the example even while pending; collapse $0 sections behind a 'show all' until the person has a line there.

**Keep**

- The model is the lock: 20 sections A–T in the brief's order, 60 fields with the slash-merged labels verbatim, U refused off A–M and never a section or chip (ladder.ts:31–76, :117–121; tests/financial-ladder.test.mjs 59 assertions pass).
- Net = I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr holds at every one of the eight periods on the sheet (probe: −200 per 33 d → −0.0042087 per minute → −2,212.12 per 365-day year), transfers subtract once, a balance in P never nets against income, negative and unknown fields are never counted (ladder.ts:104, :108–111).
- toPeriod is one constant ratio of integer second counts (ladder.ts:27, :88) — a round trip returns the amount to a billionth; the print rounding is the only rounding.
- The record's hash is stable under the new optional keys: an r.006–r.011 entry with `category` and no `field` hashes identically with `field: undefined` present (probe oldHashStable: true; record.ts:22–29 key-sorted JSON drops undefined), and the glass still prints its category icon (command-ux1.tsx:181).
- The unit dropdown IS full width in portrait: measured 290 px of a 290 px content box at 390×844, 224 px of 668 px in landscape by design (block unchanged between 201a8ec and the r.013 export it was measured on); no horizontal scroll at either width; no page errors.
- Section → field cascade resets the field to the section's first (command-ux1.tsx:160), so a field that does not belong to the shown section cannot be produced from the UI.
- Honest words on the record: the staged keys are listed, FIN-06.06 names what is owed for r.013, the year's 363–368-day swing and the 365-day ladder year vs the 365.259636-day MoT whole are both said in the source (ladder.ts:13–15, calendar.ts:16–20).

**Report (verbatim)**

ATHENA · ADVERSARIAL · Financial-2525 v.000_r.012 (201a8ec) — GRADE C

What I did. Read every file under review from the commit (git show 201a8ec:…), the ask through addendum 24, the record JSON, the r.007–r.011 captures (there are NO r012-*.png in shots/ — r012-shots.mjs exists, the chain log ran tsc → test:ci → build and stopped). Extracted the r.012 lib files into scratchpad/fleet-fin/athena-adv/ and ran three probes: probe-math.mjs (ladder, accrual, record with a fake device store), probe-cal.mjs (calendar), and two headless-browser runs — remount-probe.mjs (a minimal reproduction of the component shape) and export-probe.mjs against frontend/out at 390×844 and 844×390. Caveat stated plainly: the export on disk is already the r.013 build (stamp v.000_r.013); the unit-dropdown block and the ladder table are byte-identical between 201a8ec and that build (git diff), so the width measurement stands; the forms are behind sign-in and cannot be reached headless at all.

The 60-second path, as a stranger on a phone.
LAND (0–10 s): 'FINANCIAL · 2525 · the MoT Financial System', a five-phase rail, a 'YOUR TURN' card saying sign in, and a clock reading $0.00 with the pill 'Released · Deposit'. The worked example is dated 2026.10.01_07.00 — tomorrow — so nothing ticks and the per-hour/per-day/per-second ladder under the clock is hidden (command-ux1.tsx:267). The one promise of the surface, money per minute, is not on the first screen.
UNDERSTAND (10–30 s): the year panel says 'Day 271 · Quarter 3 · 89/91 · 33-day frame 9 · 7/33 · 271/365 · Perihelion (Austin CST standard) 2026.01.03_11.15..00'. Clear enough for the operator; a stranger learns 'Day' flips at 11:15 in the morning only by watching (probe: day 270 at 11:14, 271 at 11:16 CST). The ladder is 1,562 px down; it is titled 'Personal finance ladder A–M' and shows the operator's sheet — $0.0673/min income, Net −$0.0042 in red — as if it were theirs. Five of thirteen rows read $0.0000.
PLACE A TRANSACTION (30–50 s): behind sign-in. Once in, the deposit form has Amount, Deposit day and time, MoT · length in days (30.333), Memo, then Section, Field, Timeline (Every 33 days). Two lengths on one entry, disagreeing by default, giving two different $/min (8.25 vs 7.59 ¢/min on the paycheck). And the three new pickers are defined inside the ticking component (:157 under the 1 Hz :95), so every second React unmounts and remounts them — proved: focus is lost and the old node is detached within 1.6 s. On a phone this is the step that fails.
READ $/MIN (50–60 s): the clock ticks on motDays; the ladder never reads the person's entries (actualsByField is unused); a 'Weekly' withdrawal is one entry that never repeats or spreads; a typo in the MoT box releases the whole escrow at once; two same-amount entries in one second lose the second silently; a corrupted record is wiped on the next save.

The lock itself holds. Sections, fields, planes, fixed factors, Net at every period, U refused off A–M, alcohol one target, duplicate-insurance flagged in the model, old hashes stable, the unit dropdown at full portrait width (290/290 px) — all proved. What fails is everything between the model and the person: the warning is computed and discarded (:138 keeps only totals), the actuals exist and are not drawn, the record's tamper path destroys evidence, and the picker remount is a regression the class-name gates cannot see. The gates in financial-surface.test.mjs are regexes over source; the record's own r.011 lesson says a layout claim needs a measurement, and r.012 shipped without one.

Lexicon: the 90 ladder keys were staged in AFTER_FILL, so on the shipped r.012 every section, field, timeline and the two labels read English in all 32 other languages (es.ts at 201a8ec: 0 of them; 88 after 50e57c0). Said in the commit; still true on the glass for a day.

Calendar: the 2026 perihelion year is 364.39 days, so the down day is 9.3 hours and pastFull cannot fire; 2027 is 367.41. Mars uses Earth's perihelion table and tops out at 1,909 A of its 3,600 before the Earth reset. Both are declared placeholders; the anchor note should say Earth's.

Order of repair I would ask for: (1) hoist LadderPicker — one line, the whole transaction step; (2) never overwrite a store that failed verify; (3) one length per entry, the timeline's or the MoT's, not both; (4) draw totals.warnings; (5) a diag door so the forms can be measured headless, then the captures. Grade C: an A− model under a D+ glass, averaged honestly at the step a stranger needs.

### Athena · synthesis — grade C

**One sentence:** The lock's model is exact, pure and gated, and the ladder reads right at every unit in two taps, but the stranger's sixty seconds break at the one step that matters: the first screen's one promised number reads $0.00 because the example is dated fourteen hours after the ship, nobody can place a transaction without signing in, the signed-in person's three new pickers are torn down every second by the clock so a phone dropdown cannot be finished, and the one deposit the form takes carries two contradicting lengths (30.333 and 33 days) that give two different $/min.

**Top three**

- frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:157 — `const LadderPicker = (…) => (` is defined INSIDE FinancialCommandUX1 (body runs :88–:226), whose 1 Hz clock (:95 `setInterval(() => setNow(Date.now()), 1000)`) re-renders it every second; :306 and :315 mount it on both forms, so each tick unmounts and remounts the three <select>s (the UI framework reconciles by component identity, and the identity is new on every render). Re-ran the reviewer's headless reproduction myself: focus the section select → 1.6 s later {sameDomNode:false, stillFocused:false, oldNodeDetached:true}. On a phone the picker sheet closes or its change lands on a detached node — the 'place a transaction' step cannot be completed by the only person allowed to attempt it. (The operator hit this himself: addendum 26 → r.014.) FIX: hoist LadderPicker to module scope, or inline the three selects like the unit dropdown at :341; add a source gate in tests/financial-surface.test.mjs that refuses any component definition between `export function FinancialCommandUX1` and its `return (`.
- frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:145 + :151 + :190 — one deposit is asked for its length twice and the two defaults disagree: `dMot` = SRC.mot.payMotDays (30.333, label 'MoT · length in days', :304) and `dRec` = "days33" ('Timeline', :170); :190 writes BOTH `motDays` and `recurrence` into the same chain-hashed entry. The clock rates on motDays (lib/financial-2525/accrual.ts:75 `amountCents / (motDays * MIN_PER_DAY)`), the ladder's actuals on recurrenceDays (lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:126, :129): on the $3,604.49 paycheck that is 8.2521 vs 7.5852 ¢/min (ratio 0.919). FD-26 on the record says 'the timeline IS the transaction's MoT' — the shipped glass does not do that. Same line, same class: `Math.max(0, Number(dMot) || 0)` turns a typo ('3O.333') into motDays 0, which accrual.ts:59 releases at once with no refusal. FIX: one length per transaction — derive motDays from the timeline (recurrenceDays; add 'other' → a days input), drop the free-typed box or make it the only length, refuse a non-numeric or zero MoT with its own reason beside fin.reason_amount / fin.reason_stamp (:187–:188), and gate `motDays === lengthDays(recurrence)` at commit.
- frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:85 + lib/financial-2525/domain.gen.ts:387 — the worked example is stamped `2026.10.01_07.00..00` CST (13:00 UTC), 13.96 h AFTER the ship commit (2026-09-30 23:02 UTC). balanceAt filters `x.atMs <= t` (accrual.ts:95), so for the whole review window the big clock (:263) read $0.00, the rate line fell back to the phase word 'Released · Deposit' (:262), the per-hour/day/second ladder was hidden (:267 `bal.ratePerMinCents > 0 &&`), the phone strip read '$0.00 · $0.00' with no words (:392), and the signed-out guide card never says 'pending' (:206 returns the sign-in sentence; the pending sentence at :208 is only reached when `owner` is set) — only the chart's small mono line (:447) says 'pending from …'. Capture p-01-first-screen.png shows exactly this: five zeros under 'the MoT Financial System'. It self-heals at 13:00 UTC on Oct 1, but the class remains: the surface has no honest state for a pending example. FIX: when the focus deposit is pending, print its rate anyway ('$0.0825 per minute from 2026.10.01_07.00..00' — depositView computes ratePerMinCents regardless of state, accrual.ts:75) on :262 and the pending sentence on the guide card for a signed-out reader too; or anchor the example to a rolling 'yesterday 07:00 CST' so the demo ticks on day one. Prefix the strip's two figures with their words.

**Owed next**

- HOIST THE PICKERS (CRITICAL): LadderPicker to module scope or inline selects (command-ux1.tsx:157, :306, :315); source gate refusing a component defined inside FinancialCommandUX1; a diag door (?diag=1 with a stub owner, the sign-URL pattern) so the forms can be rendered and measured headless. **Report (verbatim)**

ATHENA · SYNTHESIS · Financial-2525 v.000_r.012 (commit 201a8ec) — GRADE C

WHAT I DID. I read every file under review from the commit itself (git show 201a8ec:…): command-ux1.tsx and category-icon.tsx, ladder / budget / accrual / record / mot / calendar / planets, the ask through addendum 24 (addendum 22 is the lock), the record JSON at revision 0.012, the lexicon entries the reviewers cite, and the surface gate. I looked at the captures the fleet made on the export (p-01-first-screen.png, p-06-ladder-m33.png, athena-portrait-landing.png). I re-ran the adversarial reviewer's remount reproduction in a headless browser rather than take it on faith, and checked the arithmetic behind the two-rate and the pending-example claims with a short script. Caveat, said plainly: the export on disk is now r.017 and the captures were made on the r.013 build, so no pixel measurement here is of r.012 bytes; every line I cite is from the r.012 file, and what the captures show ($0.0000 rows, 'Personal finance ladder A–M', the stock note, '$0.00 · $0.00' on the strip, five zeros on the first screen) matches the r.012 source line for line.

THE TWO REVIEWERS. The walk (C+) and the adversarial pass (C) agree on four things: the stranger cannot place a transaction; the one deposit the form takes carries two lengths; the first screen's number is $0.00 because the example is dated tomorrow; and the sentence under the ladder points at a Balance view that does not exist. They disagree on what matters most. The walk never saw the most consequential defect because it is invisible from outside sign-in: the three new pickers are defined inside the ticking component and are torn down every second. The adversarial pass found it, proved it, and I confirmed it — and the task list shows the operator then hit it on his own phone (addendum 26 → r.014). That single fact settles the ranking: the walk's path analysis is right about the stranger, the adversarial pass is right about the person who actually gets to the form, and the grade has to be C, not C+, because the step the whole surface exists for fails for both of them.

THE SIXTY SECONDS, RECONCILED.
0–10 s, land: Trinity glyphs, 'FINANCIAL · 2525 · the MoT Financial System', a five-phase rail, a 'YOUR TURN' card, and a clock reading $0.00 beside 'Released · Deposit'. The example deposit is stamped 2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST — 13:00 UTC, 13.96 hours after the ship commit — so balanceAt (accrual.ts:95) drops it, the rate line falls back to a phase word (:262), the rate ladder is hidden (:267), the strip reads '$0.00 · $0.00' with no words (:392). The signed-out guide card never says 'pending' (:206); only the chart's small mono line does (:447). The surface's one promise, money moving per minute, is not on the first screen. It self-heals at 13:00 UTC on October 1; the class — no honest pending state — does not.
10–30 s, understand: the rail, the roster row, the chart with its clock ⇄ orbit toggle, the year line. Clear enough; the orbit reveal is confusing but reversible and never forced. 'Day' flips at 11:15 in the morning on the perihelion anchor (calendar.ts:104) and nothing says so — declared in the source, not on the glass.
30–50 s, place a transaction: behind sign-in (:297), by design since r.001. The guide button says 'Sign in ↓' (:252 appends the arrow to every label) but its action is a login redirect (:206), not a scroll. Once in, the deposit form asks the length twice: 'MoT · length in days' 30.333 (:145, :304) and 'Timeline' Every 33 days (:151, :170), both written into one hashed entry (:190). The clock rates on the first (accrual.ts:75), the ladder's actuals on the second (ladder.ts:126, :129): 8.2521 vs 7.5852 ¢/min on the paycheck. And the three pickers are a component defined inside the component body (:157) under a 1 Hz setNow (:95): every tick the selects are unmounted and remounted. Reproduced: focus lost and the old node detached within 1.6 s. On a phone this is the step that fails.
50–60 s, read $/min: here the page is right. Thirteen sections A–M in order with their stroke icons, the operator's sheet on the right fields, Net −$200.00 red; one tap on the unit dropdown and it is $0.0673 in, −$0.0042 net per minute, one constant at every period, no drift (ladder.ts:27, :88). Two blemishes: five empty sections print '$0.0000' beside '$3,200.00' (:141), and the note under the table sends the reader to a Balance view that is not there (:363, lexicon:3088; FIN-06.06 is draft).

WHAT I KEPT, RE-RANKED, OR DROPPED.
Kept at the top: the picker remount (adversarial; confirmed by re-run and by the operator's own addendum); the two lengths (both; confirmed by source and arithmetic); the pending example (both; the walk had it high, the adversarial low because it self-heals — for this lens, the stranger's first screen, it belongs in the top three, with its self-healing said).
Kept, high, but off this lens's path: a tampered or merely corrupted record is replaced by an empty one on load (record.ts:65), adopted (:114), appended to and written over the device key on the next save (:177). The glass shows 'Chain broken' for one session and then every real entry is gone. That is the 2026-09-09 AAR's class — a completed record is never discarded — and by consequence it outranks my third item; it sits first in what r.013 owes.
Kept, medium: the duplicate-insurance warning is computed (ladder.ts:110) and never drawn (no `warnings` in the component); actualsByField and ratePerMinute are exported and imported by nothing (git grep at 201a8ec), so the ladder is the seed sheet only (:138, :355) and titled 'Personal' with no EXAMPLE badge; the withdrawal's timeline is written (:197) and never read (accrual.ts:96); two same-amount entries in one second collide on id (:190/:197, record.ts:35) and the field clears as if recorded (:191/:200); a typo in the MoT box becomes 0 and releases the whole escrow at once (:190, accrual.ts:59) — same class as the two-lengths item, folded into its fix.
Kept, low: '$0.0000' for empty sections (:141); 'Deposit day and time' on the withdrawal form (:314); the unlabeled strip (:392); the inherited tab title (page.tsx exports no metadata; the export's <title> confirms); the invalid svg height (:448); the guide arrow (:252); both forms offering every section (:161); Alcohol's unset parameter (ladder.ts:100); the 9.3-hour down day and the never-firing pastFull on the 364.387-day perihelion year (calendar.ts:104, :112, declared at :18–:20); Mars resolving through Earth's perihelion table (planet-ltu.ts:55 → calendar.ts:97–:101, declared 'for now').
Re-ranked down: the lexicon staging. True — the 90 keys sat in AFTER_FILL (tests/lexicon-coverage.test.mjs:28) and es.ts at 201a8ec holds none of them, so the ladder read English in 32 languages for a day — but it was said in the commit and the fill landed; nothing is owed now beyond the lesson that a 90-key addition ships with its fill.
Dropped: nothing outright. One claim softened — the walk's '965 px to a second Sign in button' is a measurement of a later build I cannot reproduce on r.012 bytes; the structural claim (the arrow is appended regardless of the action, :252 vs :206) holds and is what I cite.

STRENGTHS, VERIFIED. The lock is in the model, not in prose: twenty sections A–T in the brief's order and sixty fields with the slash-merged labels verbatim (ladder.ts:31–:76); U refuses a non-flow destination (:118) and is never a section; Net ignores N–T and unknown fields (:104); the duplicate-insurance rule fires in the model (:110); Alcohol has one target (:83); no 30-day month exists (:27). toPeriod is one constant ratio (:88). The transaction's new field and timeline are optional so no r.006–r.011 hash moves (accrual.ts:35–:37) and an older entry still prints its category (:181). The ladder on the glass is exactly the brief's thirteen rows with the sheet under the right fields and Net last and red (:352–:360). The unit dropdown is the panel's full width in portrait (:340–:341). The record says where it lives in the person's words and names no vendor (:376, gate :57). The chart's toggle is one state for the whole card (:107, :277), and the reveal is honest. The ask was persisted whole and hashed before the build.

GRADE: C. An A− model under a D glass at the step a stranger needs, averaged honestly. The fixes are small and named: hoist the picker (one move), one length per transaction (the fold r.013 was already asked for), never overwrite a store that failed verify (one key), print the pending rate (one line), draw the warning (one row), tell the truth under the ladder (one sentence). r.013 owes them in that order, each with a measured gate — the record's own r.011 lesson says a regex over source is not proof, and r.012 shipped with no capture at all.
