## Christo

### Christo · walk — grade C

_Constructive walk — a stranger with the operator's own sheet, on a phone, 60 seconds_

**One sentence:** The ladder table is the one place on this surface where a couple would agree what every row means; the transaction side (a withdrawal that is both "money moved to my bank" and "rent I paid", a deposit with two different lengths, a refusal that appears under the wrong panel) is where they would argue — and the operator's own rent cannot be recorded on the day he pays it.

**Findings**

- **A withdrawal means two things at once — and the operator's own rent is refused on day 1** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:152 defaults the withdrawal to B · Rent / Mortgage and :197 files a ladder field + timeline on it (FIN-03.02's own dtm: 'a withdrawal typed as B · Rent / Mortgage · Monthly (91) appears on the record as Rent / Mortgage', record.json:944). But :198-199 still runs validateWithdrawal, and frontend/lib/financial-2525/accrual.ts:112-120 refuses anything above what escrow has released past the 3-hour hold (reasons HOLD / INSUFFICIENT; lexicon 'Held — withdrawable 3 hours after the deposit', 'More than is withdrawable'). With the sheet (Income 3,200 per 33 days ≈ $97/day) a $700 rent on the day it is paid is refused until day 7.2 of the pay period; car 1,800 until day 18.6. Two readings of one row — 'I moved released pay to my account' (r.001 law, phases WITHDRAW/RECORD) and 'I paid rent' (r.012 field) — and the code enforces the first while the words promise the second.
  - Fix: Decide the word. Either a withdrawal stays an escrow draw (then drop the ladder field from it and add a third kind, 'Spend', that is never validated against escrow and feeds actualsByField), or a withdrawal IS the spend (then validateWithdrawal must not gate it). State the invariant in the operator's terms before the code, and put the refused rent from his own sheet in the gate.
- **One deposit, two lengths: 'MoT · length in days 30.333' and 'Timeline · Every 33 days' on the same form** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:145 (dMot default 30.333) and :304 render the MoT input; :151 (dRec default 'days33') and :169-173 render the Timeline dropdown beneath it. The accrual reads only motDays (accrual.ts:53, :75); recurrence is stored and never used for the rate. FD-26 (record.json:1125-1128) says 'the timeline is the transaction's MoT: its $/min runs from its date for that length' — the record says one thing, the glass shows two numbers 8.8% apart, and the code obeys neither consistently. A couple entering a paycheck will ask which one is the length; the honest answer is 'the top one, the bottom one is written down and ignored'.
  - Fix: One length per transaction. Let the Timeline dropdown drive motDays (once → instant, weekly → 7, every 33 days → 33, monthly-91 → 91, yearly → 365, plus the operator's 30.333 pay MoT as its own option, which r.013's 'Length (MoT)' dropdown already moves toward) and remove the free-text MoT field, or label the second one 'Repeats' if it is truly a recurrence.
- **The refusal for the DEPOSIT form is printed under the WITHDRAWAL form** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:187-188 set refusal from recordDeposit ('Enter an amount above zero', 'The day and time must read …'), but the only render of {refusal} is :318 inside the #fin-withdraw-form panel (:310-320); :319 saveFailed likewise. A stranger who presses 'Record it' with an empty amount sees nothing happen where they pressed and a red 'Refused ·' line under the next panel, attributed by position to a withdrawal they never made.
  - Fix: Render the refusal beneath the button that was pressed (one refusal slot per form, or one slot keyed by which action set it), and scroll it into view.
- **'In escrow: $0.00' beside a listed $3,604.49 deposit — three answers to 'is the money in?'** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: Probe of the export at 390 px (signed out, example dated 2026-10-01 07:00, now 2026-09-30): roster 'YOUR DEPOSITS 1. … $3,604.49 · 30.333' (command-ux1.tsx:213-216), record 'Deposit · … $3,604.49' (:370), rail 'DEPOSIT 1 · RELEASE $0.00', and the balance block 'Released · Deposit $0.00 / In escrow: $0.00 · Withdrawable: $0.00 …' (:262-266) because accrual.ts:95 counts only deposits with atMs <= t. The chart alone says 'Pending — releases from 2026.10.01_07.00..00' (:447). One partner reads 'the payer has put it in escrow' (fin.ph.deposit_earns says exactly that), the other reads 'escrow is empty'.
  - Fix: Say 'Pending: $3,604.49 from 2026.10.01_07.00..00' in the balance block when a deposit is dated ahead, or count a dated-ahead deposit as escrowed with its release start shown; the words in the rail, the block and the chart must give one answer.
- **The bare number after the dollar amount — '$3,604.49 · 30.333' — has no word anywhere on the glass** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:215 (roster label `${usd(d.amountCents)} · ${d.motDays}`), :370 and :373 (the record), :447 (the chart line '2026.10.01_07.00..00 · $3,604.49 · 30.333 · Pending …'). The only place the unit is named is the form input 'MoT · length in days' (lexicon 2911), which a signed-out stranger never sees. Days? A-units? A rate? A couple would not agree.
  - Fix: Print '30.333 d' (or '30.333 days') everywhere the MoT appears beside money; on the record, 'over 30.333 d'.
- **Empty sections print '$0.0000' in a different format from the filled rows** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:141 usdUnit sends |cents| < 100 — including exactly 0 — to usd4, so H · Family, I · Debt service, J · Taxes, K · Work and M · Transfers read '$0.0000' at per 33 days while their neighbours read '$700.00' (probe rows; r013-ladder.png shows the same). Four decimals on a zero reads as 'a tiny amount we have not seen', not 'nothing here'; and the same column mixes two formats.
  - Fix: Zero prints as '—' or '$0.00' in the column's format; keep usd4 only for non-zero values under a dollar. Consider folding empty sections (the brief's 13 rows can stay, dimmed).
- **'Monthly (91)' and 'per month (91)': monthly does not mean 91 days to anyone entering a paycheck** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:134 labels the ladder period 'per month (91)' — the brief's own word, locked — but :171 offers the transaction Timeline 'Monthly (91)' (lexicon 3082, fin.rec.month91) next to 'Every 33 days' (3081). The timeline words are the build's own choice (FD-26, DECLARED). The operator's own example ('I got paid monthly', 30.333 days) has no matching option among once · 7 · 33 · 91 · 365 (ladder.ts:124-126). A person paid monthly who picks 'Monthly (91)' gets a quarter of accrual; the partner who picked 'Every 33 days' gets a different $/min for the same pay.
  - Fix: Name the timeline by its length only: 'every 7 days', 'every 33 days', 'every 91 days', 'every 365 days', and add the 30.333-day pay MoT (r.013 moves this way). Keep 'per month (91)' on the ladder because the brief locked it, but never write 'Monthly' for 91.
- **The note promises 'the Balance view' — there is no such view to press** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:363 renders fin.stock_note (lexicon-data.ts:3088: 'Cash, investments, debts, net worth, credit, protection and goals live on the Balance view — never per minute'); FIN-06.06 is 'draft (planned for r.013)' (record.json:955-969). A stranger scrolls for the door and finds none.
  - Fix: Until the view ships, say so in the note ('… will live on a Balance view — not on this ladder') or omit the sentence.
- **The section/field/timeline pickers are re-created every second** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:157 defines LadderPicker as a component inside FinancialCommandUX1's body, so its identity changes on every render; :95 re-renders the whole surface at 1 Hz (setNow). React therefore unmounts and remounts the three <select>s each tick. By construction an opened list on desktop closes within a second and keyboard focus is dropped; on phones the native picker sheet is bound to an element that is removed beneath it. Not reproduced here because the forms are sign-in gated in the export; the pattern is textbook and the unit dropdown (:341, not inside LadderPicker) does not have it.
  - Fix: Hoist LadderPicker to module scope (as MotChart is at :417) and pass t/labels as props; add a surface gate that no component is declared inside the 1 Hz component.
- **The withdrawal form asks for 'Deposit day and time (CST)'** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:314 uses t('fin.deposit_at') (lexicon-data.ts:2910 'Deposit day and time (CST)') on the withdrawal form. HEAD (r.013) already relabels it 'Day and time (CST)' on the merged form.
  - Fix: Already folded in r.013; record it as answered.
- **The phone strip shows two dollar figures with no words, and the balance heading glues a phase word to a money word** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:392 renders `${usd(bal.releasedCents)} · ${usd(bal.availableCents)}` — the probe shows '$0.00 · $0.00 DEPOSIT'; nothing says released · available. :262 prints 'Released · Deposit' when the rate is zero (the phase label after the word Released), which reads as a row title, not 'phase: deposit'.
  - Fix: Strip: 'Released $x · Available $y'. Heading: keep 'Released' and put the phase in the pill only.
- **The sheet's words were re-filed without saying so: Insurance → Auto / Renters / Home, Fitness → Health, Other → Fun** (LOW)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/budget.ts:72-75 CATEGORY_FIELD: Insurance 200 → D.auto_renters_home (the brief says housing insurance stays in B; the sheet does not say which policy the 200 is), Fitness 50 → G.mental_physical (the ladder shows it as 'G · Health · Mental Health / Physical Fitness'), Food 300 → 'Groceries', 'Dining Out' → 'Dining / Work', and both Fun and Other → L.fun_hobbies_clothing, so an old 'Other' entry is counted as Fun. The operator's word 'Insurance' now appears twice on the ladder (D · Insurance and the field Auto / Renters / Home) and once more as B's 'Housing insurance'.
  - Fix: Flag the four re-filings on the record for the operator's word (as r.008 did for Car/Auto), and give Other its own destination or keep it off the ladder.
- **Words that say more than the mode shows** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:288 label 'Year position — the orbit (A.B..C)' (lexicon 2931) stays when the Clock icon is pressed and the line reads '271/365'; :239 and :437 print 'Money as time — this MoT' twice on one card; :161 offers all thirteen sections on both forms, so a deposit can be filed under 'M · Transfers' and a withdrawal under 'A · Income'.
  - Fix: Drop '(A.B..C)' from the year label (the toggle says which reading is on); make the chart's label a sub-label; limit the deposit's sections to A (+ M for a transfer in) and the withdrawal's to B–M.
- **No r.012 capture exists and the export on disk is r.013** (LOW)
  - Evidence: scratchpad/shots/ holds r005–r011 and r013 PNGs only; r012-shots.mjs names r012-ladder-portrait/landscape/ledger but no such file landed; frontend/out/financial-2525/index.html prints v.000_r.013. This walk's pictures of the ladder are therefore r.013's (its ladder code is unchanged from 201a8ec) and its form pictures are r.013's merged form, not the two r.012 panels.
  - Fix: Re-run r012-shots.mjs against a 201a8ec build if the record is to carry r.012 captures; otherwise note on the record that r.012 shipped without its own pictures.

**Keep**

- The ladder table is the clearest thing on the surface: 'A · Income' with its icon, 'Income / Wages (take-home)' beneath, one figure in one unit, Net last and red — a couple reads the same −$200.00 (and −$0.0042 per minute) and agrees what it is (command-ux1.tsx:347-362; ladder.ts:100-112 gives exactly the locked Net).
- The thirteen sections, the sixty field labels and the fixed factors match the brief word for word (ladder.ts:31-76, :27); amortize refuses N–T (ladder.ts:117-121); the duplicate-insurance warning exists (ladder.ts:110). The model is what the lock asked for.
- The unit dropdown is one control, full width in portrait (measured 290 px of a 316 px panel at 390 wide), eight readable options, and switching per 33 days → per minute is one constant (the probe's two columns agree to the cent).
- No horizontal overflow at 390×844 and no page errors in the export; the chart's five axis marks read as '0 h 0 min · 7 d 13 h · … · 30 d 7 h' in Clock mode and as three-line A.B..C in MoT mode without overprint.
- The guide card says the next action in one sentence and the signed-out state is honest: 'EXAMPLE · the operator's paycheck as data — sign in to record your own'; the record's sentence names no vendor.
- The section icon in the picker's label changes with the chosen section (command-ux1.tsx:159), so the word and the picture agree while choosing.
- The perihelion line — 'Perihelion (Austin CST standard) · 2026.01.03_11.15..00 CST' — is one sentence, sourced, and needs no explanation to a stranger.

**Report (verbatim)**

I opened /financial-2525 on a 390-wide phone with the operator's sheet in my other hand (Income 3,200 · Home 700 · Car 1,800 · Insurance 200 · Electric 150 · Food 300 · Fitness 50 · Fun 200, every 33 days). Code read from commit 201a8ec; pictures from the r011/r013 captures and my own headless probe of frontend/out, which is r.013 on disk (its ladder and balance code are unchanged from 201a8ec; its form is the r.013 merged form). Written under scratchpad/fleet-fin/ only.

Seconds 0–10, the shell. Globe, three glyphs, 'FINANCIAL · 2525 · the MoT Financial System · Version 000 · revision'. A card titled 'Money as time — this MoT' with a pill 'DEPOSIT', a five-step rail 'DEPOSIT 1 · HOLD · RELEASE $0.00 · WITHDRAW 0 · RECORD 1', and one line under it: 'The payer puts the period's pay into escrow — amount, day and time, MoT'. I understood: a paycheck goes into a box and comes out by the minute. I did not yet know what MoT was; the next line told me nothing.

Seconds 10–25, the money. An amber card: 'YOUR TURN — Sign in to record your own deposits — the example below is the operator's paycheck as data', a Sign in button. Then 'YOUR DEPOSITS · 1. 2026.10.01_07.00..00 · Income / Wages (take-home) · PAYROLL · PENDING DIRECT DEPOSIT · $3,604.49 · 30.333'. I pressed nothing; I asked '30.333 what?' — the word 'days' never appears beside it on the glass (only on the gated form's label). Then the cyan block: 'Released · Deposit $0.00 · In escrow: $0.00 · Withdrawable: $0.00 · Withdrawn: $0.00 · Available: $0.00'. Here two people argue: one says 'the deposit is listed, the payer put it in escrow — the rail says so'; the other says 'escrow is $0.00'. The chart below is the only one that says 'Pending — releases from 2026.10.01_07.00..00'. Five money words (released, in escrow, withdrawable, withdrawn, available) for one paycheck is more words than a couple will hold in their heads; 'Released · Deposit' reads like a row name.

Seconds 25–35, the chart and the year. The MoT ⇄ Clock toggle works as a pair of icons, ringed when pressed; Clock reads '0 h 0 min · 7 d 13 h · 15 d 3 h · 22 d 17 h · 30 d 7 h', MoT reads three-line A.B..C. I understood the axis; I would not press the orbit icon twice. The year panel: 'Day 271 · Quarter 3 · 89/91 · 33-day frame 9 · 7/33 · 2026.09.30 CST · 2026 · SOURCED · 271/365 · Perihelion (Austin CST standard) · 2026.01.03_11.15..00 CST'. Its label says '(A.B..C)' even when I chose the clock. 'SOURCED' is a word for the operator, not for me.

Seconds 35–50, the forms — read in code, since they are behind Sign in. Deposit: Amount · Deposit day and time (CST) · MoT · length in days [30.333] · Memo · Section [A · Income] · Field [Income / Wages (take-home)] · Timeline [Every 33 days] · Record it. Two lengths on one paycheck: 30.333 and 33. The record (FD-26) says the timeline IS the MoT; the accrual uses only the top number. Withdrawal: Amount · 'Deposit day and time (CST)' (on a withdrawal) · Section [B · Housing] · Field [Rent / Mortgage] · Timeline [One time] · Withdraw. This is the row the couple will fight over. To one it is 'I moved $700 of released pay to our account'; to the other it is 'we paid the rent'. The code sides with the first (validateWithdrawal, HOLD, INSUFFICIENT) while the words side with the second — and with this very sheet, rent 700 on the first of the period is refused as 'More than is withdrawable' for seven days, car 1,800 for eighteen. The '$/min against budget' the brief asks for cannot be typed on the day it happens. And if I get the amount wrong on the deposit, the red 'Refused' line appears under the withdrawal panel, not under the button I pressed.

Seconds 50–60, the ladder and the record. This is the good part. 'PERSONAL FINANCE LADDER A–M · Unit [per 33 days]' full width; 'A · Income $3,200.00 / Income / Wages (take-home) $3,200.00', B Housing 700, C Transportation 1,800, D Insurance 200 (Auto / Renters / Home), E Utilities 150, F Food 300 (Groceries), G Health 50 (Mental Health / Physical Fitness), then H, I, J, K each '$0.0000', L Lifestyle 200, M Transfers '$0.0000', 'Net −$200.00' in red. Switch to per minute: 0.0673 in, 0.0147 rent, net −0.0042 — the same constant everywhere. I understood every row; I would pick this table over my sheet. Two quibbles a couple would raise: '$0.0000' on the empty sections looks like a tiny number in a different format from '$700.00', and 'Fitness' has become 'Health', 'Insurance' has become 'Auto / Renters / Home' without anyone saying so. The closing note sends me to 'the Balance view' — there is no such thing to press. The record reads '2026.10.01_07.00..00 · Deposit · PAYROLL · PENDING DIRECT DEPOSIT · $3,604.49 · 30.333 · On this phone only — a cloud copy follows once sign-in carries it': honest, vendor-free, and again a bare 30.333.

What the brief (addendum 22) promises that I do not see: the N–T Balance / Goals surface (declared draft, but the glass still names it), the U amortize entry (model only), the detail sheet, the duplicate-insurance warning on the glass (computed in netLadder, never rendered), the actuals beside the plan (actualsByField exists, no column), and a deposit whose timeline is its MoT (two numbers instead). What it promises that I do see: 13 + Net, exact labels, fixed factors, no drift, red Net, no 'affordable' copy, no U chip.

Grade C. The ladder alone would be a B+; the transaction words — one row with two meanings, one deposit with two lengths, a refusal under the wrong panel — pull the whole to a C, because the operator said the goal is to track $/min against budget on his own finances first, and on this revision his own rent is refused.

### Christo · adversarial — grade C-

_Adversarial pass (B) on Financial-2525 v.000_r.012, commit 201a8ec, read-only_

**One sentence:** The A–M model in ladder.ts is sound and behaviourally gated, but the glass over it lets a couple record two paychecks and keep one, shows one deposit with two lengths and never shows the timeline it recorded, prints the gym at $0.0000 per second, labels a withdrawal's date "Deposit day and time", promises a Balance view that does not exist, anchors Mars to Earth's perihelion while printing SOURCED beside "declared, not sourced", and rebuilds every picker once a second so the dropdown closes under the finger — and most of this passed because the surface gate reads class names, not behaviour.

**Findings**

- **Every picker on both forms is destroyed and rebuilt once a second — the dropdown closes under the person's finger** (critical)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:157 declares `const LadderPicker = (...) => (...)` INSIDE `FinancialCommandUX1`'s render body; command-ux1.tsx:95 ticks `setInterval(() => setNow(Date.now()), 1000)`. A component whose identity changes every render is unmounted and remounted by the UI library on every tick, so the three `<select>` nodes (section · field · timeline, :160 / :165 / :170) are new DOM nodes each second. Headless proof (fleet-fin/remount.mjs, the same pattern with the library from node_modules): after 2.5 s `nestedPickerSameNode:false`, `focusStillOnPicker:false`, while the inline unit select (`inlineUnitSelectSameNode:true`) survives. The operator later reported exactly this on his phone (ask addendum 26, 'this drop down goes away quick!'). The regex gate financial-surface.test.mjs:51 only checks the selects EXIST in the source text.
  - Fix: Hoist `LadderPicker` (and any picker) to module level with a stable identity; pass `t`/labels as props. Add a glass gate: mark a select's DOM node, wait > 1 s, assert the same node is still in the document — and a source gate that no component declared inside a render body renders a `<select>`.
- **Two deposits with the same instant and amount: the second is silently dropped — a couple's two paychecks become one** (critical)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:190 builds the id as `d-${when}-${cents}` (withdrawals :197 `w-${when}-${cents}`); record.ts:35 `if (rec.entries.some((e) => e.tx.id === tx.id)) return rec;` returns the SAME record with no refusal, and :177 `commit` then sets state and saves it as if it had been recorded. `when` falls back to `at`, the 1 Hz tick (:117 · :186), so two 'Record it' taps within one second with the same amount, or two partners typing the same day-and-time stamp for equal paychecks, keep one entry and say nothing. Probe (fleet-fin/probe-model.mjs §1): `same-id second deposit kept? NO — entries 1, rec2 === rec true`.
  - Fix: Make the id unique to the entry (a counter or random suffix, or `rev` in the id) and make `commit` refuse aloud when `append` returns the same record (`fin.reason_duplicate`). Gate it: two appends with equal instant and amount → two entries, or one refusal shown.
- **A record that fails its chain is read as EMPTY, and the next entry overwrites the old entries on the device for good** (critical)
  - Evidence: record.ts:63–66 return `emptyRecord(owner)` with `tampered:true` for any parse failure, owner mismatch or hash break; command-ux1.tsx:114 sets that empty record as state; :177 `commit` appends to it and `saveRecord(next)` (record.ts:70 `localStorage.setItem`) replaces the stored copy. Probe (probe-model.mjs §2): after one byte moved in entry 2 of 3, `loadRecord → {tampered:true, entries:0}`; one new deposit → store holds `entries:1, firstId:'d4', threeOldEntriesStillOnDevice:false`. The screen says 'This device's record failed its chain — shown empty, never repaired silently' (lexicon-data.ts:2952) and then does repair it silently by burying the evidence. The r.001 invariant 'a transaction, once recorded, is on the record for good' (record.ts:10) does not hold across a broken chain.
  - Fix: Never write over a store that failed verification: keep the raw bytes under a quarantine key (`exel-fin:<owner>:broken:<ts>`), refuse `commit` while `tampered` is true (or append to a new record with a 'continues after break' entry), and surface the quarantined copy on the diag door. Gate: tamper → load → append → save → the original bytes still exist somewhere on the device.
- **One deposit carries two lengths: the MoT field (30.333) and the timeline (Every 33 days) disagree, and only one of them does anything** (high)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:85 the worked example itself: `motDays: SRC.example.motDays` (30.333) AND `recurrence: 'days33'` (33 d). The deposit form shows both controls side by side: 'MoT · length in days' (:304, default `SRC.mot.payMotDays`) and 'Timeline' (:169–172, default `days33` :151). accrual.ts:53 `motMs` reads only `motDays`; `recurrence` is declared (:37) and never read; `actualsByField` (ladder.ts:134) is imported by nothing on the glass. Probe §4: the same paycheck runs at 8.252 ¢/min by its MoT and 7.585 ¢/min by its timeline — 8.79 % apart. FD-26 in the record says 'the timeline is the transaction's MoT: its $/min runs from its date for that length' — the code does not do that.
  - Fix: One length per transaction. Either the timeline sets `motDays` (weekly → 7, every 33 days → 33, one time → 0) with a single 'Length' control (as addendum 25 later asks), or the MoT field is the only length and the timeline is removed. Correct FD-26 on the record to say what runs the $/min. Gate: a FinTx never has `recurrenceDays(recurrence) > 0` differing from `motDays`.
- **The timeline chosen at entry is hashed into the record but never shown back; the MoT prints as a bare number** (high)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:179–183 `txWhat` prints only `tx.field` (or the old `category`); the roster row :215 prints `${usd(d.amountCents)} · ${d.motDays}` and the record line :373 `${e.tx.motDays ? \` · ${e.tx.motDays}\` : ''}` — '· 30.333' with no unit and no word for it; `recurrence` appears in neither. A couple cannot see whether the rent was recorded weekly or once; the record's own reader cannot tell what its timeline was.
  - Fix: Print the timeline word beside the field on the roster and the record (`t(\`fin.rec.${tx.recurrence}\`)`), and print the length with its unit ('30.333 d'). Gate: the record line for an entry with `recurrence` contains its timeline label.
- **Per second, the sheet's Fitness row reads $0.0000 — a real cost printed as nothing; one column mixes two number formats** (high)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:140–141 `usdUnit = cents >= 100 ? usd(Math.round(cents)) : usd4(cents)` — four decimals under a dollar, two above, and 4 decimals of dollars cannot carry $50 / 33 d / 86,400 s = $0.0000175. Headless on the export at 390 px (fleet-fin/probe-glass.mjs, code for these hooks unchanged since 201a8ec): per second `Mental Health / Physical Fitness = $0.0000`, `NET -$0.0001`; per hour `Income = $4.04` beside `Rent / Mortgage = $0.8838`; per 33 days the empty sections H–K and M print `$0.0000` under `$3,200.00` (also visible in shots/r013-ladder.png). Two people read '$0.0000' as 'we spend nothing on the gym'.
  - Fix: One format per column: choose the decimals from the UNIT (per second/minute → significant-figure or ¢-per-unit notation, e.g. '0.0175 ¢'), never from the value; zero rows print '—' or '$0.00' in the column's format. Gate: every amount cell in one ladder render matches one regex.
- **A withdrawal's date field is labelled 'Deposit day and time (CST)'** (medium)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:314 the WITHDRAWAL form uses `t('fin.deposit_at')` for its stamp input; lexicon-data.ts:2910 `fin.deposit_at` = 'Deposit day and time (CST)'. The person is asked, on the withdrawal panel, for the deposit's day and time.
  - Fix: A neutral key (`fin.at` = 'Day and time (CST)') on both forms, or `fin.withdraw_at` on the withdrawal form (r.013's one form makes the neutral word the right one).
- **'Withdrawal' means two different things on one screen: escrow → own account (the law) vs. paying the rent (the picker)** (medium)
  - Evidence: accrual.ts:12–14 define a WITHDRAWAL as money 'withdrawn to the person's own account' from escrow and validateWithdrawal (:113–120) refuses anything beyond the released, un-held balance; command-ux1.tsx:152 defaults the withdrawal form to section `B` · `B.rent_mortgage` and :159–172 offer every A–M field — Income included — as the 'field' of a withdrawal. A rent payment to a landlord and a transfer to checking are the same button and the same refusal ('HOLD' / 'INSUFFICIENT'). One partner reads the entry as 'the rent is paid', the other as 'the money moved to checking'; the record cannot say which, and a withdrawal placed on section A (Income) is accepted.
  - Fix: Say which it is: either the field on a withdrawal is the outflow it paid (then say so in the panel title and refuse section A for a withdrawal), or withdrawals carry no ladder field and outflows are a separate transaction type feeding the ladder's actuals. Gate: a withdrawal with a section-A field is refused.
- **The ladder tells a signed-in person it is theirs but shows the operator's seed sheet, and its note points to a Balance view that does not exist** (medium)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:138 `netLadder(SHEET_LINES, period)` — the table is always budget.ts:33–42 (Income 3,200 · Car 1,800 …), for the signed-in owner too; no entry from the person's record reaches it and no 'example' word appears in the budget panel (:334–364; the example badge :253 sits in the guide card only). :363 prints `fin.stock_note` = 'Cash, investments, debts, net worth, credit, protection and goals live on the Balance view — never per minute' (lexicon-data.ts:3088); no route or panel named Balance exists at 201a8ec (git grep over frontend/app and frontend/components: none) — FIN-06.06 is a draft for r.013.
  - Fix: Label the table as the example sheet until the person's own lines exist (or seed the signed-in ladder from their record via actualsByField), and word the note in the present tense of what ships ('…are not on this ladder; a Balance view follows') until the view exists.
- **Mars is anchored to Earth's perihelion and its year line prints SOURCED beside 'declared, not sourced'** (medium)
  - Evidence: calendar.ts:97–101 the perihelion anchor reads `perihelionOf(g)` — Earth's table (:34–47) — with no planet argument; `positionInYear(now, planet.yearAnchor, planet.yearDays)` (command-ux1.tsx:125) therefore opens the Mars year at 2026.01.03_11.15 CST, counts Earth days and Earth quarters, and `status` (:109) is the Earth row's SOURCED. Headless on the export, planet = Mars: 'Day 271 · Quarter 3 · 89/91 … 2026 · SOURCED · 271/365' followed by 'no perihelion table for this planet yet — declared, not sourced'. The chart header line command-ux1.tsx:447 calls `positionInYear(from, planet.yearAnchor)` WITHOUT `planet.yearDays` while the axis :433 passes it: once the example starts releasing, on Mars the header reads 2669.0833..0514 → 2968.0708..2340 above axis marks 1419.0722..1931 … 1578.0566..0971 (probe-cal.mjs). The gate financial-surface.test.mjs:38 regex-checks three call sites and misses :447.
  - Fix: Give `positionInYear` the planet (its own perihelion table or a DECLARED anchor that returns status DECLARED and `lengthDays` of its revolution); pass `planet.yearDays` at :447; on a planet without a table print DECLARED, not SOURCED. Gate on the rendered text: the year line's status word and the anchor note never contradict.
- **The duplicate-insurance warning and the alcohol target exist only in the model — no glass reads them, and moving alcohol to L splits the table** (medium)
  - Evidence: ladder.ts:110 pushes `DUPLICATE_INSURANCE` into `warnings`; command-ux1.tsx never reads `.warnings` (grep: no match) and the sheet can never raise it (budget.ts:73 bridges the one Insurance line to `D.auto_renters_home`; there is no `B.housing_insurance` line). ladder.ts:105 moves `F.alcohol` into section L when `alcohol === 'L'`, but the table :355 lists fields by `fieldOf(l.fieldId)?.section` — still F — so the row would sit under F while its money is in L's total (probe §3: `F:0, L:60, fieldSectionForTableFilter:'F'`); no caller ever passes the target. Rule 5 and rule 6 of the lock are claimed on the record as shipped.
  - Fix: Render `totals.warnings` on the ladder (one sentence under the D row) and gate it on the glass; when the alcohol target is L, place the row under L too (filter by the effective section) — or defer rule 6 honestly on the record until a control exists.
- **The section, field and timeline words read English in the other 32 languages at r.012** (medium)
  - Evidence: tests/lexicon-coverage.test.mjs:28 stages the 90 keys (`fin.sec.*`, `fin.field.*`, `fin.rec.*`, `fin.section`, `fin.field`, `fin.timeline`, `fin.ladder_title`, `fin.stock_note`) in AFTER_FILL; at 201a8ec lib/i18n-app/fr.ts carries 99 `fin.*` keys and 0 of these, so `t()` falls back to the English default on every picker, every ladder row and the N–T note for a non-English reader. Listed, not silent — but the r.012 ship is English-only for the whole new surface (the ×32 fill landed later as 50e57c0).
  - Fix: Say 'English until filled' on the record for r.012 (the ask's r.004 pattern already does); ship the fill with the surface next time or keep the staged list on the same commit.
- **The surface gate passes on regexes over the source text, not on behaviour — the same class that let r.010 ship a half-width select** (medium)
  - Evidence: tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:23–79 are `/…/.test(ux)` string matches: :54 asserts the class text `w-full … landscape:w-auto` (the record's revision 0.011 note: 'its gate read class names (w-full on the select) and passed, while the r.010 capture measured the select at 145 px of the panel's 292 px'); :51 asserts the three picker selects exist but not their width, identity or that they render at all (they need a login — r012-shots.mjs shoots only [data-fin-budget] and [data-fin-ledger], and no r012-*.png exists in shots/); :56 asserts no typed `86400|1440|525600` but not the Net figures; :38 checks three `positionInYear` call sites and misses :447. Only financial-ladder.test.mjs is behavioural, and it is model-only.
  - Fix: Add a glass gate in test:ci with a forged login (the r.013 capture already has one): measure each picker's width against the panel at 390 px, hold a marked select node across 2 s, read the Net cell at three units and compare to netLadder, read the Mars year line. Regex gates stay as source hygiene, never as the proof of a decision.
- **Small words a couple will argue over: 'per month (91)', 'Monthly (91)', a Fixed Fitness line, and 'Chain verified'** (low)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:134 `${t('fin.per_month')} (91)` and lexicon-data.ts:3082 `fin.rec.month91` = 'Monthly (91)' — a month of 91 days is a quarter to anyone who did not read the brief (the brief locks the factor, not the word). budget.ts:74 bridges `Fitness` → `G.mental_physical` (kind Fixed) and `Other` → `L.fun_hobbies_clothing` — a parking ticket recorded as Other becomes 'Fun'. lexicon-data.ts:2951 `fin.chain_ok` = 'Chain verified' over an FNV-1a chain that any hand editing the store can recompute (record.ts:24 says 'not a signature'); 'verified' promises more than the hash can. The example deposit is dated 2026.10.01 07:00 CST (domain.gen example), so every visitor today sees Pending, $0.00 released and no ladder line (:267 hides it at rate 0).
  - Fix: Word the 91-day base as 'per 91 days (the month)' so the number leads; map Other to a neutral field or keep it off the ladder; say 'Chain intact' rather than 'verified'; date the example in the past so the clock moves on first sight.

**Keep**

- The model is the lock, exactly: ladder.ts:31–76 carry the twenty sections and sixty labels verbatim; Net = I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr (:108–111); transfers outflow; P never nets against I; U is a rule that refuses N–T (:117–121); a period switch is one constant (:88) — probe §8 year→second→year drifts 0 cents.
- financial-ladder.test.mjs is a real behavioural gate (Net at all eight periods, odd-cent round trips, duplicate-insurance, alcohol one target, U refusals, windowed actuals) — the one gate in this revision that would catch a model regression.
- The unit dropdown is genuinely full width in portrait now: measured 290 of 292 px at 390 × 844, 224 px at its own width at 844 × 390; its DOM node survives the 1 Hz tick (it is inline, not a nested component).
- Old r.006–r.011 entries keep their category and their hash: `field` and `recurrence` are optional on FinTx (accrual.ts:35–37) and the record line still prints the category icon (command-ux1.tsx:181).
- The record says where it lives without naming a vendor (lexicon-data.ts:2953) and says when the device would not take a save (:319).

**Report (verbatim)**

FINANCIAL-2525 v.000_r.012 (commit 201a8ec) — Christo, adversarial pass. Read from the commit, never the working tree; the built export under frontend/out had moved to revision 0.014 while I read, so every headless measurement was taken on hooks whose lines are byte-identical between 201a8ec and the tree (checked by diff), and is said so. Probes and captures are under scratchpad/fleet-fin/ (probe-model.mjs, probe-glass.mjs, probe-cal.mjs, remount.mjs, christo-b-ladder-per-second-390.png). No repository file was touched.

WHAT HOLDS. The model is the lock. ladder.ts carries the twenty sections and sixty slash-merged labels as the brief writes them, the two planes, U as a refusing rule, the fixed factors, and the Net equation; toPeriod is one multiplier and a year→second→year round trip on an odd cent drifts zero. The gate financial-ladder.test.mjs is behavioural and would catch a regression in any of that. The unit dropdown measures 290 of 292 px in portrait and 224 px at its own width in landscape, and its node survives the clock. Old entries keep their category and their hash.

WHERE TWO PEOPLE WOULD DISAGREE ABOUT WHAT A ROW MEANS — and the code lets them.
1. Two paychecks, one entry. The id is instant + amount (command-ux1.tsx:190, :197); the instant defaults to the 1 Hz tick; append() returns the same record for a repeated id with no refusal (record.ts:35) and commit() saves it as recorded (:177). Two 'Record it' taps in one second with the same amount, or two partners typing equal paychecks on the same stamp, keep one. Proven: probe §1.
2. A broken chain empties the record and the next entry buries it. loadRecord returns an empty record on any failure (record.ts:63–66); commit appends to that and saveRecord overwrites the store (:70). Proven: probe §2 — three entries, one byte moved, one new deposit, the three are gone from the device. The screen's sentence 'never repaired silently' is then untrue.
3. One deposit, two lengths. The example carries motDays 30.333 AND recurrence days33 (:85); the form shows 'MoT · length in days' next to 'Timeline: Every 33 days'; the accrual reads only motDays (accrual.ts:53), the timeline is never read anywhere on the glass, actualsByField is imported by nothing. Probe §4: 8.252 vs 7.585 ¢/min for the same paycheck. FD-26 says the timeline IS the MoT; the code says otherwise.
4. The timeline is hashed and never shown (:179–183, :215, :373); the MoT prints as a bare '· 30.333'. A couple cannot see whether rent was recorded weekly or once.
5. 'Withdrawal' means escrow → own account in the law (accrual.ts:12–14) and 'the rent' on the form (:152, section B · Rent / Mortgage, any A–M field accepted, Income included).
6. The withdrawal panel asks for 'Deposit day and time (CST)' (:314 uses fin.deposit_at).
7. Per second the gym reads $0.0000 and Net -$0.0001 (measured); per hour $4.04 sits over $0.8838; per 33 days $3,200.00 over $0.0000 (usdUnit :140–141 picks decimals from the value, not the unit).
8. The signed-in ladder is the operator's seed sheet with no 'example' word on the panel (:138, :334–364), and its note sends the reader to a Balance view that does not exist at 201a8ec.
9. Mars opens at Earth's perihelion (calendar.ts:97–101) and prints 'SOURCED · 271/365' one line above 'declared, not sourced' (measured); the chart header (:447) omits planet.yearDays while the axis (:433) uses it — 2669 → 2968 above marks 1419 … 1578 once the example releases (probe-cal.mjs).
10. The duplicate-insurance warning is never rendered and the sheet can never raise it (budget.ts:73); the alcohol target has no caller and would split the row from its total (:355 vs ladder.ts:105; probe §3).

THE PICKER. LadderPicker is declared inside the render body (:157) under a 1 Hz setNow (:95). Headless proof with the same library: the nested picker's DOM node is replaced and focus is lost within 2.5 s; the inline unit select is not. The operator's addendum 26 ('this drop down goes away quick!') arrived after the ship; the code had it before the ship. No r012 form capture exists (the forms need a login the r.012 shot script never forged), so no eye saw the pickers before Verify Live.

THE LEXICON. Ninety keys (sections, fields, timelines, the five labels) are staged in AFTER_FILL at 201a8ec with zero fills in the per-language stores — the whole new surface reads English in 32 languages at r.012. Listed, not silent; but shipped English-only.

THE GATES. financial-surface.test.mjs is regexes over source text (:23–79): it asserts class names, existence of selects, absence of typed constants, and three of four positionInYear call sites. It is the same class of gate that passed r.010's half-width select (the record's own 0.011 note: 145 of 292 px). Nothing in test:ci renders the pickers, holds a select across a tick, reads Net off the glass, or reads the Mars year line.

GRADE C-. B+ for the model and its gate; D for the glass and its gates; the words on the screen do not yet let two people agree on what a row means, and three of the defects lose or confuse money on the record. Fix the class each time: one id law, one 'never overwrite unverified bytes' law, one length per transaction, one number format per column, one planet argument through the calendar, one module-level picker, and a glass gate with a forged login that measures instead of greps.

### Christo · synthesis — grade C-

**One sentence:** The ladder table is the one place a couple agrees what a row means; every transaction row is a place they argue — a withdrawal that is both "moved to my bank" and "paid the rent" and is refused on the day the rent is due, one deposit with two lengths and no word for either, and two equal paychecks silently kept as one — so the operator's own first use of this surface (track $/min against budget on his own finances) cannot be done on r.012.

**Top three**

- 1. A withdrawal means two things at once, and the operator's own rent is refused on the day he pays it. frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:152 defaults the withdrawal to B · Rent / Mortgage and :197 files a ladder field and timeline on it (the record's own dtm, financial-2525.v.000.json:946: 'a withdrawal typed as B · Rent / Mortgage … appears on the record as Rent / Mortgage'); but :198-199 still runs validateWithdrawal, and frontend/lib/financial-2525/accrual.ts:12-14 defines a withdrawal as escrow → own account while :112-120 refuses anything above what has released past the 3-hour hold ('More than is withdrawable'). With his example paycheck ($3,604.49 over 30.333 d ≈ $118.83/day) a $700 rent on day 1 is refused until day ~5.9; the $1,800 car until day ~15.1 (with the sheet's 3,200/33 d: 7.2 and 18.6 days). One partner reads the row as 'the rent is paid', the other as 'money moved to checking'; the code enforces the second while the words promise the first. Still gated at r.013 (1c89639:215). FIX: state the invariant in the operator's words before the code — either a withdrawal IS the outflow (then it is never validated against escrow, it refuses section A, and it feeds actualsByField), or a withdrawal stays an escrow draw and a third kind 'Spend' carries the ladder field. Gate it with the operator's own sheet: rent 700 recorded on the deposit's day must be accepted and appear under B.
- 2. One deposit carries two lengths, and neither is printed with a word. command-ux1.tsx:145 ('MoT · length in days', default 30.333, rendered :304) and :151 (Timeline, default 'Every 33 days', rendered :169-172) sit on the same form; the worked example carries both (:85: motDays 30.333 AND recurrence 'days33'). accrual.ts:53 and :75 read only motDays; recurrence is hashed and never read anywhere on the glass; FD-26 (financial-2525.v.000.json:1126) says 'the timeline is the transaction's MoT: its $/min runs from its date for that length' — which the code does not do (probe: 8.252 vs 7.585 ¢/min for the same paycheck, 8.79 % apart). Then the roster (:215 `${usd(d.amountCents)} · ${d.motDays}`), the record (:370, :373) and the chart line (:447) print a bare '30.333' with no unit, and the timeline word appears nowhere a signed-out stranger or a partner can see. r.013 already made the length ONE dropdown that drives motDays (1c89639:207, 'Length (MoT) · 30.333 days (pay MoT)') — record that as answered, do not rebuild it. FIX for what is still owed: print the length with its unit ('30.333 d') and the timeline word beside the field on the roster, the record line and the chart line; correct FD-26 to say what actually runs the $/min; gate that a FinTx never has recurrenceDays(recurrence) > 0 differing from motDays, and that a record line for an entry with a recurrence contains its timeline label.
- 3. Two equal transactions at one stamp keep one entry, and nobody is told. command-ux1.tsx:190 builds the deposit id as `d-${when}-${cents}` (withdrawals :197 `w-${when}-${cents}`); frontend/lib/financial-2525/record.ts:35 returns the SAME record for a repeated id with no refusal; :177 commit then sets state and saves it as if recorded. A couple on one payroll who each type $3,604.49 at 2026.10.01_07.00..00, or two $700 rent withdrawals typed with the same stamp, end with one row and no word (probe-model.mjs §1: 'entries 1, rec2 === rec true'). The 'two taps within one second' member is weaker than the adversarial pass said — :191 clears the amount after the first commit, so the second tap refuses on amount — but the same-stamp member stands and is the one a couple hits. Same id law at r.013 (1c89639:207, :214). FIX: make the id unique to the entry (the record's rev or a counter suffix) and make commit refuse aloud when append returns the same record (a new key, fin.reason_duplicate, in the operator's voice: 'This exact entry is already on the record'); gate: two appends with equal instant and amount → two entries, or one visible refusal.

**Owed next**

- ALREADY ANSWERED since 201a8ec — record as answered, never rebuild (NO REWORK): r.013 (1c89639) merged the two panels into one form, so the refusal now renders under the button that was pressed (1c89639:332) and the stamp label reads 'Day and time (CST)'; r.013 made the length one dropdown driving motDays (1c89639:207); r.014 (84f28f5) hoisted every picker to module level so the 1 Hz clock no longer remounts a <select> (the class the operator reported in addendum 26 on his r.011 phone and that command-ux1.tsx:157 under :95 carried at r.012).
- THE WITHDRAWAL WORD (top 1): one invariant in the operator's terms, then either a Spend kind never gated by escrow or a withdrawal that carries no ladder field; refuse section A on an outflow (command-ux1.tsx:161 offers all thirteen sections to both forms); gate with the sheet's rent 700 on the deposit's day. Open at r.013 (1c89639:215).
- ONE ID PER ENTRY (top 3): unique ids (rev/counter suffix) at command-ux1.tsx:190/:197 and a spoken refusal when record.ts:35 returns the same record; gate: equal instant + amount twice → two entries or a visible refusal. Open at r.013 (1c89639:207/:214).
- NEVER WRITE OVER A STORE THAT FAILED VERIFICATION: record.ts:63-66 returns an empty record on any parse failure, owner mismatch or hash break; command-ux1.tsx:114 adopts it, :177 appends to it and record.ts:70 overwrites the device copy — probe-model.mjs §2 shows three old entries gone after one new deposit, while lexicon-data.ts:2952 promises 'never repaired silently'. Owed: keep the raw bytes under a quarantine key, refuse commit while tampered (or append to a new record with a 'continues after a break' entry), show the quarantined copy behind the diag door; gate: tamper → load → append → save → the original bytes still exist on the device.
- THE LENGTH AND THE TIMELINE SAID BACK (top 2, remaining half): '30.333 d' with its unit and the timeline word on the roster (:215), the record line (:370, :373) and the chart line (:447); FD-26 corrected to what runs the $/min; the two gates named in top 2.
- ONE NUMBER FORMAT PER COLUMN: command-ux1.tsx:140-141 picks decimals from the value, so H · Family, I · Debt service, J · Taxes, K · Work and M · Transfers print '$0.0000' per 33 days beside '$700.00' (shots/r013-ladder.png; the operator's own addendum 31 screenshot notes 'five sections at $0.0000'), per hour '$4.04' sits over '$0.8838', and per second the gym reads '$0.0000' (probe §5). Owed: zero prints '—' or '$0.00' in the column's format; decimals chosen from the UNIT, never the value; gate: every amount cell in one ladder render matches one pattern.
- THE BALANCE BLOCK FOR A DATED-AHEAD DEPOSIT: accrual.ts:95 counts only deposits with atMs <= t, so the roster lists '$3,604.49 · 30.333' while :262-266 print 'Released · Deposit $0.00 / In escrow: $0.00 / Withdrawable: $0.00' (fleet-fin/christo-390-balance.png) and only the chart (:447) says Pending. Owed: 'Pending: $3,604.49 from <stamp>' in the block (or count a dated-ahead deposit as escrowed with its release start), so the rail, the block and the chart give one answer; heading 'Released' with the phase in the pill only (:262 'Released · Deposit' reads as a row name); strip (:392) 'Released $x · Available $y' instead of two bare figures.
- 'MONTHLY (91)' NEVER: lexicon-data.ts:3082 fin.rec.month91 = 'Monthly (91)' beside 'Every 33 days' (3081) — a 91-day 'month' is a quarter to anyone who did not read the brief, and a person paid monthly who picks it gets a quarter of the accrual. The ladder keeps 'per month (91)' (command-ux1.tsx:134) because the lock names it; the timeline options name their length only ('every 91 days').
- THE NOTE THAT POINTS AT A DOOR THAT IS NOT THERE: command-ux1.tsx:363 renders fin.stock_note (lexicon-data.ts:3088 'live on the Balance view') while FIN-06.06 is draft (financial-2525.v.000.json:963-970) and no Balance route or panel exists at 201a8ec. Owed: present tense of what ships ('are not on this ladder; a Balance view follows') until the view lands.
- THE SHEET'S WORDS RE-FILED WITHOUT SAYING SO: budget.ts:72-75 sends Insurance 200 → D · Auto / Renters / Home (the brief keeps housing insurance in B; the sheet does not say which policy the 200 is), Fitness 50 → G · Mental Health / Physical Fitness, Food 300 → Groceries, Dining Out → Dining / Work. Owed: flag these on the record for the operator's word, as r.008 did for Car/Auto. (The adversarial claim that an old 'Other' entry 'becomes Fun' is dropped: CATEGORY_FIELD is used only at budget.ts:77 to bridge the sheet's eight lines, the sheet has no Other line, and recorded entries keep their own category at :181 — the Other → L row is latent, not on the glass.)
- RULE 5 ON THE GLASS OR DEFERRED ON THE RECORD: ladder.ts:110 pushes DUPLICATE_INSURANCE into warnings; nothing in frontend/components or frontend/app reads .warnings at 201a8ec, and the sheet can never raise it (one Insurance line → D only). The alcohol target (ladder.ts:105) has no caller passing 'L' (command-ux1.tsx:138 uses the default) and :355 filters rows by the field's static section, so if it ever were passed the row would sit under F while L's total carried it. Owed: render totals.warnings under the D row and gate it, or mark rules 5 and 6 as model-only on the record until a control exists.
- WORDS THAT SAY MORE THAN THE MODE SHOWS: fin.year_position (lexicon-data.ts:2931 'Year position — the orbit (A.B..C)', rendered :288) keeps '(A.B..C)' when the Clock icon is pressed and the line reads '271/365'; fin.chart_title is printed twice on one card (:239 and :437); 'SOURCED' (:290) is a word for the operator, not the person. Owed: drop '(A.B..C)' from the label (the toggle says which reading is on); the chart's label becomes a sub-label.
- THE PLANET THROUGH THE CALENDAR (outside this lens's centre, verified, a words contradiction): calendar.ts:97-101 anchors every planet on Earth's perihelion table and :290 prints that table's SOURCED one line above :292's 'declared, not sourced' on Mars; command-ux1.tsx:447 calls positionInYear(from, planet.yearAnchor) WITHOUT planet.yearDays while :433 passes it; financial-surface.test.mjs:38 regex-checks three call sites and misses :447. Owed: positionInYear takes the planet (its own table or a DECLARED anchor with its revolution's length); :447 passes planet.yearDays; DECLARED without a table; gate on the rendered year line.
- A GLASS GATE THAT MEASURES: financial-surface.test.mjs:23-79 are regexes over the source text (the same class that passed r.010's 145 px select — the record's own revision 0.011 note). r.014 added a probe on the glass for the picker; still owed in test:ci with a forged login: picker width against the panel at 390 px, a marked select node held across 2 s, the Net cell read at three units and compared to netLadder, the Mars year line read as text.
- BOOKKEEPING ON THE RECORD: r.012 shipped without a capture of its own in scratchpad/shots/ (r012-shots.mjs named r012-ladder-portrait/landscape/ledger; none landed); the fleet built 201a8ec itself and captured it under scratchpad/fleet-fin/shots/r012-*.png (stamp v.000_r.012, forged login, both panels visible) — say so on the record rather than leaving the revision unpictured. Also: at 201a8ec the 90 ladder keys were staged in lexicon-coverage.test.mjs:28 with zero fills in lib/i18n-app (fr.ts: 99 fin.* keys, none of the 90), so the whole new surface read English in 32 languages until 50e57c0 — listed, not silent; now moot under the operator's addendum 30 (no translation until the English is final), which the record should cite.

**Report (verbatim)**

FINANCIAL-2525 v.000_r.012 (commit 201a8ec) — Christo, the synthesis. Two reviewers of this lens reported: a walk (grade C) and an adversarial pass (grade C-). I read every file they cite from the commit with `git show 201a8ec:<path>`, never from the working tree (the export on disk has moved to v.000_r.017 while this fleet ran); I re-ran the adversarial reviewer's `probe-model.mjs` under scratchpad/fleet-fin/ to confirm the two record defects, and I read the captures: the fleet's own r012-forms-390.png and r012-ledger-dup-390.png (built from 201a8ec, stamp v.000_r.012, a forged login), christo-390-balance.png, and the record's r013-form-portrait.png and r013-ladder.png. I touched no repository file. Lens question: where would two people — a couple — disagree about what a row means, and are the words on the glass the reason.

WHAT HOLDS. The two reviewers agree, and I confirm, that the ladder is the one surface on this page where a couple reads the same thing. ladder.ts:31-76 carries the twenty sections and sixty slash-merged labels exactly as the brief writes them; :27 the fixed factors; :108-111 the locked Net (I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr); :117-121 U as a rule that refuses N–T; toPeriod (:88) is one constant and a year → second → year round trip on an odd cent drifts zero (probe §8). financial-ladder.test.mjs is a behavioural gate and would catch a regression in any of that. On the glass (command-ux1.tsx:347-362) 'A · Income $3,200.00 / Income / Wages (take-home) $3,200.00 … Net −$200.00' in red, one unit dropdown that is genuinely full width in portrait (290 of 292 px), the section icon beside the word in the picker's label (:159) and on every row (:354). A couple reads '−$200.00 per 33 days' and '−$0.0042 per minute' and agrees what it is. The signed-out state is honest ('EXAMPLE · the operator's paycheck as data — sign in to record your own') and the record's sentence names no vendor.

WHERE THEY ARGUE — the transaction side. Reconciled and deduplicated, in the order a couple would hit them:

1. The withdrawal row has two meanings and the code sides with the one the words do not. accrual.ts:12-14 says a withdrawal is escrow → the person's own account; :112-120 refuses anything above what has released and cleared the 3-hour hold. command-ux1.tsx:152 defaults the same withdrawal to 'B · Rent / Mortgage', :197 files a ladder field and a timeline on it, and the record's own acceptance line (financial-2525.v.000.json:946) reads 'a withdrawal typed as B · Rent / Mortgage … appears on the record as Rent / Mortgage'. So one partner reads 'the rent is paid' and the other 'the money moved to checking'; the record cannot say which; and :161 offers every section A–M to a withdrawal, Income included. The consequence on the operator's own sheet: with the example paycheck ($3,604.49 over 30.333 d) a $700 rent typed on the deposit's day is refused as 'More than is withdrawable' until day ~5.9, the $1,800 car until day ~15.1 (with the sheet's 3,200 per 33 d: 7.2 and 18.6 days). The brief's purpose — 'track $/min against budget … first with my own finances' — cannot be typed on the day it happens. This is the first defect because it is the one the operator will hit first, and it is still gated at r.013 (1c89639:215).

2. One deposit, two lengths, and no word for either. :145 'MoT · length in days' (30.333) and :151 'Timeline · Every 33 days' sit on one form; the worked example itself carries both (:85). accrual.ts:53 and :75 read only motDays; recurrence is hashed into the entry and read by nothing on the glass; actualsByField (ladder.ts:134) is imported by nothing in frontend/components or frontend/app. FD-26 says the timeline IS the MoT; the probe shows the same paycheck at 8.252 ¢/min by one number and 7.585 by the other. Then the number that does run the clock is printed bare — '$3,604.49 · 30.333' on the roster (:215), the record (:370, :373) and the chart line (:447) — with 'days' nowhere a signed-out stranger or a second partner can see it. r.013 already collapsed the two into one 'Length (MoT)' dropdown that drives motDays (1c89639:207; the r013 capture shows '30.333 days (pay MoT)') — that half is answered and must not be rebuilt. The other half — say the length with its unit, and the timeline word, back on every row — is still owed.

3. Two equal transactions at one stamp keep one, silently. The id is instant + amount (:190, :197); record.ts:35 returns the same record for a repeated id with no refusal; :177 saves it as recorded. A couple on one payroll who each type $3,604.49 at the same 07:00 stamp end with one row and no word (probe §1). I weaken one member the adversarial pass claimed: two 'Record it' taps within one second do not collide, because :191 clears the amount after the first commit and the second tap refuses on amount. The same-stamp member stands, and it is exactly the couple's case. Same id law at r.013 (1c89639:207, :214).

4. The refusal appears under the panel the person did not press. :187-188 set the refusal from the deposit form; the only render is :318 inside the withdrawal panel. A stranger who presses 'Record it' with an empty amount sees nothing where they pressed and a red 'Refused ·' line under the next panel. Confirmed at r.012; answered by r.013's single form (1c89639:332). Record it as answered.

5. A broken chain reads as empty and the next entry buries the old ones for good. record.ts:63-66 returns an empty record on any parse failure, owner mismatch or hash break; :114 adopts it, :177 appends to it, record.ts:70 overwrites the store. Probe §2: three entries, one byte moved, one new deposit, the three are gone from the device. The glass says 'never repaired silently' (lexicon-data.ts:2952) and then does precisely that. This is a words-versus-behaviour defect in the signer's voice, and it loses money on the record; it belongs in this lens.

6. 'In escrow: $0.00' beside a listed $3,604.49 deposit. accrual.ts:95 counts only deposits with atMs <= t, so for the example dated 2026.10.01_07.00 the roster lists the deposit, the rail says 'RELEASE $0.00', the block says 'Released · Deposit $0.00 / In escrow: $0.00 / Withdrawable: $0.00' (:262-266, christo-390-balance.png), and only the chart line (:447) says 'Pending — releases from …'. One partner reads 'the payer put it in escrow — the rail says so'; the other reads 'escrow is empty'. The instance passes at 07:00 CST today; the class — any dated-ahead deposit — does not.

7. Two number formats in one column. :140-141 picks four decimals when |cents| < 100, including zero, so H, I, J, K and M print '$0.0000' per 33 days under '$3,200.00' (r013-ladder.png — the ladder code is unchanged from 201a8ec; the operator's own addendum 31 screenshot notes 'five sections at $0.0000'); per hour '$4.04' sits over '$0.8838'; per second the gym reads '$0.0000' and Net '-$0.0001' (probe §5). A couple reads '$0.0000' as 'a tiny amount we have not seen', not 'nothing here'.

8. Smaller words that will be argued over, all confirmed: 'Monthly (91)' as a timeline option (lexicon-data.ts:3082) beside 'Every 33 days' — the ladder's 'per month (91)' (:134) is the brief's word and stays, but 'Monthly' for 91 days should never be offered to someone entering a paycheck; the withdrawal panel asking for 'Deposit day and time (CST)' (:314, lexicon 2910 — answered in r.013); the note that sends the reader to 'the Balance view' (:363, lexicon 3088) when FIN-06.06 is a draft and no such panel exists at 201a8ec; 'Chain verified' (lexicon 2951) over an FNV-1a chain that record.ts:24 itself calls 'not a signature'; the year label keeping '(A.B..C)' in Clock mode (:288, lexicon 2931); 'Money as time — this MoT' printed twice on one card (:239, :437); the strip's two bare dollar figures (:392) and the heading 'Released · Deposit' (:262) that reads as a row title; the sheet's words re-filed without a note — Insurance → 'D · Auto / Renters / Home', Fitness → 'G · Mental Health / Physical Fitness', Food → 'Groceries' (budget.ts:72-75). I drop the adversarial claim that an old 'Other' entry 'becomes Fun': CATEGORY_FIELD is applied only to the sheet's eight lines (budget.ts:77), the sheet has no Other line, and recorded entries keep their own category on the glass (:181). The map's Other → L row is latent, not a defect anyone can see.

9. Outside the lens's centre but verified and worth the record: the pickers were declared inside the render body (:157) under the 1 Hz clock (:95) — the adversarial reviewer's remount.mjs proves the select's node is replaced and focus dropped within 2.5 s, and the operator reported the same class on his r.011 phone (addendum 26) — fixed in r.014 (84f28f5), still nested at r.013 (1c89639:164); Mars is anchored on Earth's perihelion table (calendar.ts:97-101) so its year line prints 'SOURCED' (:290) one line above 'declared, not sourced' (:292), and :447 omits planet.yearDays while :433 passes it, which the gate at financial-surface.test.mjs:38 does not see; the duplicate-insurance warning is computed (ladder.ts:110) and never rendered; the surface gate is regexes over source text (financial-surface.test.mjs:23-79), the same class that passed r.010's half-width select by the record's own 0.011 note.

ON THE EVIDENCE. The record's scratchpad/shots/ holds no r012 capture (r012-shots.mjs named three and none landed), so the walk reviewer's pictures of the forms were r.013's; the adversarial reviewer built 201a8ec and captured both r.012 panels under fleet-fin/shots/ with a forged login. The two reviewers' probes agree with each other and with the code. At 201a8ec the 90 ladder keys were staged with zero fills (lexicon-coverage.test.mjs:28; fr.ts carries none of them), so the surface read English in 32 languages until 50e57c0; the operator's addendum 30 has since made that the law rather than a defect.

GRADE C-. The model is the lock and its gate is real; the ladder would stand alone at B+. The transaction side is where a couple would argue about every row, three of its defects lose or confuse money on the record without a word (one of two paychecks kept, a broken chain overwritten, a refusal under the wrong panel), and the operator's own first use — record my rent on the day I pay it and watch $/min against budget — is refused by the code while the words promise it. Of the five things r.013 owed from this lens, three have since landed (one refusal slot, the neutral date label, one length) and one more in r.014 (the pickers); what remains is the withdrawal word with its invariant and gate, one id per entry, never overwriting an unverified store, the length and timeline said back with a unit, one format per column, and a glass gate that measures rather than greps. Fix each as a class, not as the row in front of you.
