## Odin

### Odin · walk — grade C+

_The constructive walk — a stranger with the operator's own sheet (Income 3,200 · Home 700 · Car 1,800 · Insurance 200 · Electric 150 · Food 300 · Fitness 50 · Fun 200 per 33 days) opening /financial-2525 on a phone for 60 seconds_

**One sentence:** The A–M flow model is exact, pure and gated, but a stranger's 60 seconds end at a ladder they cannot edit, a "Balance view" they cannot reach, a chart stuck at $0.00 until the example wakes at 07:00 CST, and (behind sign-in) pickers that close every second — and the three things r.013 must build (N–T values, a U entry with somewhere to land, actuals wired from the record with a sign and one length) have rules in ladder.ts but no shapes, so r.013 will add types, not reuse them.

**Findings**

- **The ladder pickers remount on every clock tick — a dropdown closes before the person can pick (blocks the one act r.012 adds)** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:157 declares `const LadderPicker = (...) => (` INSIDE the render body of FinancialCommandUX1; the 1 Hz clock at command-ux1.tsx:95 (`setInterval(() => setNow(Date.now()), 1000)`) re-renders the surface, so React sees a new component type each second and unmounts/remounts the three <select>s at :160, :165, :170 (section, field, timeline) on both forms (:306, :315). The repository's own r.014 record names this exact instance ('r.012's LadderPicker', operator addendum 26 'this drop down goes away quick!'). The r.012 surface gate (tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:51) only regex-checks that the selects exist, not that their DOM node survives a tick. The unit dropdown (:341) and planet select (:279) are inline JSX and do not remount — measured stable in the headless walk.
  - Fix: Lift LadderPicker to module level with a stable identity (pass `t` as a prop), as r.014 later did; add the r.014-style gate that the same DOM node is present three ticks later. Class rule: no component declared inside a render body on any surface driven by a clock.
- **N–T has no value shape — the Balance / Goals view (FIN-06.06, r.013) cannot be built on LadderLine without bending it** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:72-74 give Q (Assets − Debts, Liquid / Home / Invested), R (Score / Utilization / Collections) and S (Will / Beneficiaries / POA, ID / Docs / Umbrella) `kind: "Stock"`; ladder.ts:86 `LadderLine { fieldId; amountNative; nativePeriod }` is the ONLY line type and carries a period, meaningless for a balance at an instant, a credit score or a yes/no document. Q is derived (N + O − P) yet modelled as fields a person enters; T goals (ladder.ts:75) have no target or date. netLadder silently drops any N–T line (probe P4: Net unchanged, warnings []). The record's FIN-06.06 metric says 'the model already carries every rule' — it carries planes and names, not the shapes r.013 renders.
  - Fix: Before the glass: declare `BalanceLine { fieldId; amount; asOfMs }` for N/O/P, `netWorth(balances)` deriving Q (never entered), `StatusLine` for R (score 300–850, utilization %, collections count) and S (checklist), `Goal { fieldId; target; byMs }` for T; make `kind` honest (Status, Goal) and gate it in financial-ladder.test.mjs. One commit, model first, exactly as the brief ordered A–M.
- **No bridge from the record to actuals: TimedTx is a second shape, the sign is missing, and a deposit carries two lengths with no rule which one counts** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: ladder.ts:130 `TimedTx { fieldId?; amount; atMs; days }` vs accrual.ts:27-38 `FinTx { kind; amountCents; atMs; motDays?; field?; recurrence? }` — nothing maps one to the other. actualsByField (ladder.ts:134-146) has no kind, so a withdrawal tagged A.income_wages ADDS to income (probe P3: 3,200 deposit + 50 withdrawal on A → 3,250). The r.012 deposit form records BOTH motDays (command-ux1.tsx:145 default 30.333, :190) and recurrence (:151 default days33, :190); the escrow runs at motDays (0.0825 $/min) while a 33-day recurrence gives a different per-minute (0.0759 over the frame, probe P1) — r.013's actuals column has no stated rule for which length is the transaction's MoT. Both forms offer the same A–M sections (:161 FLOW_SECTIONS on hook 'deposit' and 'withdrawal'), so a deposit on 'B · Rent / Mortgage' is legal and meaningless.
  - Fix: Add `toTimed(tx: FinTx): TimedTx` in ladder.ts (or accrual.ts) with ONE length rule (motDays when present, else recurrenceDays) and a signed amount by kind; restrict deposits to A and withdrawals to B–M on the picker (or say in the model that a refund is a deposit on B); gate: a withdrawal on A never raises income.
- **The plan is a frozen constant — the person cannot enter their own sheet, and the U entry has nowhere to land** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/budget.ts:76-77 `SHEET_LINES` is a constant of eight lines; command-ux1.tsx:138 `netLadder(SHEET_LINES, period)` — no input, no storage, no per-owner plan; FIN-06's statement 'seed data the person edits' has no edit on the glass. ladder.ts:117 `amortize()` returns a fieldId + amount but no type persists an Amortization (FinTx has none, LadderLine has no provenance), so r.013's U entry would have to invent a store. The alcohol target (ladder.ts:83) likewise has no home. The r.012 full-page capture shows the operator's figures under a stranger's eyes with nothing to press to change them.
  - Fix: A per-owner PLAN record beside the transaction record (append-only revisions of `LadderLine[]` plus `Amortization[]` and `alcohol: AlcoholTarget`), loaded like loadRecord; the ladder reads plan lines + amortized lines; the seed is merged, never replacing a saved plan (the seed law).
- **The timeline names mislead the plan-vs-actual comparison the operator wants** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: lexicon fin.rec.month91 reads 'Monthly (91)' and fin.timeline reads 'Timeline' (lexicon-data.ts:3082, :3086) while ladder.ts:123-126 model a one-shot LENGTH with no repetition. A stranger entering rent as 'Monthly (91)' spreads $700 over 91 days: probe P2 shows $253.85 in the 33-day frame against the plan's $700 per 33 days — a 2.8× under-read that is the person's wording, not their money. 'Weekly' / 'Yearly' invite the same reading of repetition.
  - Fix: Rename the control to what it is ('Length the money covers') with the days printed in each option, and have r.013's actuals column compare in the plan line's nativePeriod (toPeriod) so a 91-day length and a 33-day plan meet on one base; if repetition is wanted, model it explicitly (nextAtMs) rather than by name.
- **Rule 5's duplicate-insurance warning is computed and never shown** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: ladder.ts:109-110 push 'DUPLICATE_INSURANCE' into `warnings`; command-ux1.tsx reads `totals.sections` (:354) and `totals.net` (:360) but `warnings` appears 0 times in the file (grep at 201a8ec). The brief: 'If a premium is entered in both, warn'. Today the sheet never trips it, so the gap is invisible until a person adds Housing insurance.
  - Fix: Render `totals.warnings` under the ladder (one lexicon key), and add a surface assert that the glass reads `totals.warnings`.
- **A promise with nothing to press: 'live on the Balance view' — there is no Balance view, link or button** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:363 prints fin.stock_note ('…live on the Balance view — never per minute'); the headless walk found zero pressable elements containing Balance / Goals / stock at 390×844 and 844×390. A stranger scrolls looking for it. The record itself marks FIN-06.06 'draft — r.013'.
  - Fix: Until r.013 lands, word it as a future ('will live on a Balance view') or add a disabled anchor with the revision it arrives in; when it lands, make the note the link.
- **For the whole 60-second walk every money figure is $0.00 — the example deposit has not started** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:83-86 build EXAMPLE from SRC.example.depositStamp = 2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST (the record's example block); the walk at 2026.09.30 18:01 CST reads Released $0.00, 'In escrow: $0.00 · Withdrawable: $0.00', the strip '$0.00 · $0.00', the chart 'Pending — releases from 2026.10.01_07.00..00' with the NOW line absent (MotChart :456 only inside the MoT). The surface's thesis — money ticking by the minute — is invisible to anyone who opens it before Oct 1 07:00 CST, and after Oct 31 15:00 CST it will be fully released and static again.
  - Fix: Anchor the demonstration example relative to `now` (e.g. a deposit that began 3 h 1 min ago at the operator's amount and MoT) so the clock, the hold mark and the chart move on first paint; keep the operator's dated paycheck as the roster/ledger row.
- **Empty sections print '$0.0000' — four decimals read as per-second figures on a per-33-days ladder** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:141 `usdUnit` falls to usd4 for |cents| < 100, so zero prints '$0.0000'; H · Family, I · Debt service, J · Taxes, K · Work, M · Transfers show it at every unit (probe: 5 rows at m33, min and year; visible in ladder-m33-portrait.png).
  - Fix: Print '$0.00' (or '—') for an exact zero regardless of unit; optionally fold empty sections under a 'show all 13' toggle so the phone shows the person's own lines first.
- **Two equal entries in one second collapse into one, silently** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:190 and :197 build ids as `d-${when}-${cents}` / `w-${when}-${cents}`; record.ts:35 `append` returns the same record when the id exists. Probe P7: two $50.00 groceries at the same instant → 1 entry, no refusal, no message.
  - Fix: Include the record's next rev in the id (`w-${rev}-${when}-${cents}`), or refuse with a sentence; the chain stays append-only either way.
- **The MoT length prints as a bare number — '30.333' with no unit on the roster, the chart line and the record** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:215 `${usd(d.amountCents)} · ${d.motDays}`, :447 `· {tx.motDays} ·`, :373 `· ${e.tx.motDays}`; the walk reads '$3,604.49 · 30.333' three times with nothing saying days.
  - Fix: One helper `fmtDays(d)` → '30.333 d' used in all three places.
- **Two years on one surface: the ladder's 'per year' is 365 d while the financial year the chart and year line run on is 365.259636 d** (LOW)
  - Evidence: ladder.ts:27 `year: 365 * DAY` (FD-25, said on the record) vs mot.ts:48 `EARTH_REVOLUTION_DAYS = 365.259636` (calendar.ts positionInYear). Probe P6: plan A per ladder-year $35,393.94, over one revolution $35,419.12. Harmless on the ladder; a trap for r.013 if the actuals window is 'this financial year' from perihelion.
  - Fix: Make r.013's window a ladder period or the current 33-day frame (calendar.ts:121 `frameOf` already exists), never the revolution; say the 0.07 % difference once in the FIN-06.06 statement.

**Keep**

- ladder.ts (146 lines) is pure and exactly the brief: twenty sections A–T in the brief's order, sixty fields with the slash-merged labels verbatim, two planes, the fixed factors (never a 30-day month), toPeriod as one constant — a minute→33-days round trip returns the amount to a billionth; 59 asserts in tests/financial-ladder.test.mjs lock it in test:ci.
- Net = I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr holds at all eight periods on the operator's sheet (−$200 per 33 d, −$0.0042 per minute, −$2,212.12 per year, measured on the r.012 export); transfers are outflow; a balance in P never nets; unknown or negative lines are never counted.
- U is correctly a rule, not a row: amortize() refuses N–T, a U destination and a zero period; no chip, no section, no icon.
- FinTx was extended additively (field and recurrence optional, accrual.ts:36-37) — every r.006–r.011 entry and its chain hash is untouched; loadRecord verifies before trusting and says 'tampered' instead of repairing.
- The glass derives every figure from netLadder/toPeriod; the surface gate (financial-surface.test.mjs:56) forbids typed 86400 / 1440 / 525600 on the surface — the ladder cannot drift from the model by a stray constant.
- actualsByField's overlap arithmetic is right (half window → half of every timed transaction; a 'once' lands whole inside the window) and calendar.ts frameOf already provides the natural actuals window for r.013 — the two pieces exist, they only need joining.
- Measured on the phone: the unit dropdown is 290 of 290 px inner width in portrait and 224 px at its own width in landscape (FD-24 met), no horizontal overflow at 390 px, Net red when negative, every section A–M wears a stroke icon before its word.
- The note under the ladder is honest about N–T ('never per minute'), and the record line is honest about where the data lives ('On this phone only') — no machine word on the person's screen.

**Report (verbatim)**

ODIN · the constructive walk · Financial-2525 v.000_r.012 (commit 201a8ec)

How I looked. I read the eight files under review from the commit itself (git show 201a8ec:…), the ask with addenda 1–24, and the record JSON. The export on disk at review time was already r.015, so I rebuilt the r.012 tree from 201a8ec under scratchpad/fleet-fin/odin/r012 and probed it headless at 390×844 and 844×390 (odin-walk.log, ladder-*.png, chart-*.png, walk-*-full.png). The git-less rebuild logs hydration noise that the real export does not show (the r.015 export and the sibling's 23:13 probe of the original r.012 export both report none), so I attribute nothing to it. I also ran the pure model from the commit (odin-model-probe.log, P1–P9) to ask the only question my lens owns: can r.013 be built on this without rework?

The sixty seconds, as a stranger with the operator's sheet in my pocket.
0–10 s. A dark card: FINANCIAL · 2525, 'the MoT Financial System · Version 000 · revision 0.012'. A five-step rail — DEPOSIT · HOLD · RELEASE · WITHDRAW · RECORD — and an amber YOUR TURN telling me to sign in. I understand this is about a paycheck moving through time. I do not yet know what 'MoT' is; nothing on the first screen says 'Measure of Time'.
10–25 s. 'YOUR DEPOSITS · 1. 2026.10.01_07.00..00 · Income / Wages (take-home) · PAYROLL · $3,604.49 · 30.333'. Released $0.00. In escrow $0.00. Withdrawable $0.00. The chart — 'MONEY AS TIME — THIS MOT · 0 H 0 MIN ELAPSED' — shows a green line rising and an orange line falling with 'Pending — releases from 2026.10.01_07.00..00'. I press the orbit icon: the axis becomes 2646.4257..1234-style numbers and the header reads A-units of the revolution. I press the clock icon and it comes back to '7 d 13 h … 30 d 7 h'. I understand the toggle; I do not understand why everything is zero. The honest reason (the example wakes tomorrow at 07:00 CST) is nowhere on the glass. For my whole minute the surface's thesis — money ticking by the minute — is a promise, not a demonstration.
25–35 s. 'YEAR POSITION — THE ORBIT · Day 271 · Quarter 3 · 89/91 · 33-day frame 9 · 7/33 · Perihelion (Austin CST standard) · 2026.01.03_11.15..00 CST'. I understand quarters of 91 days. 'Frame 9 · 7/33' I half-understand. Fine.
35–50 s. The ladder. This is the part I came for, with my own numbers in hand. 'PERSONAL FINANCE LADDER A–M · Unit · per 33 days'. Thirteen lettered rows with icons; my sheet is already there — A Income 3,200, B Housing 700, C Transportation 1,800, D Insurance 200, E Utilities 150, F Food 300, G Health 50, L Lifestyle 200, Net −$200.00 in red. I switch the unit to per minute: Net −$0.0042. Per year: −$2,212.12. The factors are honest and the switch is instant. Then I look for where to type MY rent (mine is not 700). There is nothing to press. The rows are the operator's sheet, a constant in budget.ts, and no sign-in changes that — the signed-in forms record transactions, not the plan. Five rows read '$0.0000', which on a per-33-days ladder looks like a per-second figure that forgot its unit.
50–60 s. 'Cash, investments, debts, net worth, credit, protection and goals live on the Balance view — never per minute.' I scroll for the Balance view. There is none — no link, no button, no tab. Then THE RECORD · CHAIN VERIFIED, one line, and 'On this phone only'. I would press: Sign in (to find out if I can enter my own sheet — I cannot), the orbit toggle (works), the unit dropdown (works), and the words 'Balance view' (nothing).
Behind sign-in, by code. Two panels, DEPOSIT and WITHDRAWAL, each with Section (A–M), Field (the section's fields) and Timeline (One time · Weekly · Every 33 days · Monthly (91) · Yearly). The deposit panel ALSO has 'MoT · length in days' = 30.333 — so one deposit asks me for two durations and never says which governs the $/min. And the three pickers are built inside the render body (command-ux1.tsx:157) on a surface re-rendered every second (:95), so on a phone the picker sheet closes before I can choose — the repository's own r.014 record confirms this on r.012's LadderPicker. That is the one act r.012 added, and a stranger cannot complete it.

What the brief (addendum 22) promises that I do not see. 'N–T on a second surface (Balance / Goals)' — none. 'U amounts must name a destination field' — amortize() exists, no entry, no store. 'Duplicate insurance … warn' — computed (ladder.ts:110), never rendered. 'Seed data the person edits' (FIN-06) — not editable. The acceptance list the brief gives is met for A–M (13 rows render; Net matches at every base; switching is one constant; U is never a chip; labels exact) — that part is clean.

Is the model ready for r.013 without rework? Partly, and the honest word is 'rules without shapes'.
1. N–T. ladder.ts carries the names and the planes, but the only line type is LadderLine {amount, period}. A cash balance has no period; a credit score is not dollars; S is a checklist; Q is derived from N + O − P and should never be typed; a goal has a target and a date. Q, R and S are labelled kind 'Stock' (ladder.ts:72-74). r.013 will add BalanceLine, StatusLine, Goal and netWorth() — new types, not reuse — and FIN-06.06's 'the model already carries every rule' should be re-stated to say so.
2. U. amortize() returns {fieldId, amount} and refuses N–T. Nothing persists an Amortization, and the plan it would add into is a constant. The U entry needs a per-owner plan record first (lines + amortizations + the alcohol target), merged with the seed under the seed law. That is the real r.013 prerequisite, and it is also what unblocks the stranger's own sheet.
3. Actuals over a window. actualsByField is correct in its arithmetic (P1, P2 agree with hand sums) and frameOf gives the window for free. What is missing is the join: FinTx → TimedTx does not exist; there is no sign (a withdrawal on A adds to income — P3 shows 3,250); a deposit has motDays AND recurrence with no rule which is the length; both forms offer all thirteen sections. One function, `toTimed(tx)`, with a sign by kind and one length rule, plus a picker rule (deposits on A, withdrawals on B–M), makes the column honest. And the names: 'Monthly (91)' on a $700 rent spreads it over 91 days — $253.85 in a 33-day frame against a 700 plan (P2). The control is a length, not a repetition; call it that.
4. Small traps for r.013: ids collide within a second and append() swallows the second entry (P7); the ladder year is 365 d while the revolution is 365.259636 d (P6, $25 on the operator's income) — keep the actuals window a ladder period or the frame.

Grade C+. The flow model (A–M) deserves a B+ on its own: exact to the brief, pure, one-constant conversions, fifty-nine asserts. The surface a stranger meets is a C: an honest ladder with the wrong person's numbers, a chart at zero, a view that is named but not there, and — behind sign-in — pickers that will not stay open. Readiness for r.013 is a C: the right rules are in the right file, and nothing in them must be undone, but every one of the three r.013 items needs a new shape or a new store before it needs a pixel. Build those shapes first, as the brief said: model first, styling second.

Probe artifacts (read-only, under scratchpad/fleet-fin/odin/): odin-walk.log, odin-model-probe.log, ladder-{m33,min,year}-{portrait,landscape}.png, chart-{portrait,landscape}.png, walk-{portrait,landscape}-full.png, r012-build.log.

### Odin · adversarial — grade C+

_Adversarial pass (break the ladder math, the pickers, the timelines, the record, the calendar, the unit dropdown, the lexicon, the gates) on commit 201a8ec, read with git show; headless probes on a scratchpad build of that exact commit (frontend/out in the working tree now carries v.000_r.015, not r.012, so it was not used for measurement)_

**One sentence:** The pure ladder in lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts holds under every period, chain and sign attack I threw at it (worst chained-conversion drift 2.7e-16, Net correct at all eight bases), but the data model carries TWO timelines per deposit that disagree by 8.8 percent on the operator's own worked example, actualsByField is sign-blind and alcohol-blind, the duplicate-insurance rule fires on any household with both auto and home insurance, Mars is anchored to Earth's perihelion while the glass prints SOURCED beside "declared, not sourced", and the pickers remount every second — so r.013's actuals column and Balance view cannot be built on r.012 as shipped without rework in the model, not only in the styling.

**Findings**

- **One deposit, two timelines: motDays (30.333) and recurrence (days33) disagree, and FD-26 says the timeline IS the MoT** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:85 seeds EXAMPLE with motDays: SRC.example.motDays (30.333) AND recurrence: "days33"; :145 defaults dMot to the pay MoT while :151 defaults dRec to "days33"; :190 commits BOTH onto the entry. accrual.ts:75 releases at amountCents / (motDays × 1440) while ladder.ts:126-129 spreads the same deposit over recurrenceDays. Measured on the worked paycheck (probe.mjs 5): accrual 8.2520 ¢/min vs ladder 7.5852 ¢/min, ratio 1.0879. A deposit typed with MoT 0 and timeline Yearly releases whole at once (accrual.ts:59) while the ladder spreads it over 365 d (probe 5b). The record's own decision FD-26 (docs/financial-2525/financial-2525.v.000.json) states 'the timeline is the transaction's MoT: its $/min runs from its date for that length' — the code keeps two.
  - Fix: One length per transaction: derive motDays from the timeline (recurrenceDays) or make the timeline the only length control, with 'Other' typing a number; assert in financial-accrual/financial-ladder that depositView(tx).ratePerMinCents equals ratePerMinute(tx.amountCents, recurrenceDays(tx.recurrence)) for every recorded deposit. r.013's actuals column is wrong by the ratio above until this is one number.
- **actualsByField is sign-blind: a withdrawal on an income field adds to income; r.013 cannot sum the record on it as is** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:130 TimedTx has amount but no kind/sign; :140-143 add every tx's overlap. Probe 6a: a 3,200 deposit (33 d) plus a 500 withdrawal on A.income_wages over the 33-day window returns A.income_wages amount 3700. The record's FinTx (accrual.ts:27-38) carries kind but the bridge FinTx → TimedTx does not exist anywhere in r.012; the ladder's Net also drops negatives (:104), so the two halves cannot meet.
  - Fix: Define the bridge once: a deposit is +amount on its field, a withdrawal −amount (or a deposit on A only; a withdrawal on B–M only), and gate it; give TimedTx a sign or kind; add the alcohol target to actualsByField (probe 6c shows it keys on the raw 'F.alcohol' id, so an alcohol→L choice would split one line across two sections).
- **Duplicate-insurance warning fires on any household with auto insurance in D and housing insurance in B (false positive by construction)** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:110 warns whenever both B.housing_insurance and D.auto_renters_home are seen; D.auto_renters_home is the merged 'Auto / Renters / Home' field (:56), so the model cannot tell an auto premium from a home premium. Probe 3: housing 100 in B + auto 150 in D → ["DUPLICATE_INSURANCE"]. Rule 5 in the brief (docs/asks/…addendum 22) warns on 'the same premium' entered in both; the gate tests/financial-ladder.test.mjs:49-51 only tests the true-positive, never the ordinary household.
  - Fix: The warning needs the detail sheet's components (which premium D holds) — until r.013 ships the detail sheet, either narrow the trigger to a component tagged 'home' or word the warning as a question ('is the housing premium also inside D?'), and add the false-positive case to the gate.
- **Mars on the perihelion anchor is anchored to EARTH's perihelion table; the glass prints SOURCED beside 'declared, not sourced'** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/calendar.ts:97-101 use perihelionOf(g) (Earth's PERIHELION table, :34-47) for every yearDays, so Mars (686.98) resets at Earth's perihelion every ~364 d and never reaches 3600 A: probe 8a/8b — 33 min before Earth's 2027 perihelion Mars reads 1909.1402..2631, 27 min after it reads 0.0353..2600; status is SOURCED (probe2: 'SOURCED 2026 271'). Headless on the r.012 build, planet = mars, 390×844 and 844×390: the year line prints '2026 · SOURCED · 271/365' (Earth's day/length) and under it fin.anchor_note 'no perihelion table for this planet yet — declared, not sourced' (shots/odin-r012-mars-year-portrait.png). command-ux1.tsx:290 prints year.status and `${year.day}/${Math.ceil(year.lengthDays)}` for every planet; :292 prints the note for non-Earth.
  - Fix: positionInYear must take the planet's own epoch (a Mars perihelion table or a DECLARED Mars epoch), return status DECLARED for Mars, and the glass must not print Earth's day/365 for Mars. Gate: for planet mars, year.status === 'DECLARED' and lengthDays ≈ 686.98.
- **The three ladder pickers remount every second: LadderPicker is a component created inside the render function and fed by the 1 Hz clock** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:157 declares `const LadderPicker = (…) => (…)` inside FinancialCommandUX1, and :306/:315 mount it as `<LadderPicker …/>` — a new component type on every render; :95 re-renders the whole card every 1000 ms. React unmounts and remounts the three selects each tick, so an open dropdown closes. The surface gate tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:51 asserts `<LadderPicker[^>]*hook="deposit"` and so LOCKS the broken pattern in. Confirmed by the operator's later addendum 26 ('this drop down goes away quick!') and the r.014 correction 84f28f5 ('every picker a module-level component; the $/min clock never remounts a select'). Not measured headlessly here because the forms render only behind Auth0 (:297).
  - Fix: Hoist every picker to module level (done in r.014); replace the regex in the surface gate with a probe that opens a select, waits 2 s, and asserts it is still the same DOM node (isSameNode).
- **A second transaction with the same stamp and amount is dropped silently; the form clears as if it had been recorded** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:190 builds the id as `d-${when}-${cents}` and :197 `w-${when}-${cents}`; record.ts:35 returns the same record when the id exists. Probe 7b: append of a second deposit with id d-1-100 → entries 2 → 2, sameObject true. :177 commit then saveRecord()s the unchanged record and :191 clears the amount — no refusal, no row. Two paychecks of equal amount typed with the same stamp (two jobs, a typed date without seconds) lose one.
  - Fix: Derive the id from rev + a nonce (or include the memo/field), or have commit() refuse with a visible reason when append() returns the same record.
- **A tampered record is overwritten by the next entry — the evidence is destroyed, not kept** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/record.ts:65 returns emptyRecord() when verify() fails; command-ux1.tsx:114 stores that empty record, :177 appends to it and saveRecord()s under the same key (record.ts:70), replacing the stored chain. The banner (:368, fin.chain_broken 'shown empty, never repaired silently') is true until the first new deposit, after which the broken chain is gone and the banner reads 'Chain verified'. Also, record.ts:25-29 is FNV-1a with no secret: probe 7a edits entry 1's amount, recomputes both hashes, and verify() returns ok — the glass's 'CHAIN VERIFIED' (:368) claims more than the mechanism gives.
  - Fix: On tamper, keep the stored bytes under a quarantine key before any write; word the label 'chain intact' rather than 'verified'; r.013's cloud half should sign, not hash.
- **The 90 new keys are staged, so every language but English reads a half-translated ladder** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: At 201a8ec no store under frontend/lib/i18n-app carries fin.sec.* / fin.field.* / fin.rec.* (git grep returns none); tests/lexicon-coverage.test.mjs:28 lists them in AFTER_FILL. Headless on the r.012 build with locale es: 'Unidad' and 'por 33 días' are Spanish while the title 'PERSONAL FINANCE LADDER A–M', every section ('A · Income', 'H · Family'), every field ('Income / Wages (take-home)') and the N–T note stay English (shots/odin-r012-ladder-es.png). Filled later in 50e57c0, i.e. r.012 as shipped was English-only on the ladder.
  - Fix: Ship staged keys and their fill in the same commit, or hold the surface change until the fill lands; the coverage gate should fail a surface that renders a staged key.
- **Empty sections print '$0.0000' beside '$3,200.00' — two precisions in one column, the zero rows look like per-second figures** (LOW)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:141 usdUnit prints usd4 whenever |cents| < 100, so a zero prints four decimals. Measured headless at per 33 days: 'H · Family $0.0000', 'A · Income $3,200.00'; at per hour the column reads $4.04 / $0.8838 / $2.27 / $0.2525 (shots/odin-r012-ladder-portrait.png; also visible in shots/r013-ladder.png).
  - Fix: Pick the column's precision from the unit (2 dp for day and up, 4 dp for hour and below), never per cell; print an empty section as '—'.
- **The Earth 'offline Dec 31' claim is not what the perihelion anchor does: the DOWN day is Jan 2–3** (LOW)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/calendar.ts:13 and :67 say Dec 31 stays the offline day; :104-105 compute day and down from the perihelion instant. Probe 8c: 2026-12-31 is day 362, down false; 2027-01-02 20:00Z is down true; the 2026 perihelion year is 364.39 d, so day 365 lasts ~9 h and 3600 A is never reached (ends at 3591.0642..0325). The Earth LTU row carries offlineDay '12-31' (lib/planet-ltu.ts:50). Stale doc comments say the year is 'exactly 365 days' (mot.ts:44, :106; calendar.ts:8) against :48-49.
  - Fix: Decide one law (offline = day 365 from the anchor, or the calendar date) and make the Earth row and the comments say it; gate it.
- **amortize accepts a negative or NaN amount and refuses the brief's own example id** (LOW)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:117-121 check only the field and periodDays: probe 4b (amount −1200) returns −108.49 into B.property_tax_hoa_fees; 4c (NaN) returns amount null. The brief's rule 7 example mapsTo 'B.property_tax' (docs/asks addendum 22) is refused NOT_A_FLOW_FIELD (probe 4a) because the field id is B.property_tax_hoa_fees. r.013's U entry will inherit both.
  - Fix: Refuse !(amount > 0) and !isFinite; keep a small alias map (brief slug → field id) or validate the U entry's destination from the field dropdown only.
- **The surface gates pass on regexes over source text, not behaviour; 'full width' is a className match** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:54 asserts the unit select's className contains 'w-full … landscape:w-auto'; :51 asserts the JSX of the pickers; :87 counts a pass when out/ does not exist. No gate opens a dropdown, measures a width, or renders a non-English locale. Measured here instead: portrait 390×844 the unit select is 290 of 292 px panel width (full), landscape 844×390 it is 224 of 670 px (own width) — the law holds, but the gate would also pass if a later edit broke the layout behind the same class string. The planet select is 71 px in portrait (command-ux1.tsx:279), outside FD-24 by its own wording.
  - Fix: Add one headless probe to test:ci for the financial surface (serve out/, 390×844 and 844×390, measure the three pickers and the unit select, open a select and hold 2 s, read the ladder in a second locale).
- **Console error on every load: <svg height="auto">** (LOW)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:448 sets height="auto" on the chart svg; headless console on the r.012 build: 'Error: <svg> attribute height: Expected length, "auto"' in both orientations.
  - Fix: Drop the height attribute (the viewBox and width=100% size it) or set it in CSS.

**Keep**

- ladder.ts is pure and the lock's math holds: PERIOD_SECONDS are the brief's fixed factors (:27), toPeriod is one constant (:88) — worst relative drift across every a→b→c chain of the eight periods measured 2.7e-16; Net = I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr (:108-111) matches at all eight bases and the gate tests/financial-ladder.test.mjs:35 proves it per period rather than by regex.
- The sixty fields and twenty sections are exactly the brief's labels and counts (ladder.ts:31-76), U is a rule not a section (:117), P never nets against I, negatives and unknown fields are never counted (:104) — all gated behaviourally.
- Old entries are untouched: an r.006–r.011 entry with a category and no field hashes identically with field/recurrence undefined (probe 7c true; record.ts:22-23 stable JSON), and the roster/record still print the category (command-ux1.tsx:180-181, :215).
- The record's own honesty is good where it exists: FIN-06.06 is marked draft with the r.013 scope named, FD-25 says on the record that the ladder's 365-day year differs from the MoT's 365.259636-day revolution, and the ask file's sha256 at 201a8ec matches handoffSha256 (a465d3bd…).
- The unit dropdown meets FD-24 by measurement, not only by class: 290/292 px in portrait, 224/670 px in landscape, label above in portrait, beside in landscape.

**Report (verbatim)**

## Odin · adversarial pass on Financial-2525 v.000_r.012 (commit 201a8ec)

**Method.** Every file read with `git show 201a8ec:<path>`; the working tree's `frontend/out` carries r.015, so I extracted the r.012 tree with `git archive` into the scratchpad (`fleet-fin/odin/r012`), built it (`next build`, exit 0, export stamped v.000_r.012), served it on a free port and drove Chromium 1194 at 390×844 and 844×390 (`fleet-fin/odin/probe3.mjs`, `probe4.mjs`; captures in `fleet-fin/odin/shots/`). Pure-model attacks ran with node against the r.012 libraries (`fleet-fin/odin/probe.mjs`). No repository file was touched. No r.012 capture exists in `scratchpad/shots/` (r011 then r013), so the r.012 ship's "captures" claim was not verifiable from the record.

### What held
- **Ladder math.** `toPeriod` (ladder.ts:88) is one constant; the worst chained drift over all 512 period triples is 2.7e-16. Net is correct at every base from the sheet lines (per 33 d −200.00; per minute −0.0042; per year −2,212.12). Negatives, unknown fields and the P balance are never counted (:104, gate :41-42). Transfers subtract once (:108-111). The sixty labels match the brief.
- **Record compatibility.** An old category-only entry hashes the same with `field`/`recurrence` undefined (probe 7c); the glass still prints the category.
- **Unit dropdown.** Measured, not read: 290 of 292 px in portrait, 224 of 670 px in landscape.

### What broke (file:line in the defect list)
1. **Two timelines per deposit** — `motDays` 30.333 and `recurrence` days33 both ride every deposit (command-ux1.tsx:85, :145, :151, :190); the accrual uses one (accrual.ts:75), the ladder the other (ladder.ts:126-129). On the operator's own paycheck the two $/min differ by 8.8 %. FD-26 on the record says the timeline *is* the MoT. Until there is one length, r.013's actuals column is a second number for the same money.
2. **actualsByField is sign-blind and alcohol-blind** (ladder.ts:130-143): a withdrawal on A.income_wages adds to income (probe 6a); the alcohol target is ignored (6c). The record→window bridge does not exist. r.013 needs a kind→sign law before an actuals column can be honest.
3. **Duplicate-insurance false positive** (ladder.ts:110): any household with auto insurance in D and housing insurance in B is warned forever, because D.auto_renters_home is a merged field. The gate tests only the true positive.
4. **Mars anchored to Earth's perihelion** (calendar.ts:97-101): Mars resets at ~1909 A every Earth year and never reaches 3600; the glass prints `2026 · SOURCED · 271/365` for Mars above a note saying "declared, not sourced" (captured).
5. **Pickers remount every second** — `LadderPicker` is declared inside the render function (command-ux1.tsx:157) and mounted as JSX (:306, :315) under a 1 Hz `setNow` (:95). The surface gate (:51) locks the pattern in by regex. The operator reported it minutes later (addendum 26) and r.014 fixed it; r.012 shipped with it.
6. **Silent drop on id collision** (command-ux1.tsx:190/:197 + record.ts:35) — same stamp and amount → the second entry vanishes and the form clears as if recorded.
7. **Tampered record overwritten** — loadRecord returns empty (record.ts:65), the next commit saves over the stored chain (:70). And the chain is FNV-1a without a secret: edit-and-rehash verifies (probe 7a). "CHAIN VERIFIED" overclaims.
8. **Lexicon**: the 90 ladder keys are staged (lexicon-coverage.test.mjs:28); in Spanish the panel reads "Unidad · por 33 días" over an all-English ladder (captured). Filled only in the later commit 50e57c0.
9. **Precision mixing**: empty sections print `$0.0000` beside `$3,200.00` (command-ux1.tsx:141).
10. **Dec 31 offline vs day-365 DOWN from the perihelion**: the code's down day is Jan 2–3 (probe 8c); comments and the Earth row say Dec 31; mot.ts:44/:106 still say "exactly 365 days".
11. **amortize** takes negative/NaN amounts and refuses the brief's literal `B.property_tax` (probe 4a-4c).
12. **Gates** for the surface are source regexes; nothing in test:ci opens a select, measures a width, or renders a second locale. `<svg height="auto">` throws a console error on every load (:448).

### Readiness for r.013 (the lens)
The N–T Balance view can be built on `SECTIONS`/`FIELDS` as they are — planes and kinds are right. The **U entry** needs the amount guard and an alias (or dropdown-only destination). The **actuals column** cannot be built without rework in the model: one length per transaction (defect 1) and a sign law (defect 2) are prerequisites, and both live in `lib/`, not in styling — which is the part the lock said to get right first.

**Grade C+.** The pure ladder alone would be A−; the glass and the double timeline pull the revision down, and the one defect the operator could feel on his phone within minutes (the closing dropdown) shipped behind a gate that asserted its presence.

### Odin · synthesis — grade C+

**One sentence:** The A–M ladder is exact, pure and gated, but r.012 carries two lengths per deposit and no sign between the record and the ladder, no value shape for N–T, no store for a plan or a U entry, and pickers that remount every second — so each of r.013's three items needs a new shape in lib/ before a pixel, which is the rework the lock said to avoid.

**Top three**

- No bridge from the record to actuals — one deposit carries TWO lengths that disagree by 8.8 % on the operator's own paycheck, and actualsByField has no sign. frontend/lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:130 `TimedTx { fieldId?; amount; atMs; days }` vs frontend/lib/financial-2525/accrual.ts:27-38 `FinTx { kind; amountCents; atMs; motDays?; field?; recurrence? }` — nothing maps one to the other. command-ux1.tsx:85 seeds the example with motDays 30.333 AND recurrence "days33"; :145 defaults dMot to the pay MoT, :151 defaults dRec to days33, :190 commits both; accrual.ts:75 releases at amount/(motDays×1440) = 8.252 ¢/min while ladder.ts:126-129 spreads the same deposit over 33 d = 7.585 ¢/min (ratio 1.0879). FD-26 on the record says 'the timeline is the transaction's MoT' — the code keeps two. ladder.ts:138-143 adds every transaction's overlap with no kind: a 3,200 deposit + 50 withdrawal on A.income_wages → 3,250 (probe P3); :138 keys on the raw 'F.alcohol' id, ignoring alcoholSection. Both forms offer all thirteen sections (:161), so a deposit on B is legal and meaningless. FIX: one `toTimed(tx: FinTx): TimedTx` in ladder.ts with ONE length rule (derive the length from the timeline per FD-26, or make the length the only control) and a signed amount by kind (deposit +, withdrawal −), alcohol target applied; restrict deposits to A and withdrawals to B–M on the picker; gates: a withdrawal on A never raises income; depositView(tx).ratePerMinCents === ratePerMinute(amount, recurrenceDays(tx.recurrence)) for every recorded deposit.
- N–T has no value shape — the Balance / Goals view (FIN-06.06, r.013) cannot be built on LadderLine without bending it. frontend/lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:72-74 give Q (Assets − Debts), R (Score / Utilization / Collections) and S (Will / POA, ID / Docs) `kind: "Stock"`; :75 T goals have no target or date; :86 `LadderLine { fieldId; amountNative; nativePeriod }` is the only line type and carries a period, meaningless for a balance at an instant, a credit score or a yes/no document; Q is derived (N + O − P) yet modelled as fields a person enters; netLadder (:104) silently drops any N–T line with no warning (probe P4). The record's FIN-06.06 metric (docs/financial-2525/financial-2525.v.000.json:963, 'the model already carries every rule') is true of planes and names, not of the shapes r.013 renders. FIX, model first as the brief ordered A–M: `BalanceLine { fieldId; amount; asOfMs }` for N/O/P, `netWorth(balances)` deriving Q (never entered), `StatusLine` for R (score, utilization, collections) and S (checklist), `Goal { fieldId; target; byMs }` for T; make FieldKind honest (Status, Goal); gate in tests/financial-ladder.test.mjs; re-word FIN-06.06's metric to say the shapes are owed.
- The three ladder pickers remount on every clock tick — a dropdown closes before the person can pick, and the gate locks the broken pattern in. frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:157 declares `const LadderPicker = (…) => (` INSIDE FinancialCommandUX1's render body; :306 and :315 mount it as JSX; the 1 Hz clock at :95 (`setInterval(() => setNow(Date.now()), 1000)`) re-renders the surface, so React sees a new component type each second and unmounts/remounts the section, field and timeline <select>s (:160, :165, :170). tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:51 asserts `<LadderPicker[^>]*hook="deposit"` by regex — it passes on the broken pattern. The repository's own r.014 record (financial-2525.v.000.json:241) names 'CategorySelect, then LadderPicker' as the class and the operator's addendum 26 ('this drop down goes away quick!') as the report; the unit (:341) and planet (:279) selects are inline and did not remount in the headless walk. FIX: hoist every picker to module level with a stable identity (pass t as a prop), and replace the regex with a probe that opens a select, waits three ticks and asserts the same DOM node (isSameNode) — class rule: no component declared inside a render body on any clock-driven surface.

**Owed next**

- toTimed(tx: FinTx): TimedTx in lib/financial-2525 — one length per transaction (FD-26: the timeline IS the MoT; derive motDays from recurrenceDays or make the length the only control, with Other for a typed number), a signed amount by kind, the alcohol target applied; gate: a withdrawal on A never raises income; depositView rate equals ratePerMinute(amount, recurrenceDays) for every recorded deposit.
- Picker rule on the forms: deposits land on A only, withdrawals on B–M only (or the model says in words that a refund is a deposit on B); gate it.
- Shapes for N–T before any Balance / Goals pixel: BalanceLine { fieldId; amount; asOfMs } for N/O/P, netWorth() deriving Q (never entered), StatusLine for R and S, Goal { fieldId; target; byMs } for T; FieldKind made honest (Status, Goal); gated in financial-ladder; FIN-06.06's metric re-worded to say the shapes were owed.
- A per-owner PLAN record beside the transaction record — append-only revisions of LadderLine[] plus Amortization[] and alcohol: AlcoholTarget — loaded like loadRecord, the seed merged never replacing a saved plan (the seed law); the ladder reads plan lines + amortized lines; the U entry lands there. amortize() refuses !(amount > 0) and non-finite amounts (ladder.ts:119 checks only the period); the destination comes from the field dropdown only (the brief's literal 'B.property_tax' is not a field id).
- Every picker a module-level component and a DOM-node-survives-three-ticks probe in test:ci (landed as r.014 — r.013 as shipped still carried it; say so on the record).
- Render totals.warnings under the ladder (ladder.ts:109-110 computes DUPLICATE_INSURANCE; command-ux1.tsx never reads it) and narrow or re-word the trigger: D.auto_renters_home is a merged field (ladder.ts:56), so any household with auto insurance in D and housing insurance in B warns forever; add the ordinary-household case to the gate (financial-ladder.test.mjs:49-51 tests only the true positive).
- The actuals window is the 33-day frame (calendar.ts:121 frameOf) or a ladder period — never the revolution: ladder.ts:27 year = 365 d while mot.ts:48 = 365.259636 d ($25 on the operator's income per year, probe P6); compare actuals in the plan line's nativePeriod (toPeriod) so a 91-day length and a 33-day plan meet on one base.
- Rename the timeline control to what it is — a length the money covers, with the days printed in each option ('Monthly (91)', lexicon-data.ts:3082, spreads a $700 rent over 91 d → $253.85 in a 33-day frame against a $700 plan, probe P2); if repetition is wanted, model it explicitly (nextAtMs), never by name.
- Record hygiene: ids carry the next rev (`w-${rev}-${when}-${cents}` — command-ux1.tsx:190/:197 + record.ts:35 silently swallow the second of two equal entries in one second and the form clears as if recorded); a tampered store is copied to a quarantine key before any write (record.ts:65 returns empty, :70 then overwrites the evidence on the next commit); the label reads 'Chain intact', not 'verified' (record.ts:24-29 is FNV-1a with no secret — edit-and-rehash passes verify).
- Surface honesty until the Balance view lands: word fin.stock_note as a future ('will live on a Balance view') or give it a disabled anchor with the revision — today it names a view with nothing to press (walk: zero pressable Balance/Goals elements at 390×844 and 844×390).
- Small, same commit: print '$0.00' (or '—') for an exact zero regardless of unit and pick the column's precision from the unit (command-ux1.tsx:141 prints $0.0000 beside $3,200.00 on five empty sections); one fmtDays helper ('30.333 d') at :215, :373, :447; drop height="auto" on the chart svg (:448); anchor the demonstration example relative to now so the clock, hold mark and chart move on first paint (the dated paycheck stays the ledger row).
- Outside r.013's scope but a trap for any 'this year' window on another planet: calendar.ts:97-101 anchors every planet to Earth's perihelion table, so Mars resets at ~1909 A every Earth year and the glass prints '2026 · SOURCED · 271/365' above 'declared, not sourced' (captured); positionInYear needs the planet's own epoch and a DECLARED status for Mars. Also one law for Earth's offline day: calendar.ts:13/:67 and planet-ltu.ts:50 say Dec 31, :104-105 compute day 365 from the perihelion (Jan 2–3).

**Report (verbatim)**

ODIN · SYNTHESIS · Financial-2525 v.000_r.012 (commit 201a8ec)

HOW I RECONCILED. Two Odin reviewers reported, both at C+. I re-read every file they cite from the commit itself (git show 201a8ec:<path>), the ask with its addenda, the record JSON, the three gates, and I inspected their probe artefacts and captures under scratchpad/fleet-fin/odin/ (odin-model-probe.log P1–P9, odin-walk.log, ladder-*.png, walk-*-full.png, shots/odin-r012-*.png). Two facts frame the whole review: frontend/out on disk carries v.000_r.018, so neither reviewer could read the r.012 export from the tree — both rebuilt it from the commit; and scratchpad/shots/ holds r011-*.png and r013-*.png but no r012-*.png, so the r.012 ship's own captures are not on the record. I recomputed the load-bearing numbers by hand (3,604.49 ÷ (30.333 × 1440) = 8.252 ¢/min against 3,604.49 ÷ (33 × 1440) = 7.585 ¢/min, ratio 1.0879; 700 × 33/91 = 253.85; 3,200 × 365/33 = 35,393.94 against × 365.259636/33 = 35,419.12) and they hold.

WHAT I DROPPED OR CORRECTED. (1) The adversarial's claim that the 71 px planet select is 'outside FD-24 by its own wording' does not hold: FD-24 (record :1113-1115) says the full-width rule 'reaches the category and planet pickers when the operator asks' — the planet picker is explicitly deferred. Dropped. (2) The adversarial's timing on the tampered record is slightly off: after the first new deposit the banner still reads 'failed its chain' until the next page load, because `tampered` (command-ux1.tsx:112-114) is set only in the load effect; the evidence is nonetheless overwritten at that first commit (record.ts:65 → :70). Kept, re-stated precisely. (3) The adversarial's remedy for the staged lexicon ('ship the fill in the same commit') is now contrary to the operator's later law (addenda 30/32: no translation until the English is final). The defect stands for r.012 as shipped — Spanish reads 'Unidad · por 33 días · Neto' over an all-English ladder (captured) — but the fix is to keep the surface English-only until he says final, not to fill. (4) The walk attributed React #425/#418/#423 on its rebuilt tree to a git-less build; I cannot confirm or deny that from the r.018 export, so I attribute nothing to it either. Everything else in both reports I verified at the cited line.

THE THREE DEFECTS THAT MATTER FOR MY LENS.

1. No bridge from the record to actuals; two lengths; no sign. ladder.ts:130 TimedTx and accrual.ts:27-38 FinTx are two shapes with no function between them. Every deposit carries BOTH motDays (command-ux1.tsx:85, :145 default 30.333, :190) and recurrence (:85, :151 default days33, :190); the escrow runs on motDays (accrual.ts:75) and the ladder's actuals on recurrenceDays (ladder.ts:126-129) — 8.8 % apart on the operator's own paycheck. FD-26 on the record says the timeline IS the transaction's MoT; the code keeps two, and a deposit typed with MoT 0 and timeline Yearly releases whole at once (accrual.ts:59) while the ladder spreads it over 365 d. actualsByField (ladder.ts:134-146) has no kind: a withdrawal on A.income_wages adds to income (3,200 + 50 → 3,250, probe P3); it keys on the raw 'F.alcohol' id (:138) so an alcohol→L choice splits one line across two sections; and both forms offer all thirteen sections (:161) so a deposit on 'B · Rent / Mortgage' is legal and meaningless. This is rework in the model, not in styling: r.013's actuals column is a second number for the same money until there is one length and one sign. Fix: one toTimed(tx) with one length rule and a signed amount by kind, alcohol applied; deposits on A / withdrawals on B–M at the picker; two gates (above).

2. N–T has no value shape. ladder.ts:72-74 label Q, R and S as kind 'Stock'; :75 gives T no target or date; :86 LadderLine is the only line type and carries a period — meaningless for a balance at an instant, a credit score or a will. Q is derived (N + O − P) yet modelled as two enterable fields. netLadder (:104) drops any N–T line in silence (probe P4: Net unchanged, warnings []). The record's FIN-06.06 metric (:963) says 'the model already carries every rule' — it carries planes and names; the shapes r.013 must render (BalanceLine, StatusLine, Goal, netWorth) do not exist, so r.013 will add types, not reuse them. The SECTIONS/FIELDS tables themselves are right and can be kept.

3. The pickers remount every second. LadderPicker is declared inside the render body (command-ux1.tsx:157) and mounted at :306/:315 under the 1 Hz clock at :95; React treats it as a new component type each render, so the three selects (:160, :165, :170) are unmounted and remounted each tick and a phone's picker sheet is dismissed with its element. The surface gate (tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:51) asserts the JSX by regex and so passes on the defect. The repository's r.014 record (:241) names the class ('CategorySelect, then LadderPicker') and the operator's addendum 26 the symptom; r.014 (84f28f5) fixed it and added the same-DOM-node probe. For r.012 it means the one act the revision added — placing a transaction on the ladder — could not be completed on a phone.

THE REST, DEDUPLICATED AND RANKED.
4. The plan is a frozen constant and the U entry has nowhere to land (MEDIUM, the real r.013 prerequisite): budget.ts:76-77 SHEET_LINES is eight constant lines; command-ux1.tsx:138 netLadder(SHEET_LINES, period) — no input, no storage, no per-owner plan, so a stranger sees the operator's figures with nothing to press; ladder.ts:116-121 amortize() returns a number but no type persists an Amortization and the alcohol target (:83) has no home; amortize accepts a negative or NaN amount (:119 checks only the period) and refuses the brief's literal example slug 'B.property_tax' (ask :254) because the id is B.property_tax_hoa_fees. Fix: a per-owner plan record (lines + amortizations + alcohol), append-only, seed merged under the seed law; amount guard; destination from the dropdown only.
5. Rule 5 is computed, never shown, and would false-positive when shown (MEDIUM): ladder.ts:109-110 pushes DUPLICATE_INSURANCE; command-ux1.tsx reads totals.sections (:354) and totals.net (:360) and never totals.warnings (grep: 0). When rendered, D.auto_renters_home is a merged Auto / Renters / Home field (:56), so any household with an auto premium in D and a housing premium in B warns forever; the gate (financial-ladder.test.mjs:49-51) tests only the true positive.
6. The timeline names mislead the plan-vs-actual comparison (MEDIUM): 'Monthly (91)' and 'Timeline' (lexicon-data.ts:3082, :3086) over a model that is a one-shot LENGTH (ladder.ts:123-126); rent entered as Monthly (91) reads $253.85 in a 33-day frame against a $700 plan (P2). 'Weekly' / 'Yearly' invite the same reading.
7. A promise with nothing to press (MEDIUM): fin.stock_note (command-ux1.tsx:363) names 'the Balance view'; the walk found zero pressable Balance/Goals elements in both orientations.
8. The whole 60-second walk is $0.00 (MEDIUM): EXAMPLE (command-ux1.tsx:83-86) starts 2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST; the walk at 2026.09.30 18:01 CST read Released $0.00, escrow $0.00, strip '$0.00 · $0.00', chart 'Pending — releases from …'. The surface's thesis is invisible before Oct 1 07:00 and static again after Oct 31.
9. A tampered record is overwritten and 'CHAIN VERIFIED' overclaims (MEDIUM): record.ts:65 returns empty on a failed verify, :70 then saves over the stored chain at the next commit; the hash is FNV-1a with no secret (:24-29) so an edit-and-rehash verifies.
10. Lexicon: the 90 ladder keys are staged (lexicon-coverage.test.mjs:28; no fin.sec.* in lib/i18n-app at 201a8ec); every language but English reads a half-translated ladder (captured in es). Remedy now governed by addenda 30/32: hold English-only until final.
11. Mars anchored to Earth's perihelion (MEDIUM, outside my lens but real and captured): calendar.ts:97-101 use perihelionOf(g) for every planet, so Mars (686.98 d) resets at ~1909 A every Earth year and the glass prints '2026 · SOURCED · 271/365' above 'declared, not sourced' (command-ux1.tsx:290, :292).
12. Gates are source regexes (MEDIUM): financial-surface.test.mjs:51/:54 assert JSX and class strings; :87 counts a pass when out/ is absent; nothing in test:ci opens a select, measures a width or renders a second locale.
13. LOW, all verified: $0.0000 on five empty sections (command-ux1.tsx:141 usd4 for |cents| < 100 — seen in ladder-m33-portrait.png); two equal entries in one second collapse silently (ids :190/:197 + record.ts:35; the form clears at :191/:200); the MoT prints as a bare '30.333' at :215, :373, :447; two years on one surface (ladder.ts:27 365 d vs mot.ts:48 365.259636 d — FD-25 says so on the record, harmless on the ladder, a trap for a 'this year' actuals window); Dec 31 vs day 365 from the perihelion (calendar.ts:13/:67 and planet-ltu.ts:50 vs :104-105; mot.ts:44/:106 still say 'exactly 365 days'); <svg height=\"auto\"> at :448. I also note, from the full-page capture, the phone strip ('$0.00 · $0.00 | DEPOSIT') painted over the ladder mid-page — the operator's addendum 29, fixed in r.017; not my lens.

WHAT HELD. ladder.ts is pure and is the brief: twenty sections A–T in order, sixty fields with the slash-merged labels verbatim, two planes, the fixed factors (never a 30-day month), toPeriod as one constant (worst chained drift over all period triples 2.7e-16; a minute→33-days round trip returns to a billionth). Net = I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr holds at all eight bases on the operator's sheet (−$200 per 33 d, −$0.0042 per minute, −$2,212.12 per year, read on the glass); transfers subtract once; a balance in P never nets; negatives and unknown fields are never counted — and the gate (financial-ladder.test.mjs:35) proves it per period, not by regex. U is a rule, not a row: amortize refuses N–T, a U destination and a zero period; no chip, no section, no icon. FinTx was extended additively (accrual.ts:36-37): an r.006–r.011 entry hashes identically with field/recurrence undefined, and the record still prints its category (command-ux1.tsx:180-181). The glass derives every figure from netLadder/toPeriod and the surface gate (:56) forbids a typed 86400/1440/525600. actualsByField's overlap arithmetic is right (half a window → half of every timed transaction; a 'once' lands whole), and calendar.ts:121 frameOf already gives r.013 its natural window — the pieces exist, they only need joining. Measured, not read: the unit dropdown is 290/290 px in portrait and 224/668 px in landscape (FD-24 met), no horizontal overflow at 390 px, Net red when negative, every section A–M wears a stroke icon. The record is honest where it speaks: FIN-06.06 is marked draft with r.013's scope named, FD-25 says the 365-day ladder year differs from the revolution, the ask's sha256 matches handoffSha256, and no machine word reaches the person's screen.

READINESS FOR r.013 — the honest phrase is 'rules without shapes'. The N–T Balance view can reuse SECTIONS and FIELDS as they are, but every value it shows needs a type that does not exist (defect 2). The U entry has a correct rule and no store to land in, and the plan it would add into is a constant (defect 4). The actuals column has the right arithmetic and the right window, and no bridge from the record: two lengths and no sign (defect 1), plus names that mislead the comparison (6). None of what exists must be undone; all three items need a new shape or store in lib/ before a pixel. Build those first — model first, styling second, exactly as the brief said for A–M.

GRADE C+. The flow model alone is B+ to A−: exact to the brief, pure, one-constant conversions, fifty-nine asserts. The surface a stranger meets is a C: an honest ladder with the wrong person's numbers, a chart at zero, a view named but not there, five '$0.0000' rows, and — behind sign-in — pickers that will not stay open, behind a gate that asserted their presence. Readiness for r.013 is a C: the right rules are in the right file, and each of the three r.013 items needs rework in the model to be honest. C+ is where both reviewers landed and where the evidence lands.

---

## Pangu

### Pangu · walk — grade C+

_Constructive walk — a stranger with the operator's own sheet, on a phone, 60 seconds on /financial-2525 at v.000_r.012 (commit 201a8ec)_

**One sentence:** The model now holds the one idea a bank app cannot (every transaction as a rate over its own span, summed against a budget per field), but the glass at r.012 never calls it: the ladder is a static copy of the sheet, the timeline dropdown records a word that changes nothing, and the two $/min numbers the whole system exists to compare are never placed on the same line.

**Findings**

- **The operator's stated goal — '$/min against budget' — is modelled and never shown: actualsByField, recurrenceDays and ratePerMinute are exported from ladder.ts and have zero call sites in the component** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:129-146 defines ratePerMinute/actualsByField; `grep -n 'actualsByField\|recurrenceDays\|ratePerMinute' command-ux1.tsx` at 201a8ec returns nothing. The ladder is built from the seed only: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:138 `netLadder(SHEET_LINES, period)` — the person's own record (txs, line 116) never reaches the table. On the glass the released rate (8.25 ¢/min, line 262) and the budget's income rate (6.73 ¢/min, line 354) sit in two panels 1,000 px apart with no delta. The record (FIN-06.06) files the actuals column as a draft for r.013 — honest, but it means addendum 22's 'goal is to track $/min against budget' is not visible in r.012.
  - Fix: Add an ACTUAL column (and a Δ) to the ladder: per section, `actualsByField(txs mapped to {fieldId, amount: amountCents/100, atMs, days: recurrenceDays(recurrence) || motDays})` over the current 33-day frame (frameOf already gives startMs/endMs, line 126), re-expressed in the chosen period with toPeriod. Net row gains budget / actual / Δ. One useMemo beside `totals`.
- **Every picker on the signed-in forms is defined inside the render function, so the 1 Hz clock remounts it each second and an open dropdown closes before the person can pick** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:157 `const LadderPicker = ({…}) => (…)` is declared inside FinancialCommandUX1; line 95 `setInterval(() => setNow(Date.now()), 1000)` re-renders the component every second; a new function identity per render is a new element type to React, so the three <select>s at lines 160, 165, 170 are unmounted and remounted every tick. Confirmed by the operator on the live site (addendum 26: 'this drop down goes away quick!') and by the r.014 correction (84f28f5) which hoists LadderPicker to module level for exactly this reason.
  - Fix: Hoist LadderPicker (and any future form sub-component) to module scope and pass `t` and `secLabel`/`fieldLabel` as props — the r.014 shape. Add the source gate the r.014 commit added so it never regresses.
- **A refused DEPOSIT prints its reason under the WITHDRAWAL panel, below the fold; the deposit panel stays silent** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:176 one shared `refusal` state; lines 187-188 recordDeposit sets it; line 318 is the only render site and it sits inside `#fin-withdraw-form` (line 310). Same for saveFailed (line 319). On a 390 px phone the deposit form is ~1 panel above, so an empty amount or a mistyped stamp looks like nothing happened. The deposit form also clears nothing on refusal, so the person presses Record it again.
  - Fix: Render `{refusal && …}` and `{saveFailed && …}` under each form's own button (or keep one refusal state per form). r.013's single transaction form removes the split, but the message must still sit beside the button that was pressed.
- **A deposit carries two different lengths — the 'MoT · length in days' input AND the 'Timeline' dropdown — and only the first does anything** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:304 `dMot` input (default 30.333) and lines 169-172 the recurrence <select> (default 'Every 33 days') are both on the deposit form; line 190 writes both to the record (motDays, recurrence). accrual.ts:53/75 reads motDays only; recurrence is never read by anything on the glass. A stranger with the sheet sees '30.333 days' in one box and 'Every 33 days' in the next and cannot tell which one the $/min obeys; the honest answer is 'the typed one, and the dropdown is decoration'. The withdrawal form (line 315) also asks for a timeline that has no effect at all.
  - Fix: One length per transaction: the timeline dropdown sets days (once → 0, weekly → 7, 33 d, 91, 365) with a 'custom (days)' option that reveals the number input; motDays becomes derived from it. For a withdrawal, hide the timeline or make it mean something (a recurring bill). r.013 kept both; this should be folded there.
- **The duplicate-insurance WARNING exists in the model and never reaches the glass** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: ladder.ts:110 pushes 'DUPLICATE_INSURANCE' into totals.warnings; command-ux1.tsx:138 receives `totals` and `grep -n warnings command-ux1.tsx` returns nothing. Brief rule 5 (addendum 22 line 252): 'If a premium is entered in both, warn' — the warn is a gate-only truth today. The sheet's Insurance 200 is bridged to D.auto_renters_home (budget.ts:74), so the moment a person enters B.housing_insurance the condition is real and silent.
  - Fix: Render `totals.warnings` as an amber line under the Net row (one lexicon key, 'A premium is entered in both Housing and Insurance — Net counts both'), and mark the two rows.
- **A section with nothing in it prints '$0.0000' — four decimals of nothing — on five of thirteen rows** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:141 `usdUnit` switches to usd4 whenever |cents| < 100, so zero becomes '$0.0000' for H · I · J · K · M at every unit (captures p-06/p-07; portrait-info.json rows 'H · Family | $0.0000'). A stranger reads it as a tiny sub-cent rate, not an empty row; on 'per year' the same row still says $0.0000 beside $35,393.94.
  - Fix: Print zero as '—' (or '$0.00') regardless of unit: `cents === 0 ? '—' : usdUnit(cents)`.
- **The withdrawal form asks for 'Deposit day and time (CST)'** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:314 reuses `t("fin.deposit_at")` for the withdrawal's stamp input; lexicon englishDefault 'Deposit day and time (CST)' (lexicon-data.ts:2910). A person taking money out is asked when they deposited it.
  - Fix: One neutral key ('Day and time (CST)') for both, as r.013's single form already does.
- **The chart's <svg height="auto"> throws a console error on every render in Chromium** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:448 `height="auto"`; the sibling probe log (fleet-fin/probe-log.txt) records `Error: <svg> attribute height: Expected length, "auto"` in both orientations. The chart still draws (viewBox + width 100%), but the surface's own gate rule is drawErrN === 0.
  - Fix: Drop the height attribute (CSS `h-auto`) or set a numeric height.
- **Two identical typed transactions silently become one: the record id is `d-<when>-<cents>` and append() is idempotent on id, so the second press clears the form with no entry and no refusal** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:190 and :197 build ids from the stamp and the amount only; record.ts:35 `if (rec.entries.some((e) => e.tx.id === tx.id)) return rec;` then line 191 `setDAmt("")` runs anyway. With a blank date the instant differs by milliseconds so it rarely bites; with a typed stamp (two $50 groceries at 2026.10.01_12.00..00) the second vanishes and the person believes it was recorded.
  - Fix: Either refuse with a reason ('already on the record at rev N') when append returns the same record, or include the record length / a nonce in the id and let the chain hash keep tamper-evidence.

**Keep**

- The model is the right shape for the innovation: ladder.ts:130-146 treats every transaction as a rate over its own span and sums overlaps against a window — this is the thing a bank app cannot do (a bank has balances and dated rows; it has no notion that a $3,604.49 paycheck is 8.25 ¢ every minute for 30.333 days). Once it reaches the glass, 'am I ahead of budget right now, this minute' becomes a one-line answer.
- The lock is implemented literally and verifiably: twenty sections, sixty fields with the brief's exact labels, fixed period factors, Net = I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr, U as a rule not a chip, Alcohol one target — gated by tests/financial-ladder.test.mjs (59 asserts) rather than by memory. toPeriod is one constant (ladder.ts:88), so the per-minute → per-year switch is drift-free as the brief demands.
- The ladder on the phone is readable in 60 seconds: section rows with their icon, the field beneath, Net last and red (captures p-06/p-07; fleet-fin/portrait-info.json measures the unit select at 290 of 316 px). A stranger with the sheet finds all eight of his lines where he expects them — Fitness under Health, Fun under Lifestyle — and sees his own −$200 without any 'you can afford it' copy.
- Nothing on the glass claims more than happened: the record panel says 'On this phone only — a cloud copy follows once sign-in carries it'; the example badge says whose paycheck it is; FIN-06.06 files the N–T view, the U entry, the detail sheet and the actuals column as draft rather than as shipped.
- R-CORE discipline held through a large model change: every r.006–r.011 entry and its chain hash is untouched (accrual.ts:35-37 fields optional), the old category still prints on the record (command-ux1.tsx:181), and the budget block keeps the sheet's own arithmetic slips on the record rather than fixing them silently.
- The sixty-second walk never hits a dead end: the guide card says the next action (Sign in ↓), the strip carries released · available and a DEPOSIT pill, and the chart's Clock ⇄ MoT toggle is one state for the whole card so the year line and the axis agree.

**Report (verbatim)**

PANGU — THE CONSTRUCTIVE WALK, r.012 (201a8ec), phone, 60 seconds, the operator's own sheet in hand (Income 3,200 · Home 700 · Car 1,800 · Insurance 200 · Electric 150 · Food 300 · Fitness 50 · Fun 200 per 33 days).

WHAT I SAW, TOP TO BOTTOM (captures fleet-fin/walk-portrait-full.png, p-01…p-09; code read from 201a8ec)

0–10 s · The shell. Three glyphs, FINANCIAL · 2525, 'the MoT Financial System · Version 000 · revision 0.012'. A card titled 'Money as time — this MoT' with a DEPOSIT pill and a five-step rail (DEPOSIT 1 · HOLD · RELEASE $0.00 · WITHDRAW 0 · RECORD 1). I understood: there is a sequence money goes through. I did not understand what 'MoT' is; nothing on the first screen says 'Measure of Time'. The amber YOUR TURN card tells me to sign in and that the example below is the operator's paycheck. Good — I know whose numbers these are.

10–25 s · The clock and the chart. 'Released · Deposit $0.00 · In escrow $0.00 · Withdrawable $0.00' — all zero because the example deposit is dated 2026.10.01 07:00 and it is 2026.09.30. The chart (command-ux1.tsx:417-465) shows a green line rising and an orange line falling over 30 d 7 h, five axis marks '0 h 0 min · 7 d 13 h · 15 d 3 h · 22 d 17 h · 30 d 7 h'. I pressed the orbit icon: the heading became '0.0000..0000 / 298.3463..2448 A-units of the revolution' and the axis became three-line numbers (2669 / .0833 / ..0514). As a stranger I understood the Clock view instantly (money released over a month) and the MoT view not at all — but the toggle is honest, one tap returns me, and addendum 14 wants exactly this default. What I would press next: the line itself, to see 'how much per minute' — it is printed above (ratePerMin) only when the deposit is releasing, so today I see 'Released · Deposit' and no rate. The planet dropdown (Earth · Mars) on a finance page made me smile and then I left it alone.

25–35 s · The year. 'Day 271 · Quarter 3 · 89/91 · 33-day frame 9 · 7/33 · Perihelion (Austin CST standard) · 2026.01.03_11.15..00 CST'. I understood 'Day 271 of the year, quarter 3'. '33-day frame 9 · 7/33' I could not place against the ladder's 'per 33 days' until I read calendar.ts:117-126. This is the one place where three different months collide for a stranger: the paycheck's 30.333, the sheet's 33, and the dropdown's 'per month (91)'. The brief locks 33 and 91; the cost of the lock is that the word 'month' now means a quarter on this surface, and the surface does not say so anywhere a stranger reads.

35–40 s · Sign in. 'EXAMPLE · the operator's paycheck as data — sign in to record your own' and a cyan Sign in button. The forms are gated, so I read them (command-ux1.tsx:296-331). Two panels: DEPOSIT (Amount · Deposit day and time · MoT · length in days · Memo · Section · Field · Timeline · Record it) and WITHDRAWAL (Amount · Deposit day and time · Section · Field · Timeline · Withdraw). The two dropdowns are the delegated decision and it is the right one: 'A · Income' then 'Income / Wages (take-home)'; change the section and the field resets to its first row (line 160). What I would press and what would happen: I would open 'Section' — and at r.012 it would close under my finger within a second, because LadderPicker is an inline component re-created by the 1 Hz clock (line 157 + line 95; the operator hit this on the live site, addendum 26; fixed r.014). I would then enter 3200 with no date and press Record it, which works; enter 'Oct 1' as the date, press Record it, and see nothing — the refusal prints under the WITHDRAWAL panel (line 318), one screen down. And I would look at 'MoT · length in days 30.333' beside 'Timeline · Every 33 days' and not know which one my $/min obeys (only motDays does; recurrence is recorded and inert).

40–55 s · The ladder. 'PERSONAL FINANCE LADDER A–M', Unit 'per 33 days' full width, then A · Income $3,200.00 / Income / Wages (take-home) $3,200.00, B · Housing $700.00 / Rent / Mortgage, C · Transportation $1,800.00 / Auto Payment, D · Insurance $200.00 / Auto / Renters / Home, E · Utilities $150.00 / Electric / Gas, F · Food $300.00 / Groceries, G · Health $50.00 / Mental Health / Physical Fitness, H · Family $0.0000, I · Debt service $0.0000, J · Taxes $0.0000, K · Work $0.0000, L · Lifestyle $200.00 / Fun / Hobbies / Clothing, M · Transfers $0.0000, Net −$200.00 in red. Every one of my eight lines is where I would look for it. I switched to 'per minute': Income $0.0673, Net −$0.0042; 'per year': Income $35,393.94, Net −$2,212.12. The arithmetic is one constant (ladder.ts:88) and the capture agrees to the cent. Two things a stranger stumbles on: '$0.0000' on the five empty rows reads as a sub-cent rate, not as 'nothing here'; and there is no way to touch any number — the ladder is SHEET_LINES (budget.ts:78), a seed, not my sheet. The note 'Cash, investments, debts, net worth, credit, protection and goals live on the Balance view — never per minute' is correct and the Balance view does not exist yet (FIN-06.06 draft) — the note promises a door that is not there.

55–60 s · The record. 'THE RECORD · CHAIN VERIFIED · 2026.10.01_07.00..00 · Deposit · PAYROLL · PENDING DIRECT DEPOSIT · $3,604.49 · 30.333 · On this phone only — a cloud copy follows once sign-in carries it.' Honest and short. The folded Trinity wheel and the R-CORE badge close the page.

WHAT THE BRIEF (ADDENDUM 22) PROMISES THAT I DO NOT SEE
· 'goal is to tract $/min against budget long term … and to see $/min accrual process' — the accrual process is on the chart; '$/min against budget' is not on any screen. actualsByField (ladder.ts:134) is the exact function for it and has no caller in command-ux1.tsx. The released rate (8.25 ¢/min when the paycheck is releasing, line 262) and the ladder's income rate (6.73 ¢/min at 'per minute', line 354) are never on one line. This is the Pangu finding: the model has crossed the threshold a bank app cannot cross — every transaction is a rate over its own span, so 'this minute, am I ahead or behind' is computable — and the glass stops one call short of showing it.
· Rule 5 'warn' on duplicate insurance — in totals.warnings (ladder.ts:110), never rendered.
· 'N–T on a second surface (Balance / Goals)' — declared draft (FIN-06.06), referenced by the stock_note, absent.
· 'U amounts must name a destination field' — amortize() exists (ladder.ts:117); there is no entry for it, so the lumpy premiums and taxes the sheet does not carry cannot be spread into B/D/J yet.
· Field.amountNative / nativePeriod 'stored in user's entry period' — the type exists (LadderLine); no editor exists, so the only lines on the ladder are the seed's.

WHAT THE MoT TIMELINE ENABLES THAT A BANK APP CANNOT (the constructive half)
1. A rate ledger instead of a balance ledger. A bank row is (date, amount). A Financial-2525 row is (date, amount, length). That third field turns every row into a $/min that runs from its date and stops at its end — the paycheck at +8.25 ¢/min for 30.333 days, the rent at −1.47 ¢/min for 33 days. Summed, the record IS a live Net $/min — the same equation as the ladder's Net row, but from actuals. Put them on one line: BUDGET −0.0042 · ACTUAL +0.0683 · Δ. That line is the product.
2. A window the person chooses. frameOf (calendar.ts:121) already gives the current 33-day frame's startMs/endMs; actualsByField over that window per section, re-expressed in the ladder's unit, is a third column that costs one useMemo. 'Frame 9 · 7/33' then means something: 'seven days into this frame, Food is at $0.0071/min against $0.0063 budgeted'.
3. The chart as the comparison, not the deposit. Today the chart draws one deposit's release (line 277 `tx={focus}`). The same series() machinery (accrual.ts:124) can draw two polylines: budget Net accrued across the frame (a straight line) and actual Net accrued (the real one). Where the real line is above the straight line, the person is ahead; below, behind. That is the sheet's 'Swings' drawing (sheet 3, ask line 309), which the operator already drew by hand.
4. The timeline as the only length. Once 'Timeline' sets the days (once → 0, weekly → 7, 33, 91, 365, custom), the MoT input is derived and the two-lengths confusion disappears; a withdrawal with a weekly timeline becomes a recurring bill the ladder can expect before it lands.
5. The U rule as a form row. 'Property tax 1,200 / year → B · Property Tax / HOA / Fees' is amortize() with mapsTo; shown as a per-period addition on the ladder with a small 'U' mark on the row, never a chip — exactly what the brief allows.

GRADE
C+. The model is A-grade and locked by a real gate; the ladder on a phone is readable and honest; the record never over-claims. But the stranger meets two defects on the only interactive surface (a dropdown that closes under the finger; a refusal that prints on the wrong panel), the deposit form asks for two lengths and obeys one, the brief's warning never reaches the glass, and the one number the operator built the system to see — his $/min against his own budget — is computed in ladder.ts and shown nowhere. r.013's single form and r.014's picker law close two of these; the actuals column is the one that would move this from a budget table a bank app could also draw to the thing only a MoT ledger can.
