**Keep**

- The model IS the lock, verified not asserted: Net = I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr holds at all eight periods (probe diffs ≤ 3.6e-12 absolute on $35k/year figures), 60 fields with the brief's labels byte-exact, 13 flow sections, U refuses N–T (ladder.ts:118), a balance in P never nets (ladder.ts:104), transfers subtract once.
- toPeriod is one constant and does not drift: worst round trip over all 64 period pairs 4.5e-13, 5,000 days33↔second hops on −200 drift 2.8e-14 — far inside a cent.
- FD-24 holds on the r.012 export: the unit dropdown and all six picker selects measure 290 of 290 px in portrait and sit at their own width (224 / 330 px) in landscape.
- The section→field cascade (command-ux1.tsx:160) resets the field to the section's first field, so a field never shows under the wrong section (measured: B → B.rent_mortgage with B's six options).
- Older entries with a category and no field still print with their icon (txWhat, :179-183) and their hashes are untouched because field and recurrence are optional (accrual.ts:36-37) — the record was not rewritten.
- actualsByField (ladder.ts:134) is the right primitive for the operator's goal — the overlap of a transaction's span with a window at its own rate — and FIN-06.06 says DRAFT honestly rather than claiming the column.

**Report (verbatim)**

PANGU · B — the adversarial pass on Financial-2525 v.000_r.012 (commit 201a8ec), read-only.

WHAT I READ AND HOW I PROVED IT. The seven libraries, the component, the icon map, the ask (23 addenda, the A–U brief in addendum 22), the record JSON, the three gates, and the captures. The export under frontend/out is stamped v.000_r.015, and no r012-*.png exist in scratchpad/shots (r011 and r013 do), so the headless half of this pass ran on a faithful rebuild of r.012 — `git archive 201a8ec frontend` → `next build` under fleet-fin/r012tree (its index.html reads v.000_r.012) — with a forged sign-in cache so the forms render, at 390×844 and 844×390. Everything written went under fleet-fin/ (probe-ladder.mjs, probe-cal.mjs, probe-glass.mjs, probe-dup.mjs, shots/). The r.015 export was run once as a control.

THE LADDER MATH — it holds. Net at every period equals the 33-day Net times the brief's constant to within 3.6e-12 on $35k figures; every one of the 64 period round trips returns within 4.5e-13; five thousand days33↔second hops drift 2.8e-14. The sixty labels are byte-exact; U refuses N–T; P never nets. I could not break the model. What I could break is everything around it.

THE PICKERS. command-ux1.tsx:157 defines LadderPicker inside the component body while :95 re-renders at 1 Hz, so React rebuilds every select every second. Measured: focus the section select, wait 2.6 s, the node is gone and focus is on BODY. The surface gate's regex for the picker markup (financial-surface.test.mjs:51) was green the whole time. The operator found it on his phone hours later (addendum 26). The cascade itself is right — section B → B.rent_mortgage with B's six fields — and every picker is 290 of 290 px in portrait.

THE TIMELINES. A deposit carries two lengths out of the box: motDays 30.333 (the escrow law, accrual.ts:53/75) and recurrence days33 (the ladder law, ladder.ts:126) — :145/:151/:190. They disagree by 8.8 % in $/min; at the end of the escrow's MoT the ladder still has 8 % of the paycheck to spread. The brief and FD-26 say the timeline IS the MoT; the code stores two. And \"Weekly\" is a length, not a repetition (ladder.ts:123) — a weekly $100 entered once spreads over seven days and stops.

THE RECORD. Four things. (1) id = kind·stamp·cents (:190/:197) and append is idempotent on id (record.ts:35): a second paycheck of the same amount at the same minute vanishes; the amount field clears as if recorded; no sentence — proven on the glass. (2) A one-byte corruption of the store → loadRecord returns empty (record.ts:65) → :114 adopts it → the next commit saves over the original (:177): a recorded transaction discarded, the exact class the FIX-THE-CLASS AAR names, with no gate on the device half. (3) The chain is unkeyed FNV-1a (record.ts:25-30); a re-chained forgery verifies ok, so \"CHAIN VERIFIED\" (:368) says more than it knows. (4) Kind and field are independent: a withdrawal on A·Income is accepted and printed; actualsByField has no sign (ladder.ts:138-141), so when actuals do reach the glass that withdrawal reads as income.

THE RULES ON THE GLASS. The duplicate-insurance warning is computed (ladder.ts:110) and never rendered (no reference to totals.warnings in the component), Net counts both premiums (:111) while the brief says \"do not double-count\", and the sheet never contains B.housing_insurance (budget.ts:72-75), so the rule is unreachable on r.012 anyway. Alcohol's target is not passed from the glass (:138), and with target L the row would print under F (:355 groups by fieldOf().section) while the money sits in L's total.

THE CALENDAR. Earth is right: the 2026 year opens 2026-01-03T17:15Z, runs 364.387 d, day 365 is the down day, pastFull flips only on the long years (2027, 2030). Mars is not: positionInYear uses Earth's perihelion table for any planet (calendar.ts:98-101; planet-ltu.ts:55 anchors Mars on \"perihelion\"), so Mars resets at 1909 of 3600 with Earth's 91-day quarters; and the chart sub-line at :447 omits planet.yearDays, printing Earth positions (2669 → 2968) under a Mars axis (1419 → 1578). The gate at surface:38 regexes the axis call and misses :447. Two definitions of the down day coexist (count-365 vs Dec 31).

THE PRINT. Per second, seven of 21 nonzero figures read $0.0000 (G.mental_physical is $0.0000175/s); the math has no drift, the four-decimal print throws it away.

THE LEXICON. At r.012 the 90 ladder keys are staged with no fill: the Spanish glass reads \"Unidad / A · Income / Income / Wages (take-home) / Neto / Section / Field / Timeline / Every 33 days\". The gate prints PENDING and passes. The fill landed in 50e57c0, after the ship.

THE GATES. financial-surface is 49 asserts, all regexes on source text; it proved the picker's markup, one of two positionInYear call sites, and the netLadder call without its alcohol argument. Nothing in test:ci loads the export. The ladder gate is sound for the lib and silent on the wiring.

PANGU'S QUESTION — what $/min against budget could become. r.012 ships a budget table and a transaction form that never meet: actualsByField exists (ladder.ts:134) and is not imported by the component. The one live $/min on the surface is the escrow release — money arriving — not the person's net burn. What the MoT timeline makes possible, and no bank app can do because its transactions are points with no span: every entry is a rate over a declared length, so the glass can show (a) net $/min NOW — income rates minus spend rates from the timelines, one ticking number beside the released clock; (b) pace per field inside the 33-day frame (frameOf already gives the window) — spent-so-far against budget-so-far, not against the month; (c) the projected frame-end cash position and a time-to-zero; (d) with repetition in the model, the forward stream on the year line, so a weekly entry draws next week's debit before it happens; (e) U amortizations as thin standing rates (the $1,200 property tax as 108.49 per 33 days) so the lumpy year reads as a flat line. All of it is one join away in the model and zero of it is on the glass. That is why the grade is C and not B: the acceptance list of the lock is met, the operator's sentence about why he wanted it is not yet.

GRADE: C. The model earns an A on its own; the glass carries two high-severity record defects, a picker that closes every second, two lengths per deposit, an unshown rule, a half-translated table and a Mars sub-line the gates cannot see.

## Sofia

### Sofia · walk — grade C+

_Constructive walk — a stranger with the operator's own sheet (Income 3,200 · Home 700 · Car 1,800 · Insurance 200 · Electric 150 · Food 300 · Fitness 50 · Fun 200 per 33 days) opening /financial-2525 on a phone for 60 seconds; code read from commit 201a8ec (v.000_r.012), captures and a headless probe of frontend/out at 390×844 and 844×390_

**One sentence:** The model keeps the lock to the letter and says what it has not built yet, but the glass still serves only the operator: a stranger with the sheet cannot put his own expenses against the budget, picks a timeline that changes nothing he can see, and is pointed at a Balance view that does not exist.

**Findings**

- **The timeline dropdown has no visible consequence: nothing on the glass reads a transaction's recurrence, and $/min still runs off motDays alone** (high)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:169-173 renders <select data-fin-timeline>; :180 txWhat prints only the field; :215 the roster label prints `${usd(d.amountCents)} · ${d.motDays}`; :373 the record prints motDays and the hash, never the recurrence. The live rate is frontend/lib/financial-2525/accrual.ts:75 (`tx.amountCents / (tx.motDays * MIN_PER_DAY)`). ladder.ts:134 actualsByField and :129 ratePerMinute exist but command-ux1.tsx:48 does not import them. The operator's own words (ask line 273-276): 'each transactions has an MoT timeline … so when we do $/min its based of transaction date and how long it last … goal is to tract $/min against budget'.
  - Fix: Either make recurrenceDays(tx.recurrence) the deposit's length when motDays is absent (one length, see next defect) or print the timeline beside the field on the roster and the record and add the actuals column (FIN-06.06) so the stranger sees what the pick did; until then label the dropdown 'recorded for the Balance view (r.013)' so it does not promise a $/min it does not deliver.
- **Two lengths on one deposit and their defaults disagree: 'MoT · length in days 30.333' and 'Timeline · every 33 days' on the same form** (high)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:145 `dMot` defaults to SRC.mot.payMotDays (30.333); :151 `dRec` defaults to 'days33'; :190 commits both (`motDays: …, recurrence: dRec`); the worked example at :85 ships the same contradiction (`motDays: SRC.example.motDays, recurrence: "days33"`). The stranger is asked 'how long does this money last?' twice and the two answers differ by 2.667 days; the record stores both.
  - Fix: One length per transaction. Derive motDays from the timeline (once → 0, weekly → 7, 33 days → 33, month 91 → 91, yearly → 365) with the pay MoT 30.333 offered as a named option, or keep motDays only for deposits and drop the timeline from the deposit form — but never two fields answering one question.
- **A stranger cannot record his sheet's expenses: a 'Withdrawal' is validated as escrow money moving to his own account, so 'Car 1,800' on day 1 is refused as INSUFFICIENT and 'Home 700' inside 3 h is refused as HOLD** (high)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:152 defaults the withdrawal to 'B · Rent / Mortgage', inviting the stranger to log an expense; :197-199 then calls validateWithdrawal, which accrual.ts:113-119 caps at availableCents (released AND past the 3-hour hold). With the example pay releasing at $0.0825/min (accrual.ts:214), $700 is not withdrawable for ~5.9 days and $1,800 for ~15 days. The ladder (:347-362) is the operator's SHEET_LINES seed, read-only (budget.ts:78), so the stranger's own figures never reach the ladder either — the only $/min 'against budget' is the operator's sheet against itself.
  - Fix: State the invariant in the person's words first: an expense is not an escrow withdrawal. Give the stranger a way to put his sheet's lines on the ladder (addendum 28's edit mode, or a plan line per field with its own period), and let a flow transaction be recorded against a field without the escrow gate; keep the escrow law for the one kind that really moves released money out.
- **The stock note points at a 'Balance view' that does not exist; there is nothing to press** (medium)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:363 renders fin.stock_note: 'Cash, investments, debts, net worth, credit, protection and goals live on the Balance view — never per minute'; the probe counted 0 links or buttons inside [data-fin-budget]. The record is honest (FIN-06.06 status 'draft', 'the surface lands as r.013'); the glass is not.
  - Fix: Until the N–T surface ships, the sentence should say it is coming ('… will live on the Balance view, r.013') or be a disabled control labelled as such; when it ships, make the sentence the link.
- **Brief rule 5's duplicate-insurance WARNING is computed and never shown** (medium)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:138 `const totals = useMemo(() => netLadder(SHEET_LINES, period), [period])` — totals.warnings is populated by frontend/lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:110 ('DUPLICATE_INSURANCE') but no line of the budget block (:334-364) reads it. The brief's acceptance says the warning fires; today it fires into nothing.
  - Fix: Render totals.warnings under the ladder as one sentence in the person's words ('A housing premium is entered under both Housing and Insurance — Net counts both; choose one'), and gate it in tests/financial-surface.test.mjs.
- **Refusals and the save-failed notice appear only under the WITHDRAWAL panel, even when the DEPOSIT button raised them** (medium)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:187-188 recordDeposit calls setRefusal(t('fin.reason_amount') / 'fin.reason_stamp'); the only render is :318 `{refusal && …}` and :319 `{saveFailed && …}` inside the withdrawal form (:310-320). On a phone the deposit's 'Record it' (:308) sits a full panel above; the stranger taps, nothing seems to happen, and the red sentence is off-screen below the withdrawal fields.
  - Fix: Render the refusal directly under the button that was pressed (one refusal slot per form, or one slot keyed by the form that raised it), and scroll it into view.
- **The withdrawal form's date is labelled 'Deposit day and time (CST)'** (low)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:314 reuses t('fin.deposit_at') (lexicon-data.ts: 'Deposit day and time (CST)') for the withdrawal's `wAt` input. The later r.013 capture reads 'Day and time (CST)', so a new key was needed; at r.012 the stranger is asked for a deposit date on a withdrawal.
  - Fix: A kind-neutral key ('Day and time (CST)') on the withdrawal form at minimum.
- **The sheet's Fixed / Variable kind vanished from the ladder at r.012; the r.011 table had a KIND column** (medium)
  - Evidence: r011-portrait.png shows CATEGORY · KIND · PER HOUR; command-ux1.tsx:349 now renders two <th> only (section · unit) and :354-356 print no kind, although ladder.ts:45 carries `kind` on every Field and the operator's own sheet writes F / V beside every line (ask line 305). Addendum 22 asks for one row per field; it does not ask to drop F/V. CLAUDE.md rule 6: never remove a UI element the operator did not ask to change.
  - Fix: Print the field's kind (Income · Fixed · Variable · Transfer) as a muted mark on each field row, or ask the operator before leaving it out.
- **The phone strip prints two unlabelled dollar figures** (low)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:391-392: `{usd(bal.releasedCents)} · {usd(bal.availableCents)}` with no words; the fold capture reads '$0.00 · $0.00 | DEPOSIT'. The Session strip the shell borrows has meaning the stranger here does not have.
  - Fix: Two-letter labels or a title attribute ('released · available'); the strip has room at 390 px.
- **The day-and-time is hand-typed in a bespoke grammar; February 31 is accepted and silently becomes March** (medium)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:303 and :314 are plain text inputs whose only hint is the placeholder stamp; the refusal text (lexicon 'The day and time must read YYYY.MM.DD_HH.MM..SS') teaches the grammar only after a failure. frontend/lib/financial-2525/mot.ts:166 checks `d > 31` only, so '2026.02.31_07.00..00' parses and :154 Date.UTC rolls it to March 3 — a wrong instant on a chain-hashed record.
  - Fix: A native datetime-local input rendered to the stamp (the stamp stays the record's form), or at least a month-aware day check that refuses rather than rolls.
- **Zero rows print '$0.0000' beside '$3,200.00' in the same column** (low)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:141 `usdUnit = (cents) => (Math.abs(cents) >= 100 ? usd(Math.round(cents)) : usd4(cents))` — any amount under one dollar, including zero, takes the four-decimal form; the probe's 33-day view reads 'H · Family $0.0000 … L · Lifestyle $200.00'. The stranger reads five phantom-precision zeros among whole dollars.
  - Fix: Print zero as '$0.00' (or '—') in every unit; keep four decimals only when the figure is non-zero and under a dollar.
- **The EXAMPLE sentence prints twice on the signed-out glass** (low)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:253 (inside the YOUR TURN card) and :328 (the sign-in panel) both render t('fin.example_badge'); the guide sentence at :206 says the same thing a third way. The fold capture shows two of the three in one screen.
  - Fix: One sentence in the guide card; the sign-in panel keeps only the button.
- **Ninety ladder words are English in the other thirty-two languages (staged, declared)** (low)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/lexicon-data.ts:2999-3018 (fin.sec.*), the sixty fin.field.* and five fin.rec.* keys were added at r.012 and staged in AFTER_FILL (frontend/tests/lexicon-coverage.test.mjs:19); the commit says the ×32 fill follows. A Spanish or Swahili reader sees Spanish chrome with 'Rent / Mortgage' and 'Every 33 days' in English — the one surface the operator said must be in all 33 languages.
  - Fix: Already owed as its own commit; land it before the stranger in another language is invited.
- **Sections Q and R share one icon** (low)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/category-icon.tsx:37 `Q: Gauge, R: Gauge`; no stranger sees N–T at r.012 (the pickers are A–M, :161), so the twin is invisible today and becomes a defect the day the Balance view ships.
  - Fix: A distinct stroke for Net worth (done later per the ledger; said here for r.012's record).

**Keep**

- The lock is kept to the letter: ladder.ts:31-40 names exactly A–T, :48-76 carries the sixty slash-merged labels verbatim against the brief (checked row by row — 'Housing insurance', 'Alcohol (or Fun)', 'Pets (food, vet, insurance)', 'Assets − Debts'), :27 the fixed periods with no 30-day month, :111 the Net equation, :117-121 U refusing N–T, :105 Alcohol to one target — and the gate (financial-ladder, 59 cases) is in test:ci.
- The glass does render what the brief's acceptance asks on the thirteen rows: the probe read A · Income $3,200.00 … Net −$200.00 per 33 days and −$0.0042 per minute (the operator's own DTM), Net red, section icons on every row, U nowhere; a period switch is one constant (ladder.ts:88).
- Honesty on the record: FIN-06.06 is marked draft, not implemented; the ninety keys are listed in AFTER_FILL rather than silently English; the record panel speaks in the signer's voice ('On this phone only — a cloud copy follows once sign-in carries it') and names no vendor or database.
- The section → field cascade resets the field to the section's first entry (command-ux1.tsx:160), so a stranger can never commit a field from the wrong section; every picker is the panel's full width in portrait (:156, :340-341).
- The chart's one-state toggle works as addendum 13 asks: the probe flipped Clock → MoT and the title, the five axis marks (split into three lines so they fit 390 px, :459) and the year line all changed together.
- Older r.006–r.011 entries keep their category and their hashes (accrual.ts:35-37 optional fields; txWhat :179-183 prints either) — the append-only law held through the taxonomy change.
- No 'safe / affordable' copy, no CAGR, no multiples anywhere on the surface; Net is a number and a colour, nothing more.

**Report (verbatim)**

WHAT I WALKED. The code under review is commit 201a8ec (v.000_r.012): command-ux1.tsx, category-icon.tsx, the seven lib files, the ask with its twenty-four addenda, and the record JSON. The export in frontend/out was built after r.015 (the build banner reads SHA 3c9c439, revision 0.015), so the headless numbers below come from that export; for a signed-out stranger the two surfaces are the same walk — the forms are hidden either way (command-ux1.tsx:297) and the r.012 ladder, chart, year line and record are unchanged in shape. I read the forms from the r.012 code. Probe output and four captures are under scratchpad/fleet-fin/sofia-walk/.

SECOND 0–10, THE FIRST SCREEN (390×844). Build banner, globe, three glyphs, FINANCIAL · 2525, 'the MoT Financial System · Version 000 · revision'. Then a card titled 'Money as time — this MoT' with a pill DEPOSIT, a rail reading 'Step 1 of 5 · Deposit' with five chips, and a yellow YOUR TURN card: 'Sign in to record your own deposits — the example below is the operator's paycheck as data', a Sign in button, and the same sentence again in cyan. At the very bottom a fixed strip: '$0.00 · $0.00 | DEPOSIT'. What I understood: this is somebody's money app, it has steps, and I am asked to sign in. What I did not understand: 'MoT'. It is in the title, the subtitle, the rail's sentence ('amount, day and time, MoT') and nowhere is it expanded for me — the lexicon context knows it is Measure of Time (lexicon-data.ts:2900) but the glass never says so. 'Step 1 of 5' reads as a wizard; it is actually where one deposit stands. The two unlabelled dollars on the strip mean nothing yet.

SECOND 10–25, THE DEPOSIT AND THE CHART. Scrolling: YOUR DEPOSITS with one row — '2026.10.01_07.00..00 · Income / Wages (take-home) · PAYROLL · PENDING DIRECT DEPOSIT', '$3,604.49 · 30.333'. I can read the field name; I cannot read '30.333' (days, I learn later). Then the big cyan block: 'Released · Deposit $0.00 · In escrow $0.00 · Withdrawable $0.00 · Withdrawn $0.00 · Available $0.00'. All zero — the example has not started (it is Sep 30, 18:17 CST in the sandbox; the example starts Oct 1, 07:00). Then the chart: 'MONEY AS TIME — THIS MOT · 0 H 0 MIN ELAPSED', Planet [Earth ▾], two icon buttons, a line '2026.10.01_07.00..00 · $3,604.49 · 30.333 · Pending — releases from 2026.10.01_07.00..00', a box with a rising green line and a falling orange line, axis '0 h 0 min · 7 d 13 h · 15 d 3 h · 22 d 17 h · 30 d 7 h', and a legend. This I understood: the pay comes in, and over thirty days it is released to me bit by bit, with a three-hour hold. The chart is the clearest thing on the page. I pressed the orbit icon: the title became '0.0000..0000 / 298.3463..2448 A-UNITS OF THE REVOLUTION', the axis became fifteen numbers in three lines (2669 / .0833 / ..0514 …), and the year line changed too. I pressed the clock icon to go back. I would not press the orbit icon again; I now know it exists and that it is for someone else. The Planet dropdown offers Mars. I did not press it.

SECOND 25–35, THE YEAR LINE. 'YEAR POSITION — THE ORBIT (A.B..C) · Day 271 · Quarter 3 · 89/91 · 33-day frame 9 · 7/33 · 2026.09.30_18.17..32 CST · 2026 · SOURCED · 271/365 · Perihelion (Austin CST standard) · 2026.01.03_11.15..00 CST'. I understood 'Day 271 · Quarter 3'. I did not understand 'frame', 'SOURCED', or why a money app opens its year at the perihelion. Nothing here is for me; it is the operator's calendar law, correctly printed. Then the sign-in panel repeats the EXAMPLE sentence with a Sign in button — the third time I am told to sign in.

SECOND 35–50, THE LADDER (what I came for, with the sheet in my other hand). 'PERSONAL FINANCE LADDER A–M', Unit [per 33 days ▾] full width, then thirteen bold rows with icons: A · Income $3,200.00 with 'Income / Wages (take-home) $3,200.00' beneath; B · Housing $700.00 / Rent / Mortgage; C · Transportation $1,800.00 / Auto Payment; D · Insurance $200.00 / Auto / Renters / Home; E · Utilities $150.00 / Electric / Gas; F · Food $300.00 / Groceries; G · Health $50.00 / Mental Health / Physical Fitness; H · Family $0.0000; I · Debt service $0.0000; J · Taxes $0.0000; K · Work $0.0000; L · Lifestyle $200.00 / Fun / Hobbies / Clothing; M · Transfers $0.0000; Net −$200.00 in red. This is my sheet, every number, on a bigger map — I understood it at once, and the −200 is the truth of the sheet. I switched the unit to per minute: Income $0.0673, Net −$0.0042 — I understood that my sheet loses four tenths of a cent every minute. Good. Three things stopped me: the five '$0.0000' rows among whole dollars (why four zeros?); the F/V marks from my sheet are gone (the r.011 table had a KIND column; r.012 has none, command-ux1.tsx:349); and the sentence underneath — 'Cash, investments, debts, net worth, credit, protection and goals live on the Balance view — never per minute' — which I read as an invitation and looked for something to press. There is nothing (:363; the probe counts 0 links or buttons in the panel). I would also have pressed a row to edit it with my own numbers; nothing happens, the lines are the operator's seed (budget.ts:78). The unit option 'per month (91)' I read twice; the brief owns that word, but a stranger does not know a 91-day month.

SECOND 50–60, THE RECORD AND THE REST. 'THE RECORD · CHAIN VERIFIED', one line for the example, '$3,604.49 · 30.333', 'On this phone only — a cloud copy follows once sign-in carries it'. I understood that nothing I do leaves my phone; that sentence is in my language and I trust it. A folded 'Trinity wheel' caption, a stamp line, the R-CORE badge. Done; sixty seconds.

WHAT I WOULD PRESS NEXT, AND WHAT THE CODE SAYS HAPPENS. I would sign in and try to put my sheet on the record. Reading the r.012 forms (:297-331): the DEPOSIT form is Amount · 'Deposit day and time (CST)' as free text in YYYY.MM.DD_HH.MM..SS · 'MoT · length in days' prefilled 30.333 · Memo · Section [A · Income ▾] · Field [Income / Wages (take-home) ▾] · Timeline [Every 33 days ▾] · Record it. I would type 3200, leave the date, and press Record it — and I would be asked, in effect, two different lengths for the same pay (30.333 days and 'every 33 days'), and the record would keep both (:190). Then I would open WITHDRAWAL to log 'Home 700': Amount · 'Deposit day and time (CST)' (on a withdrawal, :314) · Section [B · Housing] · Field [Rent / Mortgage] · Timeline [One time] · Withdraw. And it would be refused — 'Held — withdrawable 3 hours after the deposit', then 'More than is withdrawable' for the car at 1,800 — because a withdrawal here is the escrow law's withdrawal (accrual.ts:113-119), not my expense. If I mistyped the date on the deposit form, the red refusal would print under the withdrawal form, below the fold (:318). I would then pick 'Weekly' on a deposit and look for the word anywhere afterwards; it is not on the roster (:215), not on the record (:373), and does not move the $/min (accrual.ts:75). That is the moment the stranger stops trusting the dropdowns.

WHO IS SERVED, WHO IS NOT. The operator is served: his sheet, his numbers, his equation, his paycheck as the example, his calendar. The brief is served in the model, honestly: sections, labels, periods, Net, U, the warning, Alcohol, all gated. The auditor is served: the record declares FIN-06.06 draft, the ninety English keys are listed, the hashes of older entries are untouched. The stranger is not served yet: he cannot explain MoT, A.B..C, frame or perihelion; he cannot put his own eight lines on the ladder; he cannot record an expense without hitting an escrow rule written for a different kind of transaction; his timeline pick is written down and never read back; his date is typed by hand in a grammar he learns from a refusal; the one sentence that points him onward points at a view that is not there. The non-English reader is not served either — the ladder's ninety words are English in thirty-two languages until the fill lands.

WHAT THE BRIEF (ADDENDUM 22) PROMISES THAT I DO NOT SEE. UI rule 2, 'N–T on a second surface (Balance / Goals)': absent, named in a dead sentence. Rule 4, the detail sheet: absent. Rule 5, 'if a premium is entered in both, warn': computed at ladder.ts:110, never rendered (:138 vs :334-364). Rule 7, U naming a destination: no entry anywhere. 'Each transaction has an MoT timeline … so when we do $/min its based of transaction date and how long it lasts': the timeline is recorded, the $/min ignores it. 'Goal is to track $/min against budget': actualsByField (ladder.ts:134) is written and never called. The record admits all of this as r.013 work, and the commit message says 'the model first; styling second' — which is exactly what the operator asked. So the grade is not about dishonesty. It is about the stranger's sixty seconds.

GRADE: C+. The model is an A against the lock and I would not change a line of ladder.ts. The glass for the operator is a B: his sheet reads true in every unit, the chart explains escrow better than any sentence, the pickers cascade correctly. The glass for a stranger is a C: three high defects (the timeline that does nothing visible, the two lengths on one deposit, the expense that cannot be recorded) sit exactly on the path the operator said he wants to walk first — 'testing and adjusting with my own finances … to see $/min accrual process'. The small things (the $0.0000 zeros, the withdrawal's 'Deposit' label, the displaced refusal, the unlabelled strip, the dropped F/V) are a morning's work and each is cited by line. Fix the class, not the instance: one length per transaction, one meaning per word 'withdrawal', and every sentence on the glass pointing only at something that exists.

### Sofia · adversarial — grade C+

_Adversarial pass on Financial-2525 v.000_r.012 (commit 201a8ec), read-only; model probed in node against the r.012 libraries, glass probed headless against an r.012 export (fleet-fin/r012tree, stamped "revision 0.012") at 390×844 and 844×390_

**One sentence:** The model honours the lock almost to the letter and proves it with real arithmetic, but the glass that a person actually touches re-creates its three new pickers every second, records two different lengths for one deposit, double-counts the premium the brief said never to double-count, promises a Balance view that does not exist, and reads English to thirty-two languages — so the operator can admire the ladder and nobody can yet use it.

**Findings**

- **Every new picker is a component declared inside the render, so the 1 Hz clock unmounts and remounts the three selects every second — a dropdown cannot stay open on a phone** (HIGH — the operator cannot place a transaction on the delegated two-dropdown design; this is the class the operator later reported as 'this drop down goes away quick!' (addendum 26, fixed only in r.014))
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:95 `setInterval(() => setNow(Date.now()), 1000)` re-renders FinancialCommandUX1 each second; :157 `const LadderPicker = ({...}) => (` is declared INSIDE that function body, so React sees a new component type on every render and remounts :160 `<select data-fin-section>`, :165 `<select data-fin-field>`, :170 `<select data-fin-timeline>`. Headless proof (sofia-adv/remount-sofia.mjs, the r.012 pattern on React 18.3.1): after 2.5 s `nestedPickerSameNode:false, focusStillOnPicker:false` while the inline unit select (:341) keeps its node (`inlineUnitSelectSameNode:true`). The surface gate passes because it only greps for `<select data-fin-section={hook}` (tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:51).
  - Fix: Hoist LadderPicker to module level (or memoise with a stable identity) so the tick never remounts a picker; add a behavioural gate that focuses a picker, waits two ticks and asserts the same DOM node still has focus.
- **One deposit carries TWO lengths — the MoT field (30.333 d) and the timeline (every 33 days) — and nothing reconciles them; the seed example itself contradicts itself** (HIGH — the operator asked for ONE timeline per transaction 'so when we do $/min its based of transaction date and how long it last 30.333 days'; r.012 gives him two numbers that disagree by 8 %)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:145 `dMot` defaults to SRC.mot.payMotDays (30.333); :151 `dRec` defaults to "days33" (33 d); :190 commits BOTH `motDays` and `recurrence` on one entry with no check. The EXAMPLE at :85 is `motDays: SRC.example.motDays` (30.333) AND `recurrence: "days33"`. Node probe: accrual rate 8.252 ¢/min (accrual.ts:75) vs ladder rate 7.585 ¢/min (ladder.ts:129 with recurrenceDays 33) — a 8.08 % gap on the same paycheck. A blank MoT with timeline 'every 33 days' yields motDays 0 → `depositView(...).state === "released"` at once (accrual.ts:59), while the record says it runs 33 days.
  - Fix: Make the timeline THE length: derive motDays from the recurrence (with an explicit custom-days option) or refuse an entry whose two lengths disagree; one $/min per transaction, one source.
- **The duplicate-insurance rule double-counts Net — the lock says 'warn; do not double-count Net'; r.012 warns and counts both** (MEDIUM — a direct deviation from rule 5 of the brief, admitted in a comment but never surfaced to the person)
  - Evidence: ladder.ts:106 adds every line to its section before :110 pushes DUPLICATE_INSURANCE 'Net counts what was entered'. Node probe: A 1000 · B.housing_insurance 120 · D.auto_renters_home 120 → net 760 with the warning (880 without the double count). The brief (docs/asks/…addendum 22, 'UI rules' 5 and 'What not to build': 'Silent double-count of the same premium in B and D'). The warning is also never rendered: `warnings` appears nowhere in command-ux1.tsx (grep over the commit) — so on the glass the double count IS silent.
  - Fix: Either net one premium (count the B line, treat the D line as the policy map) or keep counting but render the warning on the ladder row and on Net; add a gate asserting the rendered Net equals I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr with the duplicate removed.
- **The ladder promises a 'Balance view' for N–T that does not exist on any route** (MEDIUM — the panel claims more than happened (the signer-voice law: 'the panel never claims more than happened'); a stranger will look for the view)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:363 renders fin.stock_note: 'Cash, investments, debts, net worth, credit, protection and goals live on the Balance view — never per minute' (lexicon-data.ts:3088). The commit's routes are only app/financial-2525/page.tsx and app/main/Financial-2525/page.tsx; headless probe on the r.012 export: `balanceLink: 0` elements mention 'balance'. The record itself says FIN-06.06 is 'DRAFT (planned for r.013)' (financial-2525.v.000.json crs FIN-06.06).
  - Fix: Say what is true: 'N–T are not on this ladder (a Balance view follows)'; or ship the view before the sentence.
- **The 90 ladder keys read English in all 32 other languages at r.012 — sections, fields, timelines and the 'Section' header are untranslated while the words around them are translated** (MEDIUM — the whole-app lexicon law (operator 2026-09-13: all UX in all 33 languages); it is staged honestly, but it shipped mixed)
  - Evidence: lexicon-coverage.test.mjs:19ff stages 'sec.a'…'field.*'…'rec.*', 'section', 'field', 'timeline', 'ladder_title', 'stock_note' in AFTER_FILL at 201a8ec; lib/i18n-app/es.ts at 201a8ec has 0 of these keys. Headless, locale es on the r.012 export: unit label 'Unidad' and Net 'Neto' are Spanish but the header reads 'Section', rows read 'A · Income', 'Income / Wages (take-home)', note in English (sofia-adv/r012-es-ladder-390.png); locale de: 'Einheit' above 'A · Income'. The fill landed later as 50e57c0 — r.012 as shipped is the mixed state.
  - Fix: Ship the fill with the keys (the r.004 pattern was a separate commit; for a surface the operator shows people, fill before ship) and keep the staged list visible on the record.
- **A tampered or corrupted record is read as EMPTY and the next entry overwrites it — the append-only invariant is broken by the recovery path** (MEDIUM — 'a deposit or withdrawal, once recorded, is on the record for good' (record.ts:10) does not hold on a device whose store was altered or half-written)
  - Evidence: record.ts:65 `return { rec: ok ? v : emptyRecord(owner), tampered: !ok }`; command-ux1.tsx:177 `commit` appends to that empty record and :70 `saveRecord` writes it over the old store. Node probe with a simulated localStorage: three good entries, one byte altered → load gives 0 entries, tampered true; one new deposit → store now holds 1 entry, a reload reads `tampered:false`, the three originals are gone from the device and the evidence of tampering with them. The 'chain broken' label (:368) only shows until the next reload.
  - Fix: Never overwrite a store that failed verify: keep the broken copy under a quarantine key, refuse new appends (or append to a new chain that records the broken hash as its genesis), and keep saying 'chain broken' from the stored fact, not from a React state.
- **Two identical transactions in the same second (or at the same typed stamp and amount) are silently dropped — append is idempotent on an id built from instant + amount, and the glass shows no refusal** (MEDIUM — a real second paycheck of the same amount at the same stamp cannot be recorded, and the person is not told)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:190 `id: \`d-${when}-${cents}\`` and :197 `id: \`w-${when}-${cents}\``; `when` falls back to `at`, the 1 Hz tick (:186, :195); record.ts:35 `if (rec.entries.some((e) => e.tx.id === tx.id)) return rec;`. Node probe: two appends with the same id → 1 entry, the same object returned; :177 `commit` sets no refusal and clears the form (:191) as if recorded.
  - Fix: Give an entry a unique id (counter or random) and let the chain carry duplicates honestly; or if idempotency is wanted, show a refusal sentence when append returns the same record.
- **Mars on the 'perihelion' anchor opens and closes its year at EARTH's perihelion, so a Mars revolution never reads 3600 — its A.B..C climbs to about 1903 and resets** (MEDIUM — the r.003/r.005 law is 'A.B..C IS the revolution, 3600 = one revolution of the selected planet'; for Mars the glass contradicts it every January)
  - Evidence: calendar.ts:98-101 derives `year` from Earth's civil year and `perihelionOf(year)` (the Earth table at :34) regardless of `yearDays`; `yearDays` (686.98 for Mars, planet-ltu.ts:54) only scales :111 `abc` and :112 `pastFull`. Node probe: 2026-09-30 on Mars → day 271, abc 1415.0187..2173, startMs === Earth's 2026 perihelion; 2027-01-02 → abc 1903.2565..1637; 2027-01-04 → day 1 again. The Mars capture (fleet-fin/shots/r012-portrait-mars-mot.png) shows axis marks 1419→1578 on that Earth-anchored year.
  - Fix: Anchor a planet's year to its own epoch (Mars perihelion table or a declared epoch in the LTU row) and let the year's length be the planet's revolution; say DECLARED until sourced.
- **The ladder is the operator's own sheet for everyone — a stranger sees someone else's Income 3,200 / Car 1,800 labelled 'Personal finance ladder', and the signed-in person's own record never touches it** (MEDIUM — perspective: the operator's figures are public on the export; the person's own deposits and withdrawals do not change the ladder; the timeline just recorded feeds nothing on the glass)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:138 `netLadder(SHEET_LINES, period)` — SHEET_LINES is the fixed seed (budget.ts:33-42, :77); the component never imports or calls `actualsByField` or `recurrenceDays` (import list :48; grep over the commit finds them only in ladder.ts, the CRS text and domain.gen.ts). So FIN-03.02's 'its $/min runs from its date for its timeline's length' is true only in a function no surface calls. The r.012 export carries the sheet's amounts in the HTML for any visitor.
  - Fix: Render actualsByField over the person's replayed record beside the plan (the FIN-06.06 column), and show the seed sheet only as an example badge for an unsigned visitor — the same honesty the clock already has (:253 example badge).
- **Mixed decimal precision inside one column: amounts under $1 print four decimals, amounts over print two, so 'per hour' reads $4.04 above $0.8838 above -$0.2525** (LOW — format consistency gate (CLAUDE.md release rule 8); tabular-nums cannot align digits that differ in count)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:141 `usdUnit = (cents) => (Math.abs(cents) >= 100 ? usd(Math.round(cents)) : usd4(cents))`. Headless rows_hour on the r.012 export: 'A · Income $4.04', 'B · Housing $0.8838', 'Net -$0.2525'; rows_day: '$96.97' beside 'H · Family $0.0000'. Capture sofia-adv/r012-portrait-ladder-hour.png.
  - Fix: Pick the precision per UNIT (2 decimals from per day upward, 4 below), not per value.
- **The glass offers a withdrawal under A · Income and a deposit under M · Transfers with no refusal; the picker's default list is A–M for both kinds** (LOW — the brief says Income fields are sign-positive; an outflow filed under Income corrupts I in any future actuals)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:161 both forms map FLOW_SECTIONS A–M unfiltered; :197 a withdrawal commits `field: wField` whatever section; ladder.ts:104 counts by field only (`amountNative > 0`), so a withdrawal tagged A.income_wages would be summed as income if actuals ever reached the glass. The fleet capture r012-ledger-dup-390.png shows exactly this: 'Withdrawal · Income / Wages (take-home) $1.00'.
  - Fix: Filter the section list by kind (deposit → A plus M-in; withdrawal → B–M) or carry a sign on the entry and refuse the mismatch.
- **Alcohol mapped to L prints its row under F while its total lands in L — the one-target rule is honoured in the sum and broken in the table** (LOW — latent: the glass never passes the `alcohol` argument (:138), so today it is unreachable; the moment a toggle appears the table misreports)
  - Evidence: ladder.ts:105 re-targets the sum; :60 keeps the field's section 'F'; command-ux1.tsx:355 places a field row by `fieldOf(l.fieldId)?.section`. Node probe with target L: fieldSection F, sumF 0, sumL 60.
  - Fix: Resolve the field's effective section in one helper used by both the sum and the table.
- **Two sections share one icon (Q · Net worth and R · Credit are both Gauge) and an unknown field id silently wears the Lifestyle icon** (LOW — FD-22's law 'every section its own stroke'; fixed in r.015, broken at r.012)
  - Evidence: category-icon.tsx:37 `Q: Gauge, R: Gauge`; command-ux1.tsx:180 `fieldOf(tx.field)?.section ?? "L"` — a field the record carries but the code no longer knows (a renamed slug) prints as Lifestyle rather than as unknown.
  - Fix: One stroke per section (done r.015); print an unknown field with a neutral icon and the raw id.
- **The year that closes short of the mean revolution never reads 3600 — 2026's perihelion interval is 364.39 d, so the glass reaches 3591 and resets; only the LONG case is flagged (pastFull)** (LOW — said on the record for long years, unsaid for short ones; the 'Equal' writings of a whole never appear on an actual Earth year)
  - Evidence: calendar.ts:112 `pastFull: elapsed > yearDays * MS_PER_DAY` (long side only); node probe: 2027-01-03T02:32Z → lengthDays 364.3875, abc 3591.1430..2054, pastFull false, day 365 'down'; 2028-01-05T12:27Z → 367.41 d, abc 3621.0787..0292, pastFull true.
  - Fix: Carry a `shortYear` flag or print the year's A.B..C against its actual length so a reader is told why 3600 never came.
- **The surface gate is a regex over source text; the behaviours that failed here (remount every second, double count, English in other languages, the missing view) all pass it** (LOW — process: 'every invariant gets a gate' is met in letter by 60 regexes, not in behaviour)
  - Evidence: tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:23-79 — e.g. :53 proves 'Net red when negative' by `/totals\.net < 0 \? "text-red-500"/`, :54 proves full width by matching the className string, :51 proves the pickers exist by matching `<select data-fin-section={hook}`. Only financial-ladder.test.mjs computes anything (and does it well).
  - Fix: Keep the regexes as smoke; add one headless behavioural gate per law: picker survives two ticks, Net equals the equation on the rendered DOM, every rendered string in locale es differs from English or is on the allow-list, every promised view resolves to a route.

**Keep**

- The model is the lock, and it is proven by arithmetic, not by grep: 20 sections A–T in order, 60 fields with the brief's labels verbatim, planes, U refused for N–T and never a section — ladder.ts:31-121; financial-ladder.test.mjs computes Net at all eight periods, the duplicate warning, alcohol, amortize and actuals (59 checks).
- toPeriod is one constant multiplier with no drift: my own round-trip over all 56 ordered period pairs on every sheet line gives a maximum error of 4.5e-13 dollars (ladder.ts:88).
- The unit dropdown is genuinely full width in portrait: measured 290 px select inside a 292 px panel (label 290 px) at 390×844, 224 px at its own width at 844×390, no horizontal scroll, zero page errors on the r.012 export.
- Thirteen section rows A–M render with their strokes, the sheet's fields beneath, Net last and red (−$200.00 per 33 days, −$0.0042 per minute, −$0.0001 per second) — the DTM figures of FIN-06.05 match the glass.
- Old entries are not rewritten: FinTx.field and recurrence are optional (accrual.ts:36-37) so r.006–r.011 hashes stand, and txWhat (command-ux1.tsx:179-183) prints the category for them.
- The record is honest about what is not built: FIN-06.06 is marked draft, the 90 keys are listed in AFTER_FILL rather than silently English, FD-25 says in words that the ladder's 365-day year supersedes FD-20's planet row, and the exact 365.259636-day revolution is kept apart from the ladder's 365 (ladder.ts:13-15).

**Report (verbatim)**

WHAT I READ AND HOW. All code from `git show 201a8ec:<path>`; the operator's ask with addenda 22–24; the v.000 record (budget.ladder_r012, revisions[0.012], FD-24/25/26, FIN-03.01/03.02/06.04/06.05/06.06); the two gates; the lexicon staging; the captures in scratchpad/shots (r011, r013) and the fleet's r012 captures. Headless: a prior fleet agent had built the r.012 tree at fleet-fin/r012tree (export stamped 'revision 0.012'); I served it and measured at 390×844 and 844×390 in en, es and de (sofia-adv/glass.mjs). Model: the r.012 libraries imported into node (sofia-adv/model.mjs). The remount class: the r.012 pattern re-created on React 18.3.1 and measured over 2.5 s (sofia-adv/remount-sofia.mjs). Note: frontend/out at HEAD is v0.016, not r.012 — I did not measure that one. The forms are behind sign-in, so the three pickers were read from code and from the fleet's signed-in capture, not clicked by me.

THE BRIEF'S VIEW. Acceptance line by line: 13 flow sections render and Net matches the equation at every time base — yes, in the model and on the DOM (my rows_hour/day/sec and the test's eight periods). Period switch is one constant with no drift — yes (4.5e-13). N–T visible only on a stock view — vacuously, there is no stock view; the ladder sentence says there is. U never a chip — yes. Labels exact — yes in English. Rule 5 'warn; do not double-count' — half: warns in a return value nobody renders, double-counts (ladder.ts:106/110; Net 760 not 880). Rule 6 alcohol one target — yes in the sum, no in the table when L is chosen (unreachable today). 'Model first, styling second' — the model got the care; the glass got a declaration-inside-render that remounts the pickers every second.

THE OPERATOR'S VIEW. He asked 'each transaction has an MoT timeline established at transaction entry … 30.333 days etc' and 'goal is to track $/min against budget'. r.012 records a timeline AND keeps the MoT field, defaulting them to different lengths (30.333 vs 33) on the same deposit including the seed example — two $/min for one paycheck, 8 % apart. Nothing on the glass reads the timeline; actualsByField exists only in ladder.ts. So the thing he wants to watch — his own $/min against the ladder — is not yet on the ladder; the ladder is his static sheet. And on his phone the section/field/timeline selects close every second (the defect he reported minutes later as addendum 26, fixed in r.014).

THE STRANGER'S VIEW. Arrives in Spanish: 'Unidad', 'Neto' translated; 'Section', 'A · Income', 'Income / Wages (take-home)' and the note in English (capture sofia-adv/r012-es-ladder-390.png). Sees a red Net of −$200 that is not theirs — the operator's sheet is the public default with no example badge on the ladder (the clock block has one, :253; the ladder does not). Reads 'live on the Balance view' and finds no such view. If they sign in and type two equal deposits in the same second, the second vanishes without a word.

THE AUDITOR'S VIEW. The chain is a 64-bit FNV, documented as tamper-evidence not a signature — fine. But the recovery path defeats it: a store that fails verify is read as empty and the next commit overwrites it; the originals and the evidence are gone and a reload says the chain is fine (proved in node). Append is idempotent on `kind-instant-amount`, so the record cannot hold two identical legitimate transactions. Gates: the ladder gate computes; the surface gate greps. Every defect above except the double count passes test:ci at 201a8ec.

THE CALENDAR. Perihelion anchor on Earth is right and sourced through 2035. Two edges: a short perihelion year (2026: 364.39 d) ends at 3591 and is not flagged (only the long side has pastFull); and Mars on the perihelion anchor is opened and closed by EARTH's perihelion (calendar.ts:98-101), so a Mars revolution never reads 3600 — it reaches ~1903 in January and resets to 0 (node probe; the Mars capture's axis 1419→1578 sits on that Earth-anchored year).

THE UNIT DROPDOWN. Measured, not grepped: 290 px of 292 px in portrait with the label on its own 290 px line; 224 px at its own width in landscape; no overflow. FD-24 holds for the unit picker. The three new pickers use `w-full` inside a one-column grid at 390 px (:156, :301) so they would be full width — when they stay mounted long enough to open.

GRADE C+. The lock is implemented and proven where it is pure; the surface that carries it to a hand is not yet usable on the phone (remount), is internally inconsistent (two lengths), deviates from one rule of the lock (double count), promises a view it lacks, and speaks English to thirty-two languages. The honesty of the record (draft, staged, superseded) keeps this from a C. What folds small into r.013: hoist the picker; one length per transaction; render the warning or net the duplicate; reword the note; fill before ship; quarantine a broken store instead of overwriting it; unique ids. What is bigger: actuals from the person's record on the ladder, and a Mars epoch of its own.

## Thoth

### Thoth · walk — grade B-

_Constructive walk — a stranger with the operator's sheet, on a phone, 60 seconds_

**One sentence:** The ladder's arithmetic is exact at every one of the eight periods (drift ≤ 4e-12 dollars, probed on the shipped module), but the glass rounds each cell on its own so the printed column does not foot, a default deposit is hashed with two contradictory lengths (30.333 and 33 days — two $/min for one paycheck), a repeated entry is dropped without a word, and three rules the brief locks (the duplicate-premium warning, the N–T Balance view, $/min against budget) live only in the model while the note under the table promises a view that is not there.

**Findings**

- **One deposit carries two lengths that disagree: MoT 30.333 days AND timeline 'Every 33 days', both hashed into the entry** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:145 defaults dMot to SRC.mot.payMotDays (30.333); :151 defaults dRec to "days33" (33 d); :190 commits BOTH motDays and recurrence into one FinTx, so the chain hash seals a contradiction. :85 seeds the EXAMPLE the same way (motDays 30.333 · recurrence "days33"). frontend/lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:125-126 — RECURRENCES has no 30.333 option at all, so the pay MoT cannot be chosen as a timeline. Probe (fleet-fin/thoth-walk-probe.mts): the paycheck's $/min via the timeline (33 d) = 0.07585; via accrual.ts motDays (30.333) = 0.08252 — 8.8% apart for the same entry. FD-26 says 'the timeline IS the transaction's MoT'; the code keeps two.
  - Fix: One length per entry. Either derive motDays from the chosen timeline (and add the pay MoT 91/3 to RECURRENCES, which r.013 later did as 'paymot'), or refuse an entry whose two lengths differ. Gate it in financial-surface: every committed FinTx has recurrenceDays(recurrence) === motDays or exactly one of the two.
- **A second entry with the same stamp and amount is silently dropped — 'Record it' clears the form and nothing lands** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/record.ts:34-35 append() returns the record unchanged when tx.id already exists. frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:190 builds id as `d-${when}-${cents}` and :197 `w-${when}-${cents}` — the field, the memo and the timeline are not in the id; :177 commit() never compares entries.length before and after and sets no refusal. Probe: two $200 withdrawals at the same typed stamp, one to L.fun_hobbies_clothing and one to F.groceries → entries 1 → 1, verify ok. A person paying two $200 bills on the same day loses one with no message, while the amount field blanks as if it worked.
  - Fix: Make the id unique (include field + a per-record nonce or the next rev) and, in commit(), treat an unchanged record as a refusal in the person's words ('already on the record for that day and amount'). Add a surface assert that append grows the record or the glass says why.
- **The printed column does not foot: mixed 2-dp / 4-dp cells, and a live line prints as $0.0000 per second** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:140-141 usdUnit prints 2 decimals at or above $1 and 4 decimals below, per cell. Per hour (r011-portrait.png, the same code path at r.012): rows foot to −0.2501 while Net prints −0.2525; per day −6.07 vs −6.06; per month (91) −551.53 vs −551.52; per year −2212.11 vs −2212.12 (probe table). Per second: G · Health (Fitness 50 / 33 d = $0.0000175/s) prints '$0.0000' — the glass probe on the export (fleet-fin/walk-ladder-per-second-390.png) shows the same; a non-zero line reads as nothing. Empty sections print '$0.0000' beside '$3,200.00' in one column (r013-ladder.png).
  - Fix: One precision per column, chosen from the smallest non-zero cell of the chosen unit (or always 4 dp below per day, 2 dp from per day up), and never print a non-zero amount as 0.0000 — fall to more decimals or to ¢/s. State under the table that Net is computed from unrounded amounts, or compute the printed Net from the printed rows. Gate: for every unit, parse the printed rows and check |foot − printed Net| ≤ half a unit in the last printed place.
- **On Mars with the A.B..C reveal, the chart subtitle reads Earth's revolution while the axis reads Mars'** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:447 calls positionInYear(from, planet.yearAnchor) and positionInYear(to, planet.yearAnchor) WITHOUT planet.yearDays; :433 (the axis) passes it. Computed from the shipped calendar.ts/mot.ts (fleet-fin/thoth-mars-probe.mts): for the example deposit instant, axis mark 0 = 1419.0722..1931 (Mars, 686.98 d) while the subtitle start = 2669.0833..0514 (Earth, 365.259636 d) — two A.B..C figures for one instant on one card. The r.008 correction ('the Mars axis read Earth positions') fixed the axis and missed the sibling line. The glass probe could not show it because the example is still pending (the subtitle takes the 'releases from' branch), which is also why no capture caught it.
  - Fix: Pass planet.yearDays at :447 exactly as at :433 (one helper `abcAt(ms)` used by both), and gate that the subtitle's first A.B..C equals axis mark 0 for every seeded planet.
- **Three locked rules exist in the model only; the note under the ladder promises a Balance view that does not exist at r.012** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:110 computes warnings DUPLICATE_INSURANCE, but command-ux1.tsx:138 reads only totals.sections and totals.net — `warnings` is never rendered, so FIN-06.05's 'a premium in both B and D warns' cannot happen on the glass. ladder.ts:134 actualsByField has no caller — the operator's '$/min against budget' is not visible anywhere. command-ux1.tsx:363 prints fin.stock_note (lexicon-data.ts:3088): 'Cash, investments, debts … live on the Balance view' — FIN-06.06 is DRAFT and no such view is on the page; a stranger scrolls looking for it.
  - Fix: Render totals.warnings as a row under Net (plain words: 'Housing insurance is entered in both Housing and Insurance — counted twice until you pick one'). Reword the note until r.013 lands ('…are kept off this ladder; a Balance view follows'). Do not claim $/min against budget in the record's FIN-03.02 statement until the actuals column exists.
- **The ladder is the operator's sheet for everyone — a signed-in stranger cannot change a number and their own transactions never touch it** (LOW)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:138 `netLadder(SHEET_LINES, period)` — SHEET_LINES (budget.ts:77) is constant seed data; the forms write to the record (accrual) but nothing reads the record into the ladder. In my 60 seconds the table reads as a personal budget, yet the only person it describes is the operator; there is no pencil, no way to enter Income 3,200 as my own. Addendum 28 / r.016 (in the working tree) answers this, so it is known — recorded here because it is the first thing a stranger would press.
  - Fix: Per-owner plan seeded from the sheet (what r.016 builds), and eventually the actuals column from the record beside it.
- **The 'pay MoT' has two values: 91/3 in the law, 30.333 in the record and on the glass — different B-tier digits** (LOW)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/mot.ts:59 MOT_PAY_MONTH_DAYS = 91/3 (30.3333…, documented as 298.3475..1826 A); docs/financial-2525/financial-2525.v.000.json:361 example.motDays = 30.333, which command-ux1.tsx:85 seeds — the chart prints 298.3463..2448 A (r007-earth-mot.png and the probe). Same name, two numbers, 29 seconds apart; the record's motInA 298.97 (json:368) rounds both so the drift hides.
  - Fix: One primitive: either the example's motDays reads MOT_PAY_MONTH_DAYS, or the record states that 30.333 is the bank screen's transcription and the MoT constant is 91/3, and the glass prints which.
- **Stale r.005 constants in the law's own comments contradict the live r.007 constant** (LOW)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/mot.ts:44 dangling docstring 'the financial year is exactly 365 days = 3600 A' directly above EARTH_REVOLUTION_DAYS = 365.259636 (:48); mot.ts:106 orbitUnits docstring 'Earth (365 d): A = 8,760 s … B = 2.4333 s' (live: 8,766.23 s); frontend/lib/financial-2525/calendar.ts:8 'The year is 365 days = 3600 A exactly'; frontend/lib/planet-ltu.ts:81 'Earth 9.8630' and :87 '8,760' (live: 9.8560 A/day). Thoth's rule is no drift — a reader of the source gets two answers.
  - Fix: Comments are not the record; update them to the live constant and cite r.007 where the correction is kept. A lint that greps the lib for '8,760' / '9.8630' / '365 days = 3600' would hold it.
- **Net paints green when ≥ 0 — a judgement colour the brief withholds** (LOW)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:360 `totals.net < 0 ? "text-red-500" : "text-green-500"`. The brief (addendum 22, 'What not to build'): no 'safe / proven / you can afford it' copy from Net; it asks only for red when Net < 0. Green is that copy without words.
  - Fix: Red when negative, the foreground colour otherwise.

**Keep**

- The locked Net = I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr holds at every one of the eight periods with the operator's sheet: −200 per 33 d, −0.2525/h, −0.0042/min, −2,212.12/y; drift against the 33-day Net × constant ≤ 4e-12 dollars; 33 d → minute → year equals the direct conversion (frontend/lib/financial-2525/ladder.ts:88 toPeriod is one constant, no rounding step).
