- The period table is the brief's own (7 · 33 · 91 · 365 d), and the record SAYS that the ladder's year is 365 d while the MoT whole stays 365.259636 d (FD-25) — a known difference stated, not hidden.
- The record is tamper-evident the honest way: two-lane FNV chain, verify() replays every link, an unreadable or altered store reads as empty AND says so (record.ts loadRecord), and the sentence under it names no vendor.
- The pickers are right for a phone: section → field as dependent dropdowns, full width in portrait, the section's icon beside the word; refusals in the person's words (amount, stamp, hold, insufficient).
- The A.B..C math itself is consistent where it is wired: 30.333 d on the exact revolution → 298.3463..2448 A, one A = 8,766.23 s, 9.8560 A per day — the header's '9.3081..2196' is the same number in base-3600 writing.

**Report (verbatim)**

PROVENANCE OF THIS WALK. Everything below is read from commit 201a8ec with git show. Three caveats the fleet should know: (1) the captures named r012-*.png do not exist in the shots folder — only r011-portrait/landscape (22:42) and r013-* (23:19) bracket r.012, so I read those and say which one I cite; (2) frontend/out was rebuilt under me — it stamped revision 0.016 by the time my probe ran — so the glass probe proves only code paths unchanged since r.012 (the diff shows usdDollars/usdUnit and the chart subtitle untouched); (3) the ladder math I verified by executing the r.012 ladder.ts, record.ts, mot.ts and calendar.ts directly under node type-stripping, in fleet-fin/, not through the export.

THE SIXTY SECONDS. I open /financial-2525 on a 390-px phone with the operator's sheet in my head: Income 3,200, Home 700, Car 1,800, Insurance 200, Electric 150, Food 300, Fitness 50, Fun 200 per 33 days.

0–10 s, the shell. Globe, three glyphs, FINANCIAL · 2525, 'the MoT Financial System · Version 000 · revision 0.012'. A card 'Money as time — this MoT' with a DEPOSIT pill and a five-step rail (DEPOSIT 1 · HOLD · RELEASE $0.00 · WITHDRAW 0 · RECORD 1). I understand it is a session-shaped thing about money; I do not yet know what a MoT is. The amber card says sign in, the example below is the operator's paycheck. Clear enough.

10–25 s, the clock and the chart. 'Released · Deposit' and a big $0.00 — the example deposit is dated 2026.10.01_07.00..00 and today is Sep 30, so everything is pending. The chart (r007-earth-mot.png) draws a green line rising and an orange line falling over 30.333 days with a yellow 3-hour tick: released versus escrow. The Clock icon reads '0 h 0 min elapsed'; the Orbit icon turns the header into '0.0000..0000 / 298.3463..2448 A-UNITS OF THE REVOLUTION' and the axis into five three-line numbers. As the math lens I can confirm each number; as a stranger I would press the Orbit icon once, see 2669.0833..0514, and press the Clock icon back. That is the intended behaviour and it works. Planet: Earth, Mars. On Mars with the reveal, the chart subtitle (line 447) would print Earth's A.B..C beside an axis in Mars' A.B..C — 2669 beside 1419 for the same instant — because that one call omits planet.yearDays while the axis call passes it. It did not show on the glass only because the example is still pending and the subtitle takes the 'releases from' branch.

25–35 s, the year line. 'Day 271 · Quarter 3 · 89/91 · 33-day frame 9 · 7/33', then '2026.09.30_… CST · 2026 · SOURCED · 271/365', then the perihelion in CST. I understand a day count and a quarter; I would not understand 'SOURCED' or why there is a second calendar, but it does not block me. One number I checked: the 2026 perihelion year is 364.387 days, so '/365' is the ceiling, never reached — said on the record, fine.

35–45 s, the forms. Gated behind sign-in, so I read them in code. Two panels, DEPOSIT and WITHDRAWAL, each with Section (A–M, icon) → Field (the section's fields only) → Timeline (One time · Weekly · Every 33 days · Monthly (91) · Yearly), full width. Good. But the deposit panel ALSO has 'Length (MoT)' defaulting to 30.333 while the timeline defaults to 'Every 33 days'. If I press Record it with both defaults, the entry sealed into the chain carries motDays 30.333 AND recurrence days33 — two lengths for one paycheck, and two $/min (0.0825 by the escrow law, 0.0759 by the timeline). The operator wrote 'each transaction has an MoT timeline established at transaction entry' and 'how long it lasts 30.333 days'; the timeline dropdown cannot say 30.333. This is the defect I would fix first, because it is at the entry point and every later actuals column inherits it. And if I pay two $200 bills on the same day and type the same stamp, the second is dropped with no message and the form blanks as if it took — append() is idempotent on an id made only of stamp and amount.

45–60 s, the ladder and the record. 'PERSONAL FINANCE LADDER A–M' with Unit 'per 33 days' (r013-ladder.png shows the table r.012 draws): A · Income $3,200.00 with 'Income / Wages (take-home)' beneath; B · Housing 700; C · Transportation 1,800; D · Insurance 200 as 'Auto / Renters / Home'; E 150; F 300; G · Health 50 as 'Mental Health / Physical Fitness'; H, I, J, K '$0.0000'; L · Lifestyle 200; M '$0.0000'; Net −$200.00 in red. The sheet is all there and every placement follows the brief. Three things a stranger notices: the empty sections say $0.0000 beside $3,200.00; this is the operator's budget and I cannot change a figure; and the note under it sends me to 'the Balance view' for cash, investments and debts — I scroll and there is none. Switching the unit: per hour gives Income $4.04, Mortgage $0.8838, … Net −$0.2525; if I add the printed rows with my phone's calculator I get −0.2501, not −0.2525, because Income was rounded to cents and the rest to four places. Per second, Fitness prints $0.0000 — a line I entered now reads as nothing. The arithmetic underneath is exact (I checked all eight periods; the worst drift is 4e-12 dollars); the printing is what loses the reader. 'per month (91)' shows Income $8,824.24 — the brief chose 91 and says so; a stranger will still think the month is 30 days, so the '(91)' in the label is doing all the work. The record: '2026.10.01_07.00..00 · Deposit · PAYROLL · $3,604.49 · 30.333' and 'On this phone only — a cloud copy follows once sign-in carries it'. That sentence is exactly right.

WHAT THE BRIEF PROMISES THAT I DO NOT SEE. '13 flow sections render; Net matches at every time base' — yes. 'No rounding drift beyond cents' — true in the model; on the glass the printed rows disagree with the printed Net by a cent at four of eight units and by more than a cent at per minute. 'If a premium is entered in both, warn' — the model warns, the glass never shows it. 'N–T visible only on stock/goals view' — there is no such view at r.012, and the table's note claims one. '$/min against budget' — actualsByField exists with no caller. 'U never appears as a category chip' — true, and there is no U entry either (planned).

GRADE. The model deserves an A: twenty sections, sixty exact labels, one conversion constant, the Net equation proven at every period, U refusing N–T, a chain that verifies. The glass at r.012 is a C+: per-cell rounding that does not foot, a zero that is not zero, a promise of a view that is not there, a warning that cannot fire, a Mars subtitle on the wrong revolution, and — the two that touch the data itself — a default entry hashed with two lengths and a duplicate dropped in silence. B− overall, with the two HIGH items the ones I would not ship r.013 without, and the footing rule the one Thoth would write as a gate: parse what the glass prints and add it up.

### Thoth · adversarial — grade C-

_Adversarial pass (B) on Financial-2525 v.000_r.012, commit 201a8ec, read-only_

**One sentence:** The pure ladder model is right to the twelfth decimal — Net = I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr holds at all eight periods, the factors are the brief's and the round trip drifts 9e-13 $ — but the glass that carries it to the operator's thumb remounts its three pickers every second, gives one deposit two disagreeing lengths (30.333 d and 33 d, 8.08 % apart), destroys a flagged record on the next commit, prints Earth's coordinate and Earth's provenance on Mars, never shows the duplicate-insurance warning it computes, and reads English in 32 languages.

**Findings**

- **The three pickers (section · field · timeline) are a component declared inside the render function; the 1 Hz clock remounts them every second, so a dropdown closes before the person can pick** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: 201a8ec:frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:157 `const LadderPicker = ({…}) => (…)` is defined INSIDE `FinancialCommandUX1`; it is rendered as a JSX type at :306 and :315; :95 `setInterval(() => setNow(Date.now()), 1000)` re-renders the component every second, and a fresh function identity is a new element type to React, so the three `<select>`s unmount and mount once a second. The operator reported exactly this on the r.011 edition (ask addendum 26, 'this drop down goes away quick!') and the record names `LadderPicker` in r.012 as the same class (reading item 34, HEAD ask :451-454). The surface gate (tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:51) only asserts the selects EXIST by regex; nothing asserts they stay mounted.
  - Fix: Hoist `LadderPicker` (and any picker) to module level with props only; add a behavioural gate that opens a select, waits ≥ 2 s and asserts the same DOM node is still mounted and open (r.014's picker law — but the gate must be behavioural, not a regex on 'module-level').
- **A record flagged as tampered reads as EMPTY, and the next commit overwrites the device store with a one-entry record — three real entries vanish because one byte was off** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: 201a8ec:frontend/lib/financial-2525/record.ts:63-66 `loadRecord` returns `emptyRecord(owner)` with `tampered: true`; command-ux1.tsx:114 puts that empty record in state; :177 `commit` = `append(record, tx, at)` then `saveRecord(next)` — the store is replaced. Scratchpad probe /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-eXeL-AI-Polling/d1ee5405-1233-5ea7-aa59-4ce83334aed8/scratchpad/fleet-fin/thoth-b/probe-record.mjs: a 3-entry record with `5000` → `5001` in the raw store reads 0 entries / tampered; after one more deposit the store holds 1 entry and `verify` of the new store is `{ok:true}` — the earlier history is gone and the glass still says 'chain broken'. This is the class CLAUDE.md names ('a completed record is never discarded').
  - Fix: On `tampered`, keep the raw store as a quarantined copy under `exel-fin:<owner>:broken-<ts>` and REFUSE to write over it (append onto the longest verified prefix, or say 'the record on this phone is damaged; export it' — the signer-voice sentence); gate it: a tamper fixture must survive a commit byte-for-byte.
- **One deposit carries two lengths that disagree: motDays (30.333, the escrow's $/min) and recurrence (days33 = 33 d, the ladder's timeline); the timeline dropdown cannot express the operator's own 30.333-day paycheck, and nothing on the glass reads the recurrence** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:145 `dMot` defaults to `SRC.mot.payMotDays` (30.333) and :151 `dRec` defaults to `"days33"`; :85 the EXAMPLE itself carries `motDays: 30.333, recurrence: "days33"`; :190 both are committed. ladder.ts `recurrenceDays` maps the five timelines to 0 · 7 · 33 · 91 · 365 — no 30.333. The escrow rate is accrual.ts `amountCents / (motDays × 1440)` = 8.2520 ¢/min; the ladder's rate for the same deposit, `ratePerMinute(360449, 33)`, = 7.5852 ¢/min — 8.08 % apart (probe-math.mjs). `grep warnings|actualsByField|recurrenceDays|ratePerMinute` in command-ux1.tsx at 201a8ec returns nothing: the timeline is written into the hash and never used. The operator asked for ONE timeline per transaction ('how long it last 30.333 days etc').
  - Fix: One length per transaction: the timeline dropdown offers the pay MoT (30.333) and an 'Other (days)' entry, and `motDays` is DERIVED from it (or the reverse) — never two fields with two defaults; a gate asserts `recurrenceDays(tx.recurrence) === tx.motDays` for every committed deposit, and the actuals column (FIN-06.06) consumes it.
- **The chart subtitle's A.B..C range reads Earth's revolution on Mars (positionInYear called without planet.yearDays) while the axis three lines below reads Mars' — two coordinates for one instant on one card; the surface gate's regex misses the call** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:447 `fmtMot(positionInYear(from, planet.yearAnchor).abc)} → ${fmtMot(positionInYear(to, planet.yearAnchor).abc)}` — third argument absent, so `yearDays` defaults to 365.259636 (calendar.ts:91); :433 passes `planet.yearDays`. Pure replay for the example's instant (probe-math.mjs): subtitle 2669.0833..0514 (Earth) vs axis mark 0 = 1419.0722..1931 (Mars). The headless capture at 18:35 CST showed the 'Pending — releases from' branch because the example opens 07:00 CST Oct 1; after that instant the glass prints the two numbers side by side. tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:38 asserts 'every year position on the glass takes the selected planet's revolution' with a regex on the `from + f * len` call only — it passes on 201a8ec while :447 does not.
  - Fix: Pass `planet.yearDays` at :447 (both calls); replace the regex gate with a source assertion that EVERY `positionInYear(` call site in the component carries three arguments, or wrap it in one helper `posOf(ms)` so there is a single call.
- **On Mars the year panel prints Earth's perihelion provenance and Earth's year length beside a Mars A.B..C: '2026 · SOURCED · … 1416.1767..1682' over a note that says 'declared, not sourced'** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: calendar.ts:98-101 the perihelion anchor always reads Earth's `PERIHELION` table (`perihelionOf(year)`) whatever `yearDays` is; :109 `status` and :113 `lengthDays` come from that table; :111 only the abc uses `yearDays`. Measured on the r.012 export at 390×844 with Mars selected (probe-glass.out.json → mars.year): 'Day 271 · Quarter 3 · 89/91 … 2026 · SOURCED · 1416.1767..1682' with the anchor note 'no perihelion table for this planet yet — declared, not sourced' directly under it. So day 271 is Earth-days since EARTH's perihelion, the A-units are of MARS' revolution (271/686.98 × 3600), and the status word is wrong. Addendum 12: 'remember 3600 units for mars from perihelion to perihelion' — Mars' perihelion is not Earth's.
  - Fix: Give `positionInYear` the planet's own epoch (a `perihelionOf(planet, year)` or a DECLARED Mars perihelion row with status DECLARED) and derive `status`, `lengthDays`, day and quarter from the planet's whole; the glass prints one provenance word per planet, never two.
- **Rule 5 half-built: the DUPLICATE_INSURANCE warning is computed and never rendered, Net still counts both premiums, and the trigger fires on the normal household (housing insurance in B plus auto insurance in D)** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: ladder.ts `netLadder` pushes `"DUPLICATE_INSURANCE"` when both `B.housing_insurance` and `D.auto_renters_home` are present and nets both (probe: 120 + 120 → net −240 with the warning); command-ux1.tsx never reads `totals.warnings` (grep = 0 hits) — the ladder table at :352-360 prints sections and Net only. The brief: 'If a premium is entered in both, warn; do not double-count Net.' The test (financial-ladder.test.mjs:51) asserts only that the model's string exists. And a renter with B·housing insurance and D·auto insurance — the ordinary case, since D's field is 'Auto / Renters / Home' — would always be warned.
  - Fix: Render `totals.warnings` on the ladder (one amber line under D naming both fields); decide the double-count rule explicitly (count once, name which) and gate it on Net, not on the string; make the trigger amount-aware or per-policy so the normal case is silent.
- **The ladder's actuals are blind to the transaction's kind: a withdrawal filed under A·Income counts as income, a deposit filed under B·Rent counts as rent — and both forms offer every section A–M** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: ladder.ts `actualsByField(txs)` takes `{fieldId, amount, atMs, days}` with no kind or sign (probe: a 500 'withdrawal' under A.income_wages → +500 income; a negative amount passes through unrefused). command-ux1.tsx:161 both `hook="deposit"` and `hook="withdrawal"` map `FLOW_SECTIONS` (A–M) into the section picker; the withdrawal default is B and the deposit default is A, but nothing stops the swap. The brief: 'Income fields: kind = Income, sign positive' — the sign is the kind's, not the person's.
  - Fix: Derive sign from `FinTx.kind` × `Field.kind` (a withdrawal on an Income field and a deposit on a Fixed/Variable/Transfer field are refused or recorded as corrections), and show a deposit only A (plus M for a transfer-in) and a withdrawal only B–M; gate it.
- **Two transactions with the same stamp and amount collapse to one id and the second is dropped silently — the form clears as if it were recorded** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:190 `id: \`d-${when}-${cents}\`` and :197 `id: \`w-${when}-${cents}\``; record.ts:35 `append` returns the SAME record when the id exists (idempotent by design); :177 `commit` does not check whether anything was appended; :191 `setDAmt("")` runs regardless. Probe (probe-math.mjs): a second $50 entry with the same stamp but a different field (C.fuel vs F.groceries) → entries 1, record object identical. A person who types the same deposit stamp twice (the placeholder is the current time, which people copy) loses the second one with no refusal.
  - Fix: Put a per-record sequence or the field in the id (`d-${when}-${cents}-${rev+1}`), and have `commit` refuse with a sentence when `append` returns the same record.
- **Ninety staged lexicon keys read English in the other 32 languages: the ladder title, 'SECTION', all thirteen section names, every field label, the timeline words and the N–T note** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: 201a8ec:frontend/tests/lexicon-coverage.test.mjs:28 stages `fin.sec.*` (20), `fin.field.*` (60), `fin.rec.*` (5), `section`, `field`, `timeline`, `ladder_title`, `stock_note` in AFTER_FILL; `git show 201a8ec:frontend/lib/i18n-app/es.ts | grep -c 'fin.sec.|fin.field.|fin.rec.'` = 0. Measured on the r.012 export with locale `es` (probe-glass.out.json → es; capture thoth-r012-ladder-es.png): 'PERSONAL FINANCE LADDER A–M', 'SECTION', 'A · Income' … 'M · Transfers', 'Income / Wages (take-home)', and the N–T note are English beside 'Unidad', 'por 33 días', 'Neto'. The commit says so ('STAGED … listed, never silent'); the operator's rule is all UX in all 33 languages. The t() fallback (lexicon-context.tsx:224) makes this silent on the glass.
  - Fix: Ship the ×32 fill as the next commit (the r.004 pattern) before any further surface work, and keep `fin.*` staged keys out of a release stamp that claims 'all 33'.
- **One column, two precisions: '$0.0000' for an empty section beside '$700.00'; at per hour '$4.04' beside '$0.8838'; the printed rows differ from the printed Net by one cent at day, month and year** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:141 `usdUnit = cents >= 100 ? usd(round) : usd4` switches format per CELL, not per column. Measured on the export (probe-glass.out.json → units): per 33 days sections H, I, J, K, M print '$0.0000' while A prints '$3,200.00' (capture thoth-r012-ladder-portrait.png); per hour A '$4.04', B '$0.8838', Net '-$0.2525'. probe-math.mjs: per day printed rows sum to −6.07 vs printed Net −6.06; month91 −551.53 vs −551.52; year −2212.11 vs −2212.12 (the brief's 'no drift beyond cents' holds — the drift is exactly one cent, from rounding rows and the sum separately). Also 'PER 33 DAYS' wraps to two header lines at 390 px.
  - Fix: Pick the precision per COLUMN from the unit (4 decimals for second/minute, 2 for hour and up, 0 cents never as '$0.0000') and print Net from the rounded rows OR mark it as the exact figure; CLAUDE.md rule 8 (unit/format consistency) wants one format per display.
- **The stamp parser accepts impossible dates and rolls them forward silently: 2026.02.31 becomes 2026.03.03, 2026.04.31 becomes 2026.05.01** (LOW)
  - Evidence: mot.ts:166 checks `d > 31` only; :154 `Date.UTC(y, mo-1, d …)` rolls over. probe-record.mjs: `parseStampCST("2026.02.31_00.00..00")` → 2026.03.03_00.00..00. A typed deposit date lands on a different day with no refusal; the record then carries the wrong day forever (append-only).
  - Fix: Round-trip the parsed instant through `cstParts` and refuse when y/mo/d do not come back identical.
- **Dollar-to-cent conversion rounds through binary floats: 1.005 → 100 cents, 2.675 → 268; '1,000' and '$5' are refused as NaN** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:185 `Math.round(Number(dAmt) * 100)`; probe-record.mjs prints the cases. Half-up on the typed decimal would give 101 and 268 — the second is right by luck, the first is wrong.
  - Fix: Parse the typed string as integer cents (split on the decimal point, pad to two digits) and strip thousands separators and a leading currency sign before refusing.
- **Alcohol mapped to L would count under L while its row stays listed under F — the table's section total would not equal the fields beneath it (latent: the glass never passes an alcohol target)** (LOW)
  - Evidence: ladder.ts `netLadder(lines, period, alcohol)` moves `F.alcohol` into `sections[L]` (probe: F 0 · L 60) but `fieldOf("F.alcohol").section` stays F; command-ux1.tsx:355 groups field rows by `fieldOf(l.fieldId)?.section`, and :138 calls `netLadder(SHEET_LINES, period)` with no target, so only F is reachable in r.012.
  - Fix: When the target is L, present the row under L (resolve the row's section through the same `alcoholSection`), and expose the one-target choice on the detail sheet with a gate that section rows always equal the sum of the rows beneath them.
- **Small model gaps: the brief's own example `mapsTo: "B.property_tax"` is refused by `amortize` (the id is `B.property_tax_hoa_fees`); a negative amortized amount is accepted; Q and R share one stroke; the unsigned record line prints the example without its field while the roster prints it** (LOW)
  - Evidence: probe-math.mjs: `amortize({amount:1200, periodDays:365, mapsTo:"B.property_tax"})` → `{refused:"NOT_A_FLOW_FIELD"}`; `amount:-1200` → −108.49 into B.rent_mortgage. category-icon.tsx:37 `Q: Gauge, R: Gauge` (two sections, one icon — corrected in r.015). command-ux1.tsx:370 prints `EXAMPLE.memo` with no `txWhat`, :215 prints the field on the roster.
  - Fix: Accept a documented alias table for the brief's short ids (or state the id grammar on the record); refuse `amount <= 0` in `amortize`; one stroke per section (done r.015); one `txWhat` for every record line.
- **'CHAIN VERIFIED' on the glass claims more than FNV-1a proves: a whole forged chain verifies, and the forge needs only the same JavaScript the page ships** (LOW)
  - Evidence: record.ts:24-30 (two 32-bit FNV lanes, 'a tamper-evidence chain for a phone, not a signature' — said in code, not on the glass); probe-math.mjs: a freshly built record of one 999,999-cent deposit → `verify` `{ok:true}`. command-ux1.tsx:368 prints `fin.chain_ok` / `fin.chain_broken` with no qualifier.
  - Fix: Word the glass as what it is ('entries link; this phone has not altered them') until the cloud half signs; keep the hash, add the sentence.
- **The gates that defend this surface pass on regexes, not behaviour: picker presence (:51), portrait width (:54), planet yearDays (:38), warnings (ladder :51) — none of them exercised the four HIGH/MEDIUM failures above** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: 201a8ec:frontend/tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:51 regex on `<select data-fin-section=` (the selects remount every second and the gate is green); :54 regex on the class string `w-full … landscape:w-auto` (the width is asserted by a class name — the r.012 export measures 290 of 292 px portrait and 224 of 670 px landscape, so it HAPPENS to hold, but the gate would stay green if Tailwind dropped the class); :38 regex on one `positionInYear(from + f * len, …, planet.yearDays)` call while :447 three lines away omits `yearDays`; financial-ladder.test.mjs:51 asserts the warning string in the model, nothing asserts it reaches the glass; no gate asserts `recurrence` ≡ `motDays`, that `actualsByField` has a consumer, or that a tampered record survives a commit.
  - Fix: Add one headless gate (the r012-shots harness already serves out/) that: opens a picker and holds it 2 s; measures the select width against the panel; selects Mars, reveals A.B..C after the example's open instant and asserts subtitle start === axis mark 0; loads a tamper fixture, commits once and diffs the store. Keep the regex gates as linters, not as proof.

**Keep**

- The Net equation holds at every one of the eight periods: for the operator's sheet, Net(p) = −200 × periodFactor(days33, p) exactly (−0.004208754 per minute; −2,212.1212 per year), and Income, living, Ds, Tx, Tr each enter once (probe-math.mjs on 201a8ec's ladder.ts).
- toPeriod is one constant and drifts nowhere: the worst round trip over all 64 period pairs for $3,604.49 is 9.1e-13 $; eleven odd-cent lines through per-minute and back agree to 1e-9 (financial-ladder.test.mjs:44-46 and my probe).
- The factors are the brief's, not a 30-day month: second 1 · 60 · 3,600 · 86,400 · 7 d · 33 d · 91 d · 365 d (ladder.ts PERIOD_SECONDS), and the record SAYS that the ladder's 365-day 'year' is not the MoT's 365.259636-day revolution (ladder.ts header; FD-25) — two constants, both declared, neither hidden.
- A balance in P never nets against I (payment ≠ balance): `netLadder` ignores non-flow fields, unknown ids and negative amounts (test :41-42; probe).
- U is a rule, not a chip: `amortize` refuses N–T and U.anything and a zero period; no section U exists; the sixty labels match the brief character for character (20 sections, 46 flow fields, per-section counts A5 B6 C5 D4 E4 F3 G4 H2 I2 J3 K2 L3 M3 N1 O3 P2 Q2 R1 S2 T3).
- Old entries keep their hashes: a tx with `field: undefined, recurrence: undefined` hashes identically to one without the keys (stableJson drops undefined), so every r.006–r.011 record replays unchanged — proven, not asserted.
- The unit dropdown measures full width in portrait on the r.012 export: 290 of 292 px inner panel width at 390×844, and 224 of 670 px at its own width in landscape; no horizontal scroll (scrollWidth 390 = viewport); zero page errors in either orientation.
- The balance clock ladder reads the planet's LTU row (command-ux1.tsx:269 `planet.hoursPerDay * planet.minPerHour`), not a typed 1440 — the r.005 law held where the gate looked.
- The MoT constants reconcile: one A = 8,766.23 s; the pay MoT 30.333 d = 298.3475..1826 A; 33 d = 325.0893..0483 A; `A_PER.day` = 9.8560 (the comment in planet-ltu.ts:81 that says 9.8630 is stale by 0.007 — the code is right).

**Report (verbatim)**

THOTH — ADVERSARIAL PASS ON FINANCIAL-2525 v.000_r.012 (commit 201a8ec), read from the commit, never the working tree.

WHAT I DID. Read ladder.ts, budget.ts, accrual.ts, record.ts, mot.ts, calendar.ts, planets.ts, command-ux1.tsx, category-icon.tsx, the ask with addendum 22 (the lock), the record JSON (FIN-06.05, FIN-03.02, FIN-06.06 draft, FD-25, FD-26), the gates (financial-ladder, financial-surface, financial-i18n, lexicon-coverage AFTER_FILL). The export in frontend/out is r.016, not r.012, so I rebuilt r.012 from `git archive 201a8ec frontend` inside the scratchpad (/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-eXeL-AI-Polling/d1ee5405-1233-5ea7-aa59-4ce83334aed8/scratchpad/fleet-fin/thoth-b/wt/frontend/out, stamp v.000_r.012) and probed it headless at 390×844 and 844×390 (probe-glass.mjs → probe-glass.out.json; captures thoth-r012-*.png). Pure-library probes: probe-math.mjs, probe-record.mjs in the same folder. Nothing in the repository was touched.

THE MATH THAT HOLDS. Net = I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr at every period, to 1e-12; the factors are the brief's; the round trip drifts 9e-13 $; P never nets; U refuses N–T; sixty labels exact; old hashes unchanged. The model deserves an A−. The lock's five acceptance lines pass (13 sections render; Net matches at every base; a switch is one constant; U never a chip; labels exact), with 'N–T visible only on the stock view' passing only because no stock view exists yet (declared r.013).

WHERE IT BREAKS — the glass, the record, Mars, the languages.
1. HIGH · The pickers close themselves. `LadderPicker` is declared inside the render function (:157) and the 1 Hz clock (:95) re-creates it every second; React remounts the three selects. The operator found this with his thumb (addendum 26: 'this drop down goes away quick!'). The gate (:51) checks the selects exist by regex, so it is green while the person cannot pick.
2. HIGH · A flagged record is destroyed on the next commit. `loadRecord` returns EMPTY on tamper (record.ts:63-66), the glass holds that empty record (:114), `commit` appends to it and `saveRecord` overwrites the store (:177). Probe: three good entries with one byte off → 0 entries read → one deposit later the store holds 1 entry and verifies clean. This is the class CLAUDE.md wrote down after the three dead ends: nothing guaranteed the finished record survived a failure.
3. HIGH · Two lengths per deposit. `motDays` 30.333 drives the escrow's $/min (8.2520 ¢/min); `recurrence` 'days33' is the ladder's timeline (7.5852 ¢/min) — 8.08 % apart on the EXAMPLE itself (:85), on both defaults (:145, :151), and the timeline dropdown has no 30.333. Nothing on the glass reads `recurrence`, `recurrenceDays`, `actualsByField` or `ratePerMinute` (grep = 0). The operator asked for ONE timeline per transaction so $/min runs from its date for its length.
4. MEDIUM · Mars reads Earth. :447 calls `positionInYear(from, planet.yearAnchor)` without `planet.yearDays` while :433 passes it: subtitle 2669.0833..0514 vs axis 1419.0722..1931 for the same instant (pure replay; the glass showed the 'Pending' branch because the example opens 07:00 CST Oct 1). The year panel with Mars selected prints '2026 · SOURCED · 1416.1767..1682' above a note saying 'declared, not sourced' (measured) — day 271 is Earth-days since Earth's perihelion (calendar.ts:98-113). The gate at :38 claims 'every year position takes the planet's revolution' and regexes one call.
5. MEDIUM · Rule 5 is half-built: the warning is computed and never rendered; Net counts both; the trigger would fire on the ordinary household (B housing + D auto).
6. MEDIUM · Kind-blind actuals: a withdrawal under A counts as income; both forms offer A–M.
7. MEDIUM · Same stamp + amount = same id → silently dropped; the form clears as if recorded.
8. MEDIUM · Ninety keys English in 32 languages — measured in Spanish: the ladder title, 'SECTION', all thirteen section names, every field label and the N–T note are English beside 'Unidad', 'por 33 días', 'Neto'. Staged, listed, honest in the commit — and still English on a phone set to Spanish.
9. LOW · Format mix in one column ('$0.0000' beside '$700.00'; '$4.04' beside '$0.8838'); printed rows vs printed Net differ by one cent at day/month/year (inside the brief's tolerance, but the display should say which it prints); stamp rollover (02.31 → 03.03); float cents (1.005 → 100); alcohol-to-L latent table split; the brief's own `B.property_tax` example refused by `amortize`; Q and R one stroke; 'CHAIN VERIFIED' claims more than two FNV lanes prove.

THE GATES. financial-ladder.test.mjs is a real gate on the model. financial-surface.test.mjs is a linter wearing a gate's badge: picker presence, portrait width and planet yearDays are all regexes on source text, and three of the four largest defects live one line away from a green regex. The r012-shots harness already serves out/; it should become the gate — open a picker and hold it two seconds, measure the select, select Mars and compare subtitle to axis after the example opens, load a tamper fixture and commit once.

GRADE C−. The model is the lock and it holds. The surface that carries it to the operator's own finances cannot take a pick, can erase his record, tells two lengths for one paycheck and two provenances for one planet, and speaks English to 32 languages. Fix the record first (it is the only one that loses data), the picker second (it is the one he hit), the single timeline third (it is the ask), then make the gates behavioural so none of the four can return green.

_(did not return)_

## Thor

### Thor · walk — grade C

_CONSTRUCTIVE WALK — a stranger with the operator's own sheet, on a 390×844 phone, 60 seconds on /financial-2525 at v.000_r.012 (commit 201a8ec)_

**One sentence:** The glass speaks honestly and the ladder math is a clean constant, but the record Thor guards can be frozen by a long number in the MoT box, double-spent by a withdrawal dated tomorrow, and wiped by its own next entry after a tamper — three breaks of the invariants the code itself writes down.

**Findings**

- **A long MoT number freezes the phone tab (render loop never ends)** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:190 stores `motDays: Math.max(0, Number(dMot) || 0)` with no finiteness check; 309+ digits (or `1e400`, or any motDays ≥ ~2.1e300) make `Number()` Infinity. command-ux1.tsx:420 `len = Math.max(1, motDays * dayMs)` = Infinity, :424 `series(..., from, to, len/120)` with to = step = Infinity, and accrual.ts:127 `for (let t = fromMs; t <= toMs; t += stepMs)` never terminates (proved: fleet-fin/thor-walk/hang.mjs — 1e6 iterations, t stuck at Infinity). Because commit() saves before React re-renders (:177), the entry is already on the append-only record with motDays serialised as null, so after the forced reload a deposit that releases 'at once' sits on the chain for good.
  - Fix: Refuse non-finite or out-of-range amounts and MoT at the form (`Number.isFinite(x) && x <= CAP`) with a reason line, and guard `series()` with `if (!isFinite(toMs) || !isFinite(stepMs)) return out` so no record can ever hang the render; add both to the invariant gate.
- **A withdrawal dated in the future lets the same money be withdrawn twice — the stated invariant is broken and the clamp hides it** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: accrual.ts:116 `validateWithdrawal` checks the balance only at `w.atMs`; command-ux1.tsx:195 lets a person type any stamp. Probe (a) in fleet-fin/thor-walk/probe.out: deposit $1,000 fully released; W1 $1,000 dated +10 days → ok; W2 $1,000 dated now → ok (W1 is not counted at now, accrual.ts:96 `x.atMs <= t`); at W1's date withdrawn = $2,000 against $1,000 withdrawable, and accrual.ts:106 `Math.max(0, …)` prints available $0 instead of −$1,000. accrual.ts:15-16 promises 'a withdrawal never makes the available balance negative'. The same class: a withdrawal back-dated before an existing one.
  - Fix: Validate the whole record, not one instant: after inserting w, recompute the balance at every withdrawal instant ≥ w.atMs (and at now) and refuse if any goes negative; or forbid withdrawal stamps after `now`. Gate it with a replay test that inserts out of order.
- **A tampered record is replaced, not preserved — the next entry overwrites the evidence and every earlier deposit** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: record.ts:63-66 `loadRecord` returns `emptyRecord(owner)` with tampered=true; command-ux1.tsx:114 puts that empty record in state; :177 `commit` appends to it and `saveRecord(next)` (record.ts:70) writes it over the stored key. Probe (d): two deposits saved, one byte altered, load → 0 entries/tampered; one new deposit → the store now holds one entry (d3); the two deposits and the tamper evidence are gone from the device. The lexicon line (lexicon-data.ts:2952) promises 'shown empty, never repaired silently' — the glass shows 'chain broken' once, then the next tap silently repairs by erasure, while the forms stay enabled.
  - Fix: When tampered, refuse writes (disable both buttons, say why in one sentence) and quarantine the broken bytes under `exel-fin:<owner>:broken-<stamp>` before anything else is written; add a gate that a tampered load followed by append+save never shrinks the stored byte length.
- **An infinite amount is accepted, prints '$∞', and persists as null on the chain** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:185 `Math.round(Number(dAmt) * 100)` → Infinity for `1e400` or 309+ digits; :187 `!(cents > 0)` passes; probe (b): released shows Infinity (usd → '$∞'), JSON stores amountCents null, verify still ok after reload (stableJson wrote null both times, record.ts:23), the deposit then reads $0. The id `d-<when>-Infinity` is on the record forever.
  - Fix: Same finiteness/cap guard as the MoT; reject at the form with `fin.reason_amount`.
- **'Chain verified' overclaims — a re-chained forgery reads verified** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: record.ts:24-30 FNV-1a with no secret (the comment admits 'a tamper-evidence chain for a phone, not a signature'); probe (e): one entry rewritten to $999,999.99 and re-hashed from GENESIS loads with tampered=false and the title at command-ux1.tsx:368 prints 'Chain verified' (lexicon-data.ts:2951). The gate (financial-surface.test.mjs:58) only checks that the functions are called.
  - Fix: Say what is true on the glass ('links intact on this phone') until a key-bound signature exists; keep 'verified' for the cloud half the device_only line promises.
- **Two deposits with the same stamp and amount: the second is dropped silently and the form clears as if recorded** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:190 id = `d-${when}-${cents}`; record.ts:35 `append` returns the same record when the id exists; :191 clears the fields regardless. Probe (c): entries stay at 1, no refusal. Reachable by typing a stamp (two $300 groceries on 2026.10.01_18.00..00). The withdrawal id (:197) has the same shape.
  - Fix: Include the next rev (or a per-record counter) in the id, or make append return a refusal the form shows via `setRefusal`.
- **A deposit's refusal is printed under the OTHER form, off-screen on a phone** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:187-188 set `refusal` for the deposit; the only render is :318 inside `#fin-withdraw-form` (:310-320), ~400 px below the 'Record it' button at 390 px. A stranger taps Record it with an empty amount and sees nothing happen. `saveFailed` (:319) has the same placement and never resets. (r.013 merged the forms, so this member of the class is likely moot now — the 'refusal lives where the tap was' rule still needs a gate.)
  - Fix: Render the refusal and the save warning directly under the button that was pressed; reset saveFailed on the next successful save.
- **The brief's promises a stranger does not see: the warning, N–T, U, the detail sheet, actuals — and transactions never reach the ladder** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: ladder.ts:110 computes `warnings` (DUPLICATE_INSURANCE) and :134 `actualsByField`, :117 `amortize`, :83 `alcoholSection` — none is referenced in command-ux1.tsx (grep: no hit). The ladder (:352-360) renders only the hard-coded SHEET_LINES (budget.ts:77); the record's `field`/`recurrence` (:190, :197) are stored and printed (:180) but never summed against the ladder, so Net does not move when I record Rent. N–T is a sentence (:363). The record JSON marks FIN-06.06 'draft' honestly; the glass should say the same next to the ladder.
  - Fix: Until r.013+ lands: one line under the ladder naming what is not yet on it (actuals, Balance view, U) so the reader is not left to guess; then wire actualsByField over the chosen unit's window as the column the operator asked for.
- **Sign-out leaves the money record on a shared phone with no way to erase it** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:323 logout only redirects; record.ts:69 writes `exel-fin:<sub>` plaintext in localStorage; nothing in the tree clears it (git grep exel-fin: only record.ts). Any page on the origin, or anyone holding the phone, can read every entry.
  - Fix: Offer 'Remove this record from this phone' beside Sign out (writes a tombstone, keeps the hash of what was removed), and say on the device_only line that signing out does not remove it.
- **Malformed dates are accepted and silently moved** (LOW)
  - Evidence: mot.ts:166 allows day ≤ 31 for any month; probe (f): '2026.02.31_07.00..00' becomes 2026.03.03; '0026.10.01_07.00..00' becomes 1926 (Date.UTC two-digit-year rule at :154). The entry then sits on the chain with a date the person did not type.
  - Fix: Round-trip the parsed instant through cstParts and refuse when it does not equal the typed parts; require a four-digit year ≥ 1970.
- **The roster calls the operator's example paycheck 'you' to a stranger** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:215 `me: true` for every deposit including EXAMPLE (:83-86); the capture shows 'PAYROLL · PENDING DIRECT DEPOSIT · you' under a guide that says it is the operator's. Also note usdUnit (:141) prints '$0.0000' for empty sections beside '$3,200.00' — two formats in one column.
  - Fix: `me: !!owner`; print $0.00 for an exact zero.

**Keep**

- Every number on the glass passes through pure, clock-free laws (accrual.ts, ladder.ts); nothing random, the 1 Hz clock is null until mount so the static export never mismatches hydration (command-ux1.tsx:94-95).
- The ordinary refusals are right and say why: the 3-hour hold, more than withdrawable, zero, negative and NaN amounts, a withdrawal dated before the deposit (probe g); the reason is one sentence in the person's words, no vendor, database or schema named — and a gate (financial-surface.test.mjs:57) refuses a vendor word.
- The record is owner-keyed and owner-checked on read (record.ts:63), a bad store is flagged rather than trusted, and a store that will not take the write is reported instead of assumed (record.ts:70, command-ux1.tsx:177).
- The sign-in return path is validated against open redirects (auth-return.ts:resolveReturnTo); the memo and every stored string are rendered as React text, so a forged record cannot inject markup.
- The ladder is the brief: thirteen sections A–M in order with the exact slash-merged labels (ladder.ts:48-76), Net = I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr last and red (command-ux1.tsx:360), one constant multiplier between units (ladder.ts:88) so switching per minute → per year drifts by nothing; U is a rule, never a chip; N–T never per minute.
- The unsigned stranger is read-only and told so twice (the guide sentence and the EXAMPLE badge); the forms exist only behind sign-in (:297) and the duplicate-insurance and non-flow refusals are already in the model for the next surface.
- The record JSON is honest about state: FIN-06.06 is 'draft', human_testing is Red, the fleet is on the record as addendum 23.

**Report (verbatim)**

THE WALK (390 × 844, as a stranger holding the operator's sheet — Income 3,200 · Home 700 · Car 1,800 · Insurance 200 · Electric 150 · Food 300 · Fitness 50 · Fun 200 per 33 days)

0–10 s. A black shell with a SHA / date / time bar, three glyphs, FINANCIAL · 2525, 'the MoT Financial System · Version 000 · revision 0.012'. A card titled 'Money as time — this MoT' with a DEPOSIT pill and a five-chip rail: DEPOSIT 1 · HOLD · RELEASE $0.00 · WITHDRAW 0 · RECORD 1. I do not yet know what MoT is; the rail tells me money moves in five steps. Fine.

10–20 s. An amber 'YOUR TURN' card: 'Sign in to record your own deposits — the example below is the operator's paycheck as data', a big 'Sign in ↓'. Below it 'YOUR DEPOSITS': '1. 2026.10.01_07.00..00 · Income / Wages (take-home) · PAYROLL · PENDING DIRECT DEPOSIT · you · $3,604.49 · 30.333'. Two voices in one screen: the card says it is the operator's, the roster says 'you' (command-ux1.tsx:215, me: true for the example). I would press Sign in. I read the code behind that button instead: Auth0 loginWithRedirect with a validated same-origin returnTo (auth-return.ts) — sound.

20–30 s. The clock block: 'Released · Deposit $0.00 · In escrow $0.00 · Withdrawable $0.00 · Withdrawn $0.00 · Available $0.00'. Everything is zero because the example is dated tomorrow 07:00 CST — the page says 'pending from 2026.10.01_07.00..00'. The chart: a boxed empty graph with a legend of six colours and five axis marks '0 h 0 min … 30 d 8 h'. The MoT ⇄ Clock toggle (two small icons at the right) flips the marks to '2688.1234..5678'-style numbers. A stranger cannot tell what those are; the toggle at least leaves the plain hours as default. A planet dropdown (Earth · Mars) sits beside it — I would not press it, and I would not know why it is there.

30–40 s. 'Year position — the orbit (A.B..C)': 'Day 271 · Quarter 3 · 89/91 · 33-day frame 9 · 7/33', a stamp, 'perihelion CST'. I understand 'quarter 3, day 89 of 91'. The rest is the operator's calendar; it is labelled, not explained. Then the EXAMPLE badge again and a second Sign in button.

40–55 s. 'Personal finance ladder A–M', a full-width Unit dropdown ('per 33 days'), and the ladder: A · Income $3,200.00 with 'Income / Wages (take-home)' under it, B · Housing $700 / Rent / Mortgage, C · Transportation $1,800 / Auto Payment, D · Insurance $200, E · Utilities $150, F · Food $300, G · Health $50 / Mental Health / Physical Fitness, H I J K '$0.0000', L · Lifestyle $200 / Fun / Hobbies / Clothing, M · Transfers $0.0000, Net −$200.00 in red. This is my sheet, placed exactly where the brief puts it — the one moment the surface and the lock meet. Two things I notice: '$0.0000' beside '$3,200.00' in the same column (command-ux1.tsx:141), and that nothing on this ladder is mine to change — it is the operator's figures with no edit (r.016 added a pencil; r.012 has none). Under it: 'Cash, investments, debts … live on the Balance view — never per minute.' There is no Balance view to go to.

55–60 s. 'The record · Chain verified' — one line, the example deposit, an 8-hex hash — and 'On this phone only — a cloud copy follows once sign-in carries it'. Honest sentence. Then a folded Trinity wheel and the R-CORE badge. The fixed phone strip ('$0.00 · $0.00 · DEPOSIT') rides over whatever is under it the whole way down.

WHAT I UNDERSTOOD: money is deposited, released by the minute, withdrawable after three hours, recorded on a chain that lives on my phone; my budget can be read per second through per year. WHAT I COULD NOT: what A.B..C, MoT, perihelion and the planet are for; why the example deposit is 'you'; where the Balance view is. WHAT I WOULD PRESS: Sign in, the Unit dropdown, and — after sign-in — Record it on my first paycheck. WHAT THE BRIEF PROMISES THAT I DO NOT SEE: the N–T surface, the U entry, the detail sheet, the duplicate-premium warning (computed at ladder.ts:110, never rendered), Alcohol's one target, and above all the thing the operator said he wanted — '$/min against budget' — the transactions' field and timeline are recorded (command-ux1.tsx:190, :197) but never summed onto the ladder, so recording Rent leaves Net at −$200.00. The record JSON says FIN-06.06 is draft; the glass should say the same where the reader is looking.

THEN THE THOR PART — I signed in (in code) and tried to hurt the record. I transpiled the r.012 libraries from the commit and drove them with node (fleet-fin/thor-walk/probe.mjs, hang.mjs; outputs in probe.out):

1. A long number in 'MoT · length in days' — 309 digits, or a pasted 1e400 — freezes the tab. Number() gives Infinity, line 190 keeps it, the chart's series loop at accrual.ts:127 steps by Infinity forever (1e6 iterations, t never moves). Worse: commit() wrote the record before the render, so after the forced reload the deposit is on the append-only chain with a null MoT, releasing at once. A typo becomes a permanent entry and a frozen phone.

2. A withdrawal dated in the future double-spends. validateWithdrawal looks only at w.atMs. $1,000 released; withdraw $1,000 dated +10 days — accepted; withdraw $1,000 now — accepted, because the future one is not counted yet. At the future date the record is −$1,000 and the Math.max(0, …) at accrual.ts:106 prints $0. The invariant in the file's own header (accrual.ts:15-16) is false. Back-dating a withdrawal before an existing one is the same member of the class.

3. A tampered record is erased by the next tap. loadRecord correctly reads a changed byte as 'tampered' and shows empty — then the forms stay live, the next deposit appends to the empty record and saveRecord overwrites the store. Two earlier deposits and the evidence are gone. The lexicon promises 'never repaired silently'; the code repairs by deletion. 'A completed signature is never discarded' is this project's own law; here a completed record is.

4. An infinite amount prints '$∞' and persists as null; the chain still verifies after reload because stableJson wrote null both times. 5. 'Chain verified' is what a re-chained forgery also reads — FNV-1a with no secret; the file knows it ('not a signature'), the glass does not say it. 6. Two deposits with the same typed stamp and amount: the second is silently dropped and the form clears as if it took. 7. A deposit's refusal prints under the withdrawal panel, off-screen at phone width (r.013's one-form change probably removes this member; the rule needs a gate). 8. Sign-out leaves the plaintext record on the phone with no way to remove it. 9. Feb 31 becomes Mar 3 and year 0026 becomes 1926, silently.

What held: the hold and insufficient refusals, NaN and negative amounts, a withdrawal before the deposit, the owner check on load, the honest store-failure line, the validated login return, text-only rendering of stored strings, and the ladder arithmetic as a single constant — the brief's acceptance 'no rounding drift beyond cents' is met by construction.

GRADE C. The voice is right and the model is right; the record — the one thing a finance surface must not lose — can be frozen, double-drawn and overwritten with inputs a person can type in under a minute. All three are one class: nothing validates a transaction against the WHOLE record and the WHOLE number line before it is written. Fix the class once (finite + capped numbers at the form; whole-record validation across every instant; refuse-and-quarantine on tamper) and gate each with a replay test, and this lens moves to a B.

Note on evidence: frontend/out at the time of reading was the r.016 build (its header says revision 0.016), so the headless walk captures under fleet-fin/thor-walk/ are of that build; the shell, rail, guide, roster, clock, chart, year line, ladder and record are the same code paths as r.012 (git diff 201a8ec..HEAD shows the root and the strip unchanged). Every defect above is cited and proved from the r.012 sources shown out of commit 201a8ec, never from the working tree. Nothing in the repository was edited.

### Thor · adversarial — grade D+

_ADVERSARIAL PASS on Financial-2525 v.000_r.012 (commit 201a8ec) — the ladder math, the pickers, the timelines, the record, the calendar, the unit dropdown, the lexicon, the gates_

**One sentence:** The pure ladder model honours the lock (60/60 labels verbatim, Net exact at every period, round-trip drift 9e-13), but the money record under it loses on four hostile paths with no refusal and no gate — a dated withdrawal overdraws ($1,500 withdrawn of $1,000 withdrawable), "1e400" records an infinite deposit the chain then "verifies", a second identical entry is dropped silently, and a one-byte store corruption makes the next "Record it" destroy the whole record — while sign-in is a client-side cookie any device holder can forge and Mars' year is Earth's.

**Findings**

- **A withdrawal is validated only at its own instant — backdated or future-dated withdrawals overdraw the record silently** (CRITICAL)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/accrual.ts:113-119 validateWithdrawal computes balanceAt(txs minus w, w.atMs) and nothing else; accrual.ts:96 counts only withdrawals with atMs <= t, accrual.ts:106 clamps availableCents to 0 instead of refusing. The withdrawal's atMs is whatever the person types (frontend/components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx:195-197). Library probe (thor-adv/probe-thor.out P2/P2b): deposit $1,000 MoT 10 d; withdraw all at +10 d (ok); a $500 withdrawal dated day 5 → {ok:true}; balance at +11 d withdrawn 150000 vs withdrawable 100000, overdrawn by $500. A withdrawal dated +20 d for the whole deposit passes today, then today's $100 passes too: overdrawn $100. On the r.012 glass (glass.json G3e): after a $1,000 withdrawal 'now', a $500 withdrawal typed 2026.09.06 is accepted — the balance card reads 'Withdrawable: $1,000.00 · Withdrawn: $1,500.00 · Available: $0.00', refusal null, three rows on the record. tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs:29-38 only tests withdrawals at later instants, so the gate is green over this.
  - Fix: State the invariant (available is never negative at ANY instant on the record) and enforce it as a class: validateWithdrawal must check balanceAt at w.atMs AND at every existing withdrawal instant ≥ w.atMs (and now), refusing with a named reason (DATED) if any goes negative; alternatively refuse a withdrawal dated before the last withdrawal or after now. Add the backdated and future-dated cases to financial-accrual.test.mjs. Never clamp at accrual.ts:106 without also surfacing the overdraw.
- **A corrupt or tampered store is read as EMPTY, and the next 'Record it' overwrites it — the tamper check destroys the record it was meant to protect** (CRITICAL)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/record.ts:63-66 loadRecord returns emptyRecord(owner) when verify() fails; command-ux1.tsx:114 setRecord(r.rec) adopts that empty record; command-ux1.tsx:177 commit → append(record,…) → saveRecord(next) writes the one-entry record over the stored one. Library probe P9: five deposits saved; one byte changed (300000→300001); loadRecord → tampered:true, entries 0; one append + save → the store holds 1 entry (five gone). On the r.012 glass (G3d): after the byte flip the header reads 'This device's record failed its chain — shown empty, never repaired silently'; one more 'Record it' → localStorage entries = 1. The sentence promises what the next tap breaks. This is the exact class of docs/AAR_2026-09-09 ('a completed signature is never discarded'): the first phone with a flaky write, a storage-full partial save, or a hand edit loses every transaction.
  - Fix: Quarantine, never replace: on a failed verify keep the raw blob under exel-fin:<owner>:quarantine-<ts>, refuse recording (disable both buttons, say why in one sentence) until the person chooses, and never let saveRecord write over a key whose current contents did not verify. Gate it in test:ci: a tampered store followed by an append must leave the original bytes somewhere on the device.
- **The amount box is free text: '1e400' / 'Infinity' records an infinite deposit; the glass prints $∞, $NaN, '$Infinity per minute'; the chain 'verifies' a null** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:302 <input inputMode="decimal"> (no type, no pattern); command-ux1.tsx:185-187 cents = Math.round(Number(dAmt)*100) and only `!(cents > 0)` refuses — Infinity > 0 is true. record.ts:23 stableJson turns Infinity into 'null' so the hash is computed over null twice and verify() stays ok. On the r.012 glass (G3b, shots/infinity-clock.png): after '1e400' the balance card reads 'Released · $Infinity per minute · $∞ · In escrow: $NaN · $Infinity per hour · $∞ per day · $Infinity per second'; the record row prints '$∞ · 30.333'; header 'Chain verified'. After reload the clock reads $0.00, the stored amountCents is null, the row prints $0.00 — the same entry reads three different ways. Number('0x10') = 16 and Number(' 1e3') = 1000 are also accepted silently.
  - Fix: Refuse at the edge and in the law: accept only /^\d{1,9}(\.\d{1,2})?$/ on the glass with the existing fin.reason_amount; in record.append and accrual.balanceAt assert Number.isFinite(amountCents) && Number.isSafeInteger(amountCents) && amountCents > 0 and refuse otherwise; add '1e400', 'Infinity', '0x10' to financial-accrual.test.mjs.
- **A second transaction with the same amount at the same stamp is dropped silently — the form clears as if it were recorded** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:190 id = `d-${when}-${cents}` and :197 `w-${when}-${cents}`; record.ts:35 append returns the same record when the id exists; command-ux1.tsx:191 then clears the amount and memo, and no refusal is set. Library probe P4: two $500 deposits at one typed stamp → entries 1, same record object. On the glass (G3c): two $500 deposits typed at 2026.10.01_07.00..00 with memos 'first' and 'second' → one record row ('… first$500.00 · 30.333'), refusal null, amount box emptied. Two rent-sized deposits on the same payday, or two identical cash withdrawals in one second, lose the second with no word.
  - Fix: The id must not be the dedupe key for human entries: derive it from the record (rev + a counter, or crypto.randomUUID()) and, if the idempotency guard is kept, make append return a named refusal (DUPLICATE_ID) that the glass prints instead of clearing the form.
- **Sign-in is a client-side cookie plus a localStorage cache: a forged session is accepted and the forms open — the gate is a label, not a lock** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: frontend/components/providers.tsx:165 cacheLocation="localstorage"; frontend/lib/constants.ts:4-5 the client id is a public constant; command-ux1.tsx:110 owner = user.sub, the record key is exel-fin:<sub> (record.ts:18-19). Headless on the r.012 export (glass.json W2): setting cookie auth0.<clientId>.is.authenticated=true and two localStorage entries with an unsigned token 'eyJ.eyJ.sig' and sub 'auth0|thor-probe' renders [data-fin-forms] and prints 'Signed in as Thor Probe'; both forms record. Nothing on the device is protected by the login: anyone holding the phone reads exel-fin:<sub> directly, and anyone who knows a person's sub can open their record under it. The record sentence 'On this phone only' (fin.device_only) is true; the 'per login' promise in record.ts:2 and FIN-05 is not yet a protection.
  - Fix: Say what the login does today (names the key) and what it does not (protect it) — in the record and on the operator's ?diag door, not the signer's screen. Until a cloud half with server-side auth exists, do not describe the record as per-login-protected; consider encrypting the device record under a key derived from the session and wiping it on sign-out (command-ux1.tsx:323 logout leaves the record in place).
- **Mars' year is opened and closed by EARTH's perihelion: it reads 'SOURCED · 271/365', resets at 1909 A instead of 3600, and days 365–687 of its whole read as DOWN days** (HIGH)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/calendar.ts:98-101 use perihelionOf(year) — Earth's table — for every planet; :105 down = day > GRID_DAYS with GRID_DAYS fixed at 364 (:63) whatever yearDays is; :109 status comes from Earth's rows; :113 lengthDays is Earth's interval. Library probe P7: Mars (686.98) one millisecond before Earth's 2027 perihelion reads abc 1909.1835..0207, day 365, down:true, quarter 0, lengthDays 364.39, pastFull:false; one millisecond later it is 0.0000..0000 — the planet's whole (3600 A) is never reached; day 400 of the Martian whole reads as 'day 36 of 2027'. On the r.012 glass (G3f, shots/mars-year.png) the Mars panel prints 'Day 271 · Quarter 3 · 89/91 … · 2026 · SOURCED · 271/365' and, one line lower, 'no perihelion table for this planet yet — declared, not sourced' — the same panel calls itself SOURCED and declared.
  - Fix: Give positionInYear a per-planet anchor: a Mars perihelion table (or a DECLARED epoch) and a grid derived from yearDays (quarters = yearDays/4 rounded as the operator decides), status DECLARED when the anchor is declared; print lengthDays of the planet's whole. Gate: Mars at 686 days reads ~3595 A, never 'down' before its own whole.
- **A refused DEPOSIT prints its refusal 434 px lower, inside the WITHDRAWAL card; the save-failed line lives there too and is never cleared** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:187-188 recordDeposit sets refusal; the only renderer is command-ux1.tsx:318 inside #fin-withdraw-form (same for saveFailed at :319, which no code ever sets back to false). On the glass (G3a, shots/refusal-placement.png): an empty amount on the deposit form → 'Refused · Enter an amount above zero' appears under the Withdraw button, inDepositForm:false, inWithdrawForm:true, 434 px below the deposit button — off-screen at 844 px tall; a bad stamp ('tomorrow') prints 'The day and time must read YYYY.MM.DD_HH.MM..SS' in the same wrong card. A person tapping Record it on the phone sees nothing happen.
  - Fix: One refusal slot per form (or one slot directly under whichever button was tapped), cleared on the next successful action; clear saveFailed on the next successful save. Probe it headlessly: the refusal's closest form must be the form of the button that was tapped.
- **One deposit carries two lengths — MoT days (escrow release) and the TIMELINE (ladder) — that disagree by default; the timeline is recorded but read by nothing on the glass, including on withdrawals** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:145 dMot defaults to 30.333 and :304 keeps the MoT input beside the new timeline dropdown (:169-173) whose default is days33 (:151) = 33 days (ladder.ts:126). Probe P10: at day 15 escrow has released $1,782.44 of the paycheck while the timeline's rate would credit $1,638.40 — $144 apart on one entry. The recurrence is written at command-ux1.tsx:190/197 and never read: grep of the r.012 component for actualsByField | recurrenceDays | ratePerMinute returns nothing; a withdrawal is also asked for a timeline ('One time') that has no meaning anywhere. FD-26 says 'the timeline is the transaction's MoT' — the code keeps both.
  - Fix: One length per entry: derive motDays from the timeline (or offer 'custom' as a timeline option that reveals the days box) so escrow and ladder run at one rate; drop the timeline from withdrawals until it means something; wire actualsByField (FIN-06.06) before the dropdown ships to a person, or label it 'recorded for r.013'.
- **The ladder on the glass is the operator's static sheet, not the person's record; the lock's duplicate-insurance WARNING and the alcohol target never reach the screen** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:138 totals = netLadder(SHEET_LINES, period) and :355 lists SHEET_LINES — every signed-in person sees Income 3,200 · Home 700 · Car 1,800 · Net −200 regardless of what they recorded (confirmed in locale and forged-session runs: the ladder never changed after deposits). ladder.ts:110 pushes 'DUPLICATE_INSURANCE' and :83 alcoholSection takes a target, but grep of the component for warnings | DUPLICATE | alcohol is empty — rule 5 'warn' of the brief is computed and discarded; rule 6 has no chooser.
  - Fix: Say on the panel that the figures are the sheet (seed) until the plan is editable (r.016 later did this — fine), render totals.warnings as one line when non-empty, and give alcohol its one-target choice in the detail sheet when it ships.
- **Latent prototype pollution and sign asymmetry in the actuals law: a record-supplied fieldId of '__proto__' pollutes Object.prototype; negatives are ignored by netLadder but counted by actualsByField; amortize accepts NaN and negatives** (MEDIUM)
  - Evidence: ladder.ts:138 key = tx.fieldId ?? '?' and :143 out[key] ?? {…} then out[key] = cur. Probe (probe2.mjs): after actualsByField([{fieldId:'__proto__',…}]) typeof ({}).amount is 'number' (NaN) and Object.getOwnPropertyNames(Object.prototype) includes ['amount','perMinute']; a 'constructor' key sets Object.amount. FinTx.field is a free string written to a device store the person (or a tampered blob) controls (accrual.ts:36, record.ts:62). ladder.ts:104 skips amountNative ≤ 0 while :142 only skips zero (P8: −100 income → netLadder 0, actuals −100 / −600000 per minute); ladder.ts:118-120 amortize({amount: NaN}) returns amount NaN and −1200 returns −3.29. Dormant in r.012 (no caller on the glass) but FIN-06.06 wires actualsByField in r.013.
  - Fix: Use Object.create(null) or a Map for the accumulator and validate fieldId against fieldOf() before keying; make the two laws agree on sign (refuse or warn on negatives in both); assert Number.isFinite(amount) && amount > 0 in amortize. Add these three to financial-ladder.test.mjs.
- **The chain hash has no secret and verify() checks no shape: a re-chained forgery verifies, and a −$5,000 deposit verifies and 'releases' −$5,000** (LOW)
  - Evidence: record.ts:25-30 FNV-1a over prev|rev|at|json with no key; record.ts:45-53 checks rev, prev, hash only. Probe P9b: alter entry 3 to 99,999,900 cents and recompute every hash from there → loadRecord tampered:false. P9c: append({amountCents:-500000}) → verify ok, balanceAt releasedCents −500000 (accrual.ts:59 returns tx.amountCents unchecked). The file says 'tamper-evidence for a phone, not a signature' (record.ts:24) — true, but FIN-05's 'a changed amount breaks the chain' only holds against someone who does not re-chain.
  - Fix: Keep the honest claim (accidental-edit evidence) in FIN-05 and on the record; have verify() also assert each tx's shape (kind, finite positive integer cents, finite atMs, motDays ≥ 0, field ∈ FIELDS or absent); a signed chain waits for the cloud half.
- **A day that does not exist is silently moved: '2026.02.31' becomes 2026.03.03, '2026.04.31' becomes 2026.05.01; single-dot seconds are accepted** (LOW)
  - Evidence: frontend/lib/financial-2525/mot.ts:160 STAMP_RE allows '.' or '..' before seconds; :166 checks d ≤ 31 only, then :167 cstMs → Date.UTC rolls over. Probe P6: parseStampCST('2026.02.31_07.00..00') → '2026.03.03_07.00..00'. A deposit typed on a day that does not exist lands two days later with no word.
  - Fix: Round-trip check in parseStampCST: reject when cstParts(result) does not equal the typed y/mo/d/h/mi/s; require the '..' the sheets write or say the single dot is accepted.
- **At r.012 the whole ladder reads English in the other 32 languages: 90 keys were staged (listed in AFTER_FILL), so a French, Spanish or Japanese reader sees a mixed panel** (LOW)
  - Evidence: frontend/tests/lexicon-coverage.test.mjs at 201a8ec (AFTER_FILL lines 27-28) stages fin.sec.* (20), fin.field.* (60), fin.rec.* (5), fin.section/field/timeline/ladder_title/stock_note; frontend/lib/i18n-app/fr.ts at the commit holds 0 of them. Headless on the r.012 export (lex.mjs): fr → title 'Personal finance ladder A–M', unit 'Unité · par 33 jours', row 'A · Income', field 'Income / Wages (take-home)', note 'Cash, investments, debts…' — same for es ('Unidad · por 33 días · Neto' beside English sections) and ja ('単位 · 33日ごと · 純額' beside English). The staging is honest (listed, never silent) and the fill landed later in 50e57c0, outside the revision under review.
  - Fix: None beyond what shipped later; for the next staged batch, hold the surface behind the fill or show the 32 languages the English with a one-line 'translation follows' note on the operator door.
- **The surface gate is regexes over source text, not behaviour: width, 'no typed factor', refusal placement and the export are asserted by pattern; no gate renders, signs in, or drives the forms** (LOW)
  - Evidence: frontend/tests/financial-surface.test.mjs:23-79 — every assertion is a RegExp over the component's text; :54 'full width in portrait' is a className regex (the real measurement is mine: 290/290 px); :56 forbids 1440 | 86400 | 525600 only on the component while ladder.ts:129 types `days * 1440` (probe P11); :86-88 the export check is 'file exists and > 2000 bytes'. financial-accrual.test.mjs:29-38 never dates a withdrawal before an existing one; financial-ladder.test.mjs:42 blesses 'a negative amount is never counted' on netLadder but :63-70 never feed actualsByField a negative, a '__proto__' key or an instant exactly at toMs. Every defect above is green in test:ci.
  - Fix: Add one headless gate on the export (the shots scripts already exist) that forges nothing but the locale: measures the pickers, submits '1e400' and a duplicate, and reads where the refusal prints; move the 1440 ban into ladder.ts (ratePerMinute = amount / (days * PERIOD_SECONDS.day / PERIOD_SECONDS.minute)); add the dated-withdrawal and sign cases to the two law gates.
- **An unknown field id on a record entry wears the Lifestyle (party) icon and prints its raw id; a withdrawal entry prints nothing of its length while the roster prints motDays raw** (LOW)
  - Evidence: command-ux1.tsx:180 `fieldOf(tx.field)?.section ?? "L"` — a hand-edited or future field id (e.g. 'U.anything', 'Z.nothing') is drawn with SECTION_ICON.L and fieldLabel() falls back to the raw id (:154); command-ux1.tsx:215 label `${usd(d.amountCents)} · ${d.motDays}` prints '30.333' with no unit.
  - Fix: Fall back to CircleEllipsis (as CategoryIcon does at category-icon.tsx:29) and print 'unknown field' from the lexicon; print the MoT with its unit word.

**Keep**

- The model honours the lock where it was asked to: my independent diff of the brief (addendum 22) against ladder.ts finds all 20 section names and all 60 field labels identical, including 'Housing insurance', 'Alcohol (or Fun)', 'Assets − Debts' (probe2.mjs: mismatches []) — the gate samples only 23, the full set holds.
- toPeriod is one constant and does not drift: the worst round trip over all 64 period pairs on $3,604.49 is 9.1e-13 dollars (probe P5); on the glass the sheet's Net reads −$200.00 per 33 days, −$0.0042 per minute, red in every unit, with every section row A–M present (ladder-min.json).
- Payment ≠ balance holds in the law: a P line never nets against I (financial-ladder.test.mjs:41), U refuses N–T, and nothing past T exists.
- The unit dropdown is genuinely full width in portrait on the r.012 build: 290 px of a 290 px panel at 390×844, and its own width (224 of 668 px) at 844×390; the three new pickers measure 290/290 in portrait and 330 each in the two-column landscape grid; no horizontal scroll, zero page errors in six contexts (glass.json W1/W2).
- Tampering IS detected and said in the person's words: a one-byte change flips the header to 'This device's record failed its chain — shown empty, never repaired silently' (G3d) — the detection works; only the follow-through (the overwrite) fails.
- Refusals that exist are well worded and vendor-free ('Enter an amount above zero', 'The day and time must read YYYY.MM.DD_HH.MM..SS'); the record sentence names no vendor.
- The laws are pure and clock-free (every function takes t), CST stamps round-trip, and the ask on the record hashes to the file at the commit (sha256 a465d3bd… matches docs/asks at 201a8ec).
- The lexicon staging is honest: the 90 English keys are listed in AFTER_FILL, never silent, and the fill landed as its own commit later (50e57c0).

**Report (verbatim)**

SCOPE AND METHOD. Read-only. Every file was read from commit 201a8ec with git show; the working tree is at r.017 and frontend/out/financial-2525/index.html is stamped v.000_r.017, so I did not measure it. A prior agent had built r.012 into fleet-fin/r012tree/frontend/out (stamped v.000_r.012); I served that with my own node script and drove it in Chromium at 390×844 and 844×390 (fleet-fin/thor-adv/glass.mjs → glass.json, glass.out, shots/). Library probes ran against the r.012 sources extracted into fleet-fin/thor-adv/lib with only the import specifiers rewritten (probe-thor.out, probe2.mjs). Note for the fleet: the earlier captures in fleet-fin (p-01…, portrait-info.json) are stamped 'revision 0.013', not 0.012. The live site is unreachable from here; nothing here is a claim about what is deployed.

THE LADDER MATH — holds. Net = I − L − Ds − Tx − Tr is right at every period; the drift the brief feared is 9e-13 dollars; P never nets against I; the duplicate-insurance rule warns and does not double-count; alcohol has one target; U refuses N–T. But two of those rules never reach a person: the warning (ladder.ts:110) and the alcohol target (ladder.ts:83) have no reader in the component, and the ladder on the glass is the operator's seed sheet for everyone (command-ux1.tsx:138) — a person's own deposits never move it in r.012. Sign handling is inconsistent across the two laws (netLadder ignores negatives, actualsByField counts them), amortize accepts NaN, and actualsByField keys a plain object by a record-supplied string — a '__proto__' field id pollutes Object.prototype (proven). Dormant until r.013 wires actuals.

THE PICKERS — honest. Section → field → timeline, all 290/290 px in portrait; the section change resets the field to the section's first (command-ux1.tsx:160), so a field cannot sit under the wrong section through the UI. The record, however, accepts any field string; an unknown one wears the Lifestyle icon (:180). The timeline is recorded and read nowhere; a deposit carries MoT 30.333 and timeline 33 at the same time ($144 apart by day 15); withdrawals are asked for a timeline that means nothing.

THE RECORD — this is where r.012 fails Thor's lens, and all four failures have a green gate over them. (1) validateWithdrawal checks the balance only at the withdrawal's own typed instant, so a backdated or future-dated withdrawal overdraws: on the glass, 'Withdrawn $1,500 · Withdrawable $1,000 · Available $0.00', no refusal. (2) A failed chain reads as an empty record and the next 'Record it' writes a one-entry record over the store — five deposits became one in the probe; on the glass the header promises 'never repaired silently' and the next tap erases. (3) The amount box is free text: '1e400' records Infinity — '$∞', '$NaN', '$Infinity per minute' live, $0.00 after reload, 'Chain verified' throughout because JSON turns Infinity into null before hashing. (4) Two identical entries at one stamp collapse to one with no word, and the form clears as if both were kept. Lesser: the chain has no secret, so a re-chained forgery verifies; verify() checks no transaction shape (a −$5,000 deposit verifies and releases −$5,000); Feb 31 silently becomes Mar 3.

SIGN-IN — a label. cacheLocation='localstorage' plus a cookie named after a public client id; a forged cache with an unsigned token opened the forms as 'Thor Probe'. On the device the login names the key (exel-fin:<sub>) and protects nothing; sign-out leaves the record in place. Say so on the operator's door; do not call the record per-login-protected until a server holds it.

THE CALENDAR — Earth is right (perihelion 2026.01.03_11.15..00 CST on the panel, pastFull flagged, never clamped). Mars is wrong by construction: calendar.ts opens and closes every planet's year on Earth's perihelion table and a fixed 364-day grid, so Mars resets at 1909 A, never 3600, reads 'SOURCED · 271/365', and calls days 365–687 of its own whole 'down' — while the line beneath says 'declared, not sourced'.

THE UNIT DROPDOWN — measured, passes: 290 of 290 px in portrait, 224 of 668 in landscape, label above in portrait.

THE LEXICON — at this revision the ladder reads English under French, Spanish and Japanese ('Unité · par 33 jours' beside 'A · Income · Income / Wages (take-home)'); staged honestly, filled in a later commit.

THE GATES — financial-ladder is behavioural and good; financial-surface is 57 regexes over the source (width by className, 'no typed factor' only on the component while ladder.ts:129 types 1440, export = 'bigger than 2 KB'); financial-accrual never dates a withdrawal before an existing one. Nothing in test:ci renders, signs in, types into a box, or reads where a refusal prints. One headless gate on the export would have caught defects 3, 4, 7 and the Mars line; two assertions in the accrual gate would have caught 1 and 2.

REFUSALS — the refusal copy is right, the placement is not: a deposit's refusal prints 434 px lower inside the withdrawal card, below the fold at phone height, and saveFailed shares that slot and never clears. (Also observed in my r.012 capture: the fixed phone strip overprints the year panel — the floating strip the operator later named in addendum 29, already addressed in r.017.)

GRADE D+. The lock's model is an A on its own and deserves to be said so; but the operator intends to run his own money through this surface first, and r.012 lets a dated withdrawal overdraw, records an infinite dollar, drops a second rent silently and destroys a record on the tap after a corrupt save — each with no refusal, each green in CI. Fix the class, not the instance: one invariant per law (available never negative at any instant on the record; a stored record is never overwritten by a shorter one; an amount is a finite positive integer of cents; an entry the person recorded is on the record or refused by name), each with a gate that drives the real form.

### Thor · synthesis

_(did not return)_
